[`FastRoundErr.v`](theories/FastRoundErr.v) shows that they return a rounding of `y 2^e` for every real `y` within `err` of `h + l` (`round_dir_err_correct` and `round_near_err_correct`), and proves TwoSum (`two_sum_correct`) and `normalize` (`normalize_correct`). [`FastRoundWide.v`](theories/FastRoundWide.v) shows the same at `e` = 1023 too (`round_dir_err_wide_correct` and `round_near_err_wide_correct`), as for `round_dir` and `round_near` above, and at `e` = -1022 where `h` > 1 (`round_dir_err_normal_correct` and `round_near_err_normal_correct`). The tests `round_dir_wide_err` and `round_near_wide_err` of the fast path of x^y below are `round_dir_wide` and `round_near_wide` above with such a bound `err` in place of 2^-68: they call `round_dir_err` and `round_near_err` where those call `round_dir` and `round_near`, and `round_dir_sub_err` and `round_near_sub_err` where those call `round_dir_sub` and `round_near_sub`, which use the bounds 2 `err` C, (2^-53 - 2 `err`) C and (2^-53 + 2 `err`) C in place of 2^-67 C, 2^-53 C - 2^-67 C and 2^-53 C + 2^-67 C. [`FastRoundWideErr.v`](theories/FastRoundWideErr.v) shows that they return a rounding of `y 2^e` for every real `y` within `err` of `h + l`, at an `e` of at least -1085 and with 1/2 < `h` < 2 (`round_dir_wide_err_correct` and `round_near_wide_err_correct`), from `round_dir_sub_err_correct` and `round_near_sub_err_correct` there, for an `e` in [-1085, -1022], and `round_dir_err_normal_correct` and `round_near_err_normal_correct`. [`FastDiv.v`](theories/FastDiv.v) bounds the relative error of `dd_div` by 2^-98 (`dd_div_correct`). [`HypTaylor.v`](theories/HypTaylor.v) bounds the truncation of the two Taylor polynomials (`sinh_taylor` and `cosh_taylor`); [`FastHypPoly.v`](theories/FastHypPoly.v) and [`FastCoshPoly.v`](theories/FastCoshPoly.v) bound their errors as computed by 2^-65 sinh t (`sinh_poly_correct`, from the steps in [`FastSinhHorner.v`](theories/FastSinhHorner.v) and [`FastSinhSteps.v`](theories/FastSinhSteps.v)) and 2^-66 cosh t (`cosh_poly_correct`); and [`FastHypPos.v`](theories/FastHypPos.v), [`FastCoshPos.v`](theories/FastCoshPos.v) and [`FastTanhPos.v`](theories/FastTanhPos.v) show that `fast_sinh_pos`, `fast_cosh_pos` and `fast_tanh_pos` return what the rounding tests need (`fast_sinh_pos_correct` and the others). `fast_sinh_dir_correct` and the five others in [`FastHyp.v`](theories/FastHyp.v) combine them, and `fast_sinh_dir_fma` and the five others show that `fma` does not change their results at a finite argument, a nonnegative one for `fast_cosh_dir` and `fast_cosh_near`, which `cosh` calls with |x|, so that `DyadicExp.sinh_dir_with` and the others pass `false` too:

```coq
Theorem round_dir_err_correct :
  forall h l e err p up v y,
  is_finite h = true -> is_finite l = true -> B2R h = RN (B2R h + B2R l) ->
  (-1021 <= e <= 1022)%Z -> (55 <= p <= 105)%Z -> B2R err = bpow radix2 (- p) ->
  Rabs (y - (B2R h + B2R l)) <= bpow radix2 (- p) ->
  Fast.round_dir_err h l e err up = Some v ->
  rounds up (y * bpow radix2 e) v.

Theorem dd_div_correct :
  forall fma nh nl dh dl,
  is_finite nh = true -> is_finite nl = true -> is_finite dh = true -> is_finite dl = true ->
  bpow radix2 (-80) <= B2R nh <= 4 -> Rabs (B2R nl) <= bpow radix2 (-50) * B2R nh ->
  / 2 <= B2R dh <= 4 -> Rabs (B2R dl) <= bpow radix2 (-52) * B2R dh ->
  let '(qh, ql) := Fast.dd_div fma nh nl dh dl in
  is_finite qh = true /\ is_finite ql = true /\ B2R qh = RN (B2R qh + B2R ql) /\
  Rabs (B2R qh + B2R ql - (B2R nh + B2R nl) / (B2R dh + B2R dl))
    <= bpow radix2 (-98) * ((B2R nh + B2R nl) / (B2R dh + B2R dl)).

Theorem fast_sinh_dir_correct :
  forall fma x up v,
  is_finite x = true -> DyadicExp.fast_sinh_dir fma x up = Some v ->
  rounds up (sinh (B2R x)) v.

Theorem fast_sinh_dir_fma :
  forall x up,
  is_finite x = true ->
  DyadicExp.fast_sinh_dir true x up = DyadicExp.fast_sinh_dir false x up.
```

`encl_le E a`, in [`DyGrow.v`](theories/DyGrow.v), says that the exponents of both bounds of `a` are at most `E` in magnitude. `DyGrow.v` bounds how much each operation on enclosures can grow them, so that every enclosure of the evaluations has `encl_small`, which the theorems of the operations ask of their operands. The tables of ln 2 and ln 10 in `src/dyadic/consts.rs` are their first 4,224 bits, and `DyConst.v` shows it with `vm_compute` of sums of the series of atanh, `ln 2 = 2 atanh(1/3)` and `ln 10 = 3 ln 2 + 2 atanh(1/9)`, at 4,288 bits, whose remainders `atanh_sum_bounds` in [`AtanhSeries.v`](theories/AtanhSeries.v) bounds. `k_exp_c` in [`DyPoint.v`](theories/DyPoint.v) shows that `k_exp`, the kernel's `exp` as the field of a record `point` (see "What is assumed"), satisfies `exp_c` of `Contract.v`, so that `kernel_exp_correct` gives `exp_correct` for a record `point` whose `p_exp` is `k_exp`, and `k_exp2_c` to `k_tanh_c` and `kernel_exp2_correct` to `kernel_tanh_correct` the same for the five others:

```coq
Theorem k_exp_c : exp_c k_exp.

Theorem kernel_exp_correct :
  forall x, valid x -> is_tightest (image_dom real_dom exp x) (Interval.exp kernel_point x).
```

The tests in `src/dyadic/exp/tests.rs` take these six functions, in both directions, at random arguments, at each rule's threshold and the arguments next to it, where the reductions change their `k`, at the integers, at the arguments of MPFR's own tests of these functions, and at arguments whose results are close to an `f64`. They check them against the roundings that bounds on the functions determine, where these do, and, on 64-bit Linux and macOS, against those of `src/mpfr_calls/mpfr.rs`, which call MPFR. Those in `src/dyadic/exp/tests/levels.rs` force each level from 1 to 64 limbs at a few arguments, check that each level either fails or gives the rounding and that `ziv_cap`, the step of `ziv` at its cap, gives the rounding in both directions, as the test passes at 64 limbs there, and, on 64-bit Linux and macOS, that each enclosure holds the value that MPFR computes at 8,192 bits and that each doubling of the precision at least halves its width.

`ln_dir_correct` in [`DyLog.v`](theories/DyLog.v) shows that `ln(x, up)` of `src/dyadic/log.rs`, which computes ln x with a fast path in double-double and with the kernel, returns, at a finite `x > 0` and wherever it returns by a rule, by the fast path or by a rounding test of `ziv` that passes, ln x rounded toward +∞ if `up` and toward -∞ otherwise, as `mpfr_round` says, and `ln_dir_side` that it returns there, wherever it returns rather than panics, a value on the side of ln x that the rounding is on, as for e^x above. `log2_dir_correct` and `log10_dir_correct` in `DyLog.v`, and `asinh_dir_correct`, `acosh_dir_correct` and `atanh_dir_correct` in [`DyLogHyp.v`](theories/DyLogHyp.v), and `log2_dir_side` to `atanh_dir_side`, state the same of `log2`, `log10`, `asinh`, `acosh` and `atanh`, with `Rlog2`, `Rlog10`, `arcsinh`, `Racosh` and `Ratanh` in place of `ln`, at a finite `x` in `log_dom` for `log2` and `log10`, at every finite `x` for `asinh`, and at a finite `x` in `acosh_dom` and in `atanh_dom` for `acosh` and `atanh`. `DyadicLog.ln_dir x up` in `Model.v` is `ln(x, up)`, with `None` where the Rust panics, and `ln_dir_with`, `ln_dir_strict` and `ln_dir_post` are as for e^x. Each function first settles some arguments by rules: NaN, the infinities, the arguments outside its domain, ±0 for `ln`, `log2` and `log10` and ±1 for `atanh`, whose results are infinite, those of magnitude below 2^-26 for `asinh` and `atanh`, whose results are within an ulp of `x`, and, after the fast path below, the `f64`s `1e0` to `1e22` for `log10`, whose results are the integers from 0 to 22. At the finite arguments in the domains, the theorems above meet two kinds of these rules: that of `log10` at `1e0` to `1e22`, whose lemma is `log10_pow10_rule` in [`DyLogRules.v`](theories/DyLogRules.v), and those of `asinh` and `atanh` below 2^-26, whose lemmas are `asinh_tiny_rule` and `atanh_tiny_rule` in [`DyLogTiny.v`](theories/DyLogTiny.v). Each lemma shows that its rule returns the rounding in its direction. All six then try the fast path, `log10` before its powers of 10, which returns, where it returns, the rounding in its direction (`fast_ln_dir_correct` and `fast_asinh_dir_correct` below), and so a value with the property `post` too. `log_rules_finite_pos` shows that the rules of `ln`, `log2` and `log10` settle no finite `x > 0`. `k_ln_c` and the others below check by computation the infinite results that `Contract.v` asks for at ±0, ±1 and the infinities. Ziv's strategy settles 1 for `ln` and `acosh` and 2^k for `log2`, whose results are `f64`s, because the evaluations give exact enclosures there, as the tests check. The arguments that no rule settles go to the driver, with the enclosures that `ev_ln_correct` and the others show hold the result at each level. These build on [`DyLogCore.v`](theories/DyLogCore.v). `reduce_correct` shows that where `reduce` returns at a `Dyadic` `d`, `d` is positive, and `reduce` gives an integer `e` with `|e| <= 2048` and an enclosure of `ln d - e ln 2`, negated if its flag `less` is set. `reduce` computes it as `2 atanh(|m - 1| / (m + 1))` for `m = d / 2^e` (`ln_atanh` and `ln_atanh_inv`). `atanh_series_correct` shows that `atanh_series`, where it returns, encloses atanh of every point of its argument, by Horner's scheme on the scaled remainders of the series of atanh (`atanh_tail` and `atanh_horner`, from `atanh_sum_bounds`). `ln_dy_correct` shows that `ln_dy`, which adds `e ln 2` with the enclosures of `ln2_correct`, encloses `ln d`. `ln_encl_correct` shows that `ln_encl` encloses ln of every point of an enclosure of positive numbers, which `asinh`, `acosh` and `atanh` take where they do not take the series:

```coq
Theorem ln_dir_correct :
  forall x up v,
  is_finite x = true -> log_dom (B2R x) -> DyadicLog.ln_dir_with ziv_strict x up = Some v ->
  mpfr_round f64_format (if up then RNDU else RNDD) (ln (B2R x)) (with_val v).

Lemma atanh_series_correct :
  forall n u c E uv,
  level_ok n -> encl_ok n u -> encl_le E u -> 0 <= E <= 2 ^ 30 -> encl_has u uv ->
  DyadicLog.atanh_series n u = Some c ->
  encl_ok n c /\ encl_le (2 ^ 43) c /\ encl_has c (Ratanh uv).
```

The fast path of `ln`, `log2` and `log10`, after their rules, and before the powers of 10 for `log10`, `ziv` and `ziv_near`, is `fast_ln(x)` and `fast_log_by(x, c, d)` of `src/dyadic/log.rs`, which compute the result times 2^-k, for an integer `k`, as an unevaluated sum `h + l` of two `f64`s with `h = RN(h + l)` and 1 <= |`h`| < 2, or return `None` at `x = 1`, where the result is 0, and the rounding tests `round_dir_signed` and `round_near_signed` of `src/dyadic/fast.rs`, which apply those of `exp` to `-h` and `-l` where `h < 0`, as the rounding of `-y` toward -∞ is the negative of that of `y` toward +∞. Both start with `ln_core(x)`, which, as CORE-MATH's `log`, writes `x` as `2^e m` with `m` in one of 362 intervals [(362 + i)/512, (363 + i)/512) that cover [362/512, 724/512), about [0.707, 1.414) (`ln_split`, which first scales a subnormal `x` by 2^52), and ln m as `-ln r + ln(1 + z)` with `z = r m - 1` for the `r` of the table `LOG_TABLE` for that interval. `ln_split_correct` and `ln_z_exact` in [`FastLogReduce.v`](theories/FastLogReduce.v) show that `ln_split` writes `x` exactly and that `z` is computed exactly as `z1 + z2`, with `two_prod_err` and Sterbenz's lemma. `ltbl_correct` in [`FastLogTables.v`](theories/FastLogTables.v) shows that each entry holds -ln r in double-double within 2^-104 and that |r m - 1| <= 3/2048 on its interval, or `r = 1` on the two intervals next to 1, one on each side, where |z| <= 2^-9, by `vm_compute` on integers, with -ln r = 2 atanh((1 - r)/(1 + r)) and the bounds of `atanh_sum_bounds`, and the theorems next to it bound the constants: ln 2 and log10(2) cut to 42 bits plus their rest, within 2^-100, and 1/ln 2 and 1/ln 10 in double-double within 2^-104 (`ln2_split_correct`, `c2_correct` and the others). `log1p_taylor` in [`FastLogTaylor.v`](theories/FastLogTaylor.v) bounds the remainder of the Taylor polynomial of ln(1 + z) of each degree d by |z|^(d+1)/((d + 1)(1 - |z|)) for |z| <= 1/2, and `log1p_shift` the error of taking ln(1 + z1) + z2 (1 - z1) for ln(1 + z1 + z2). [`FastLogHorner.v`](theories/FastLogHorner.v) and [`FastLogPoly.v`](theories/FastLogPoly.v) bound the round-off of `log1p_poly`, the polynomial of degree 8 (`log1p_horner_correct` and `log1p_poly_correct`), and `ln_core_correct` in [`FastLogCore.v`](theories/FastLogCore.v) combines them: `ln_core` gives `e` and `ah + al` within 3 2^-71 |ln m| of ln m, a bound relative to ln m, as the tests need near `x = 1`. `fast_ln_correct` in [`FastLogLn.v`](theories/FastLogLn.v), and `fast_log2_correct` and `fast_log10_correct` in [`FastLogBy.v`](theories/FastLogBy.v), with the product by `c` of [`FastLogByProd.v`](theories/FastLogByProd.v) and the steps of [`FastLogFin.v`](theories/FastLogFin.v), bound the distance of `h + l` from the result times 2^-k by 2^-68, from `normalize_correct` in [`FastLogNorm.v`](theories/FastLogNorm.v) and `ln_f64_ne1`, that ln x is at least 2^-54 in magnitude for an `f64` `x` other than 1. `round_dir_signed_correct` and `round_near_signed_correct` in [`FastSign.v`](theories/FastSign.v) extend `round_dir_correct` and `round_near_correct` to an `h` of either sign, and `fast_ln_dir_correct`, `fast_ln_near_correct` and the same for `log2` and `log10` in [`FastLog.v`](theories/FastLog.v) combine the two. As for `exp`, `Model.v` takes `HAS_FMA` as an argument `fma`, and `fast_ln_dir_fma` and the others show that both values give the same result at every finite `x > 0`, so that `DyadicLog.ln_dir_with` and the others pass `false`:

```coq
Theorem ln_core_correct :
  forall fma x, is_finite x = true -> 0 < B2R x ->
  let '(e, ah, al) := DyadicLog.ln_core fma x in
  is_finite ah = true /\ is_finite al = true /\ B2R ah = RN (B2R ah + B2R al) /\
  (-1074 <= e <= 1024)%Z /\ Rabs (ln (B2R x) - IZR e * ln 2) <= 7 / 20 /\
  Rabs (ln (B2R x) - IZR e * ln 2 - (B2R ah + B2R al)) <=
    3 * bpow radix2 (-71) * Rabs (ln (B2R x) - IZR e * ln 2).

Theorem round_dir_signed_correct :
  forall h l e up v y,
  is_finite h = true -> is_finite l = true -> B2R h = RN (B2R h + B2R l) ->
  / 2 < Rabs (B2R h) < 2 -> (-1021 <= e <= 1022)%Z ->
  Rabs (y - (B2R h + B2R l)) <= bpow radix2 (-68) ->
  Fast.round_dir_signed h l e up = Some v ->
  rounds up (y * bpow radix2 e) v.

Theorem fast_ln_dir_correct :
  forall fma x up v,
  is_finite x = true -> 0 < B2R x -> DyadicLog.fast_ln_dir fma x up = Some v ->
  rounds up (ln (B2R x)) v.

Theorem fast_ln_dir_fma :
  forall x up, is_finite x = true -> 0 < B2R x ->
  DyadicLog.fast_ln_dir true x up = DyadicLog.fast_ln_dir false x up.
```

The fast path of `asinh`, `acosh` and `atanh`, after their rules and before `ziv` and `ziv_near`, is `fast_asinh_dir`, `fast_acosh_dir`, `fast_atanh_dir` and their `_near` forms in `src/dyadic/log.rs`, which have two branches. Near 0 for asinh and atanh, at t = |x| < 2^-8, they call `odd_series(t, c)`, and near 1 for acosh, at x < 1 + 2^-8, `fast_acosh_near1(x)`, which compute the result times 2^-k as an unevaluated sum `h + l` as `fast_ln` does, within 2^-67, and they round it with the tests `round_dir_err` and `round_near_err` above at `err` = 2^-67 (`ERR67`). For x < 0, as asinh and atanh are odd, they round f(t) in the other direction and negate it, as for `sinh`. `odd_series(t, c)` takes t + t^3 P(t^2), the odd Taylor polynomial of degree 9 with the coefficients `c` of P rounded to nearest: RN(-1/6), RN(3/40), RN(-5/112) and RN(35/1152) for asinh (`ASINH_ODD`) and RN(1/3), RN(1/5), RN(1/7) and RN(1/9) for atanh (`ATANH_ODD`). With u = RN(t^2), it computes P(u) by Horner's scheme in `f64` and the correction c = RN(RN(t u) P(u)), adds it to t with Fast2Sum, which is exact as |c| < t, and scales `h + l` into [1, 2) with `normalize`. `asinh_odd_correct` and `atanh_odd_correct` in [`FastAhypOdd.v`](theories/FastAhypOdd.v) show that, for 2^-27 <= t < 2^-8, `h + l` is within 2^-67 of asinh t 2^-k and of atanh t 2^-k, from `asinh_taylor9` in [`AsinhTaylor.v`](theories/AsinhTaylor.v), which bounds the remainder of the polynomial of asinh by t^11/40 for 0 <= t <= 1/16, and from `atanh_sum_bounds`. `fast_acosh_near1(x)` takes acosh(1 + d) = 2 asinh √(d/2) (`acosh_asinh_half` in [`FastAhypNear1.v`](theories/FastAhypNear1.v), for d >= 0): d = x - 1 is exact by Sterbenz's lemma, and so is u = d/2, s = √u is `dd_sqrt(u, 0)`, and asinh s is taken as s (1 + w) with w = -u/6 + u^2 (A5 + A7 u + ... + A15 u^5), its Taylor polynomial of degree 15 with the coefficients 3/40 to -143/10240 of s^5 to s^15 rounded to nearest as `A5` to `A15`. u/6 is u times `SH3` of `src/dyadic/exp.rs`, 1/6 in double-double, with u `SH3[0]` = mh + ml exactly by `two_prod_err`, the rest of w is in `f64`, s (1 + w) is summed with sh mh = ph + pe exactly by `two_prod_err` and two Fast2Sums, and the factor 2 goes into k. `acosh_near1_correct` shows that, for 1 <= x < 1 + 2^-8, `h + l` is within 2^-67 of acosh x 2^-k, from `asinh_taylor15`, which bounds the remainder of that polynomial by s^17/80 for 0 <= s <= 1/16, from `dd_sqrt_correct` below, and from the lemmas `near1_u` and `near1_w` of [`FastAhypNear1Poly.v`](theories/FastAhypNear1Poly.v), for u and the low part of w, and `near1_sum` of [`FastAhypNear1Sum.v`](theories/FastAhypNear1Sum.v), for the sums, and `acosh_near1_fma` that it returns the same with and without an FMA. Elsewhere they call `fast_asinh(x)`, `fast_acosh(x)` and `fast_atanh(x)`, which compute the result times 2^-k as `h + l` within 2^-68, or return `None`, and round it with the tests `round_dir_signed` and `round_near_signed`. `fast_asinh` and `fast_atanh` call `fast_asinh_pos(t)` and `fast_atanh_pos(t)` at t = |x| and negate `h` and `l` for x < 0, as asinh and atanh are odd (`fast_asinh_correct` and `fast_atanh_correct` in [`FastAhyp.v`](theories/FastAhyp.v)). They take asinh t = ln(t + √(1 + t^2)), acosh x = ln(x + √(x^2 - 1)) and atanh t = ln((1 + t)/(1 - t))/2. The square roots are `dd_sqrt(ah, al)` of `src/dyadic/fast.rs`, Dekker's square root of a double-double: s = RN(√ah), the residual ah - s^2, which it computes exactly, as s^2 = p + pe exactly with `two_prod_err`, ah - p is exact by Sterbenz's lemma and the remainder of a square root rounded to nearest is an `f64`, and one correction step, ((ah - p - pe) + al)/(2s), added with Fast2Sum. `dd_sqrt_correct` in [`FastDdSqrt.v`](theories/FastDdSqrt.v) shows that it is within 2^-100 of √(ah + al), relative, for 2^-900 <= ah <= 2^900 and |al| <= 2^-52 ah, and `dd_sqrt_fma` that it returns the same with and without an FMA. 1 + t^2 is exact up to one rounding of 2^-105 of it, and x^2 - 1 is exact below 2^26. The quotient of atanh is `dd_div` of `FastDiv.v` above. `fast_ln_dd(xh, xl, e0)` takes e0 ln 2 + ln(xh + xl) as e0 ln 2 + ln xh + xl/xh, with `ln_core` of xh, as ln(1 + d) is d within d^2 for |d| <= 2^-51, and `fast_ln_dd_correct` shows that, for xh > 0, |xl| <= 2^-51 xh, 0 <= e0 <= 64 and xh 2^e0 <= 2^1025, `h + l` is within 2^-68 of y 2^-k for every y of magnitude at least 2^-20 within 2^-80 of e0 ln 2 + ln(xh + xl), relative. atanh takes it at the quotient (1 + t)/((1 - t) 2^-j) with e0 = -j, where `normalize` scales 1 - t into [1, 2) by 2^-j, and asinh and acosh at the sum of the argument and the square root in double-double, with e0 = 0, below 2^26. From 2^26 on, asinh t and acosh x are taken as ln 2 + ln(t + RN(1/(4t))) and ln 2 + ln(x + RN(-1/(4x))), with e0 = 1, which need no t^2 and so do not overflow, as 2 (t + 1/(4t)) and 2 (x - 1/(4x)) are within 1/(8t^3) and 1/(4x^3) of t + √(1 + t^2) and x + √(x^2 - 1). `fast_asinh_pos_correct`, `fast_acosh_correct` and `fast_atanh_pos_correct` in [`FastAhypAsinh.v`](theories/FastAhypAsinh.v), [`FastAhypAcosh.v`](theories/FastAhypAcosh.v) and [`FastAhypAtanh.v`](theories/FastAhypAtanh.v) show that `h + l` is within 2^-68 of the result times 2^-k, with 1/2 < |h| < 2, for t >= 2^-8, for x >= 1 + 2^-8 and for 2^-8 <= t < 1, and `fast_asinh_dir_correct`, `fast_asinh_near_correct` and the same for `acosh` and `atanh` in `FastAhyp.v` that the fast path returns, where it returns, the result rounded in its direction or to nearest, at every finite x with |x| >= 2^-27, x >= 1 and 2^-27 <= |x| < 1, which include every argument that the rules leave. `fast_acosh_near1` returns `None` for x <= 1, and `fast_acosh_one` shows that the fast path of `acosh` rounded in a direction returns `None` at 1, where the result is 0. As for `ln`, `fast_asinh_dir_fma` and the others show that both values of `fma` give the same result, so that `DyadicLog.asinh_dir_with` and the others pass `false`:

```coq
Theorem asinh_odd_correct :
  forall t h l k,
  is_finite t = true -> bpow radix2 (-27) <= B2R t < bpow radix2 (-8) ->
  DyadicLog.odd_series t DyadicLog.ASINH_ODD = (h, l, k) ->
  is_finite h = true /\ is_finite l = true /\ B2R h = RN (B2R h + B2R l) /\
  (-1021 <= k <= 1022)%Z /\
  Rabs (arcsinh (B2R t) * bpow radix2 (- k) - (B2R h + B2R l)) <= bpow radix2 (-67).

Theorem acosh_near1_correct :
  forall fma x h l k,
  is_finite x = true -> 1 <= B2R x < 1 + bpow radix2 (-8) ->
  DyadicLog.fast_acosh_near1 fma x = Some (h, l, k) ->
  is_finite h = true /\ is_finite l = true /\ B2R h = RN (B2R h + B2R l) /\
  (-1021 <= k <= 1022)%Z /\
  Rabs (Racosh (B2R x) * bpow radix2 (- k) - (B2R h + B2R l)) <= bpow radix2 (-67).

Theorem dd_sqrt_correct :
  forall fma ah al sh sl,
  is_finite ah = true -> is_finite al = true ->
  bpow radix2 (-900) <= B2R ah <= bpow radix2 900 ->
  Rabs (B2R al) <= bpow radix2 (-52) * B2R ah ->
  Fast.dd_sqrt fma ah al = (sh, sl) ->
  is_finite sh = true /\ is_finite sl = true /\ B2R sh = RN (B2R sh + B2R sl) /\
  Rabs (B2R sh + B2R sl - sqrt (B2R ah + B2R al)) <= bpow radix2 (-100) * sqrt (B2R ah + B2R al).

Theorem fast_asinh_pos_correct :
  forall fma t h l k,
  is_finite t = true -> bpow radix2 (-8) <= B2R t ->
  DyadicLog.fast_asinh_pos fma t = Some (h, l, k) ->
  is_finite h = true /\ is_finite l = true /\ B2R h = RN (B2R h + B2R l) /\
  / 2 < Rabs (B2R h) < 2 /\ (-1021 <= k <= 1022)%Z /\
  Rabs (arcsinh (B2R t) * bpow radix2 (- k) - (B2R h + B2R l)) <= bpow radix2 (-68).

Theorem fast_asinh_dir_correct :
  forall fma x up v,
  is_finite x = true -> bpow radix2 (-27) <= Rabs (B2R x) -> DyadicLog.fast_asinh_dir fma x up = Some v ->
  rounds up (arcsinh (B2R x)) v.
```

`k_ln_c` to `k_atanh_c` in `DyPoint.v` show that `k_ln`, `k_log2`, `k_log10`, `k_asinh`, `k_acosh` and `k_atanh`, the kernel's six functions as fields of a record `point`, satisfy `ln_c`, `log2_c`, `log10_c`, `asinh_c`, `acosh_c` and `atanh_c` of `Contract.v`, with `kernel_field_correct_dom` for the domains other than `real_dom`, so that `kernel_ln_correct` to `kernel_atanh_correct` give `ln_correct` to `atanh_correct` for a record `point` whose fields are these:

```coq
Theorem k_ln_c : ln_c k_ln.

Theorem kernel_ln_correct :
  forall x, valid x -> is_tightest (image_dom log_dom ln x) (Interval.ln kernel_point x).
```

The tests in `src/dyadic/log/tests.rs` take these six functions, in both directions, at the special values, at 2^k, at `1e0` to `1e22` and at 1, at each rule's threshold and the arguments next to it, where the reductions change their `e` or their branch, at arguments whose results are close to an `f64`, at the hardest arguments that a search found, and at random arguments, uniform in intervals and log-uniform over the exponents. They check them against the roundings that bounds on the functions determine, where these do, and, on 64-bit Linux and macOS, against those of `src/mpfr_calls/mpfr.rs`, which call MPFR. Those in `src/dyadic/log/tests/mpfr_cases.rs` take the arguments of MPFR's own tests of these functions, with MPFR's results at 53 bits where those tests give them, and those in `src/dyadic/log/tests/levels.rs` force each level from 1 to 64 limbs at a few arguments, as for the exponentials above.

`atan_dir_correct` in [`DyAtan.v`](theories/DyAtan.v) shows that `atan(x, up)` of `src/dyadic/atan.rs`, which computes atan x with a fast path in double-double and with the kernel, returns, at a finite `x` and wherever it returns by a rule, by the fast path or by a rounding test of `ziv` that passes, atan x rounded toward +∞ if `up` and toward -∞ otherwise, as `mpfr_round` says, and `atan_dir_side` that it returns there, wherever it returns rather than panics, a value on the side of atan x that the rounding is on, as for e^x above. `atan2_dir_correct` in `DyAtan.v`, and `asin_dir_correct` and `acos_dir_correct` in [`DyAsin.v`](theories/DyAsin.v), and `atan2_dir_side`, `asin_dir_side` and `acos_dir_side`, state the same of `atan2`, `asin` and `acos`, with `Ratan2`, `asin` and `acos` in place of `atan`, at finite `y` and `x` with `y <> 0` or `x > 0` for `atan2(y, x, up)`, and at a finite `x` in `asin_dom` and in `acos_dom`, which are `[-1, 1]`. `DyadicAtan.atan_dir x up` in `Model.v` is `atan(x, up)`, with `None` where the Rust panics, and `atan_dir_with`, `atan_dir_strict` and `atan_dir_post` are as for e^x, except that `atan_dir_post` also asks of `post` that it carry over between two reals with no `f64` between them (`gap_post`, see `atan_huge_post` below). Each function first settles some arguments by rules: NaN, the infinities, the arguments outside the domains of `asin` and `acos`, the zeros and infinities of `atan2`, where it returns the values that the tests check against `mpfr_atan2`, those of magnitude at least 2^54 for `atan`, whose results round as ±π/2 does, those of magnitude below 2^-26 for `atan` and 2^-27 for `asin`, whose results are within an ulp of `x`, and, for `atan2` with `x > 0`, the quotients `y / x` that are `f64`s of magnitude below 2^-26, whose results are within an ulp of `y / x`. At the finite arguments in the domains, the theorems above meet the rules `atan_huge_post` in `DyAtan.v` and `atan_tiny_rule` and `asin_tiny_rule` in [`DyAtanTiny.v`](theories/DyAtanTiny.v), with `tiny_quotient_correct` there for the quotients of `atan2`, which `tiny_quotient` decides in `f64` for `x < 2^996`, with `exact_div`, whose `f64` is `y / x` exactly (`exact_div_correct` in [`AtanExactDiv.v`](theories/AtanExactDiv.v)), and with the division of `Dyadic`s for `x >= 2^996` (`tiny_quotient_dyadic_correct`), and the values of `atan2` at `y = ±0`, which is `y` for `x > 0`, and at `x = ±0`, which `pi_times` rounds as ±π/2 (`pi_times_post`). Each of these lemmas shows that its rule returns the rounding in its direction, or, for `atan_huge_post` and `pi_times_post`, a value with the property `post` of the driver for a `post` that the correct rounding also has (`rounds_post`): `pi_times` returns `c π` rounded from the constants of `pi_bounds` at `|c|` = 1/4, 1/2, 3/4 and 1, which include the ±1/2 of these rules (`pi_const_rounds` in [`AtanPiConst.v`](theories/AtanPiConst.v)), and calls the driver only at other `c`. `atan_dir_infinity`, `atan2_dir_inf_pos`, `atan2_dir_inf_neg_pos`, `atan2_dir_inf_neg_neg` and `atan2_dir_y_inf` give the results at the infinities that `Contract.v` asks for. The functions then try the fast path, which returns, where it returns, the rounding in its direction (`fast_atan_dir_correct` below). The fast path declines `acos` at 1, whose result 0 is an `f64`: Ziv's strategy settles it, as its evaluation gives the exact enclosure [0, 0] there (`ev_acos_one` in `DyAtanExact.v`, below). The other arguments go to the driver, with the enclosures that `ev_atan_correct`, `ev_atan2_correct`, `ev_asin_correct` and `ev_acos_correct` show hold the result at each level. These build on [`DyAtanCore.v`](theories/DyAtanCore.v). `DyAtanCore.series_correct` shows that `series`, where it returns, encloses atan of every point of its argument, by Horner's scheme on the scaled remainders of the series of atan (`atan_tail_bounds` and `atan_horner` in [`AtanSeries.v`](theories/AtanSeries.v)), from an enclosure of `[0, 1/(2k+1)]` for the remainder after the `k = terms n z` terms that it sums, at most 898 (`ac_terms`). `atan_series_correct` shows the same of `atan_series`, which takes `series` at its argument `u` if the upper bound of `u` is at most 2^-8, and otherwise twice `series` at `u / (1 + √(1 + u^2))` (`atan_halve`). `atan_encl_correct` shows that `atan_encl`, where it returns, encloses atan of every point of its argument `w`, whose lower bound its assertion keeps at least 0, as `π/4 - atan((1 - w)/(1 + w))`, `π/4 + atan((w - 1)/(w + 1))` or `π/2 - atan(1/w)` where it does not take the series at `w` (`atan_quarter_minus`, `atan_quarter_plus` and `atan_half_inv` in [`AtanIdent.v`](theories/AtanIdent.v)), and `asin_parts_correct` that `asin_parts`, where it returns, encloses asin t or acos t for `0 <= t <= 1`, as atan of `t / √((1 - t)(1 + t))` or of its inverse (`asin_as_atan` and `acos_as_atan`). `pi_correct` in [`DyConstPi.v`](theories/DyConstPi.v) shows that `pi` encloses π. The table of π in `src/dyadic/consts.rs` is `floor(π * 2^4222)`, its first 4,224 bits, and `DyConstPi.v` shows it with `vm_compute` of sums of the series of atan in Machin's formula `π = 16 atan(1/5) - 4 atan(1/239)` (Stdlib's `Machin_4_5_239`) at 4,288 bits, each term floored, whose remainders `atan_tail_bounds` bounds:

```coq
Theorem atan2_dir_correct :
  forall y x up v,
  is_finite y = true -> is_finite x = true -> (B2R y <> 0 \/ 0 < B2R x)%R ->
  DyadicAtan.atan2_dir_with ziv_strict y x up = Some v ->
  mpfr_round f64_format (if up then RNDU else RNDD) (Ratan2 (B2R y) (B2R x)) (with_val v).

Lemma atan_encl_correct :
  forall n w c E wv,
  level_ok n -> encl_ok n w -> encl_le E w -> 0 <= E <= 2 ^ 30 -> encl_has w wv ->
  DyadicAtan.atan_encl n w = Some c ->
  encl_ok n c /\ encl_le (2 ^ 51) c /\ encl_has c (atan wv).

Lemma pi_correct :
  forall n p,
  level_ok n -> DyadicConsts.pi n = Some p ->
  encl_ok n p /\ encl_le (2 ^ 15) p /\ encl_has p PI.
```

The fast path of `atan`, `atan2`, `asin` and `acos`, after their rules and before `ziv` and `ziv_near`, is `fast_atan_pos(t)`, `fast_atan2_pos(a, b, neg)`, `fast_asin_pos(t)` and `fast_acos_val(x)` of `src/dyadic/atan.rs`, which compute atan t, atan2(a, x) for `a = |y|`, `b = |x|` and `neg` the sign of `x`, asin t and acos x as `(h, l, e, err)`, an unevaluated sum `h + l` of two `f64`s with `h = RN(h + l)`, within `err` of the result times 2^-e, or return `None`, and the rounding tests `round_dir_err` and `round_near_err` of `src/dyadic/fast.rs`, to which `atan`, `asin` and `atan2` give their results at `|x|` and `|y|`, and whose results they negate for a negative `x` or `y`. `fast_atan_core(th, tl)` takes atan t, for a double-double `t = th + tl` in [2^-61, 129/128], as atan c + atan u for c = i/64, with i the integer nearest 64 th, and u = (t - c)/(1 + t c) (`atan_sub_table` in [`FastAtanIdent.v`](theories/FastAtanIdent.v), from Stdlib's `Machin.atan_sub_correct`), with atan c from the table `ATAN_TABLE` of `src/dyadic/atan/tables.rs` and u from `dd_div`, so that |u| <= 2^-7. `th - c` is exact by Sterbenz's lemma, and `th c` is exact as a pair; the core returns `None` where 0 < |t - c| < 2^-80, a numerator that `dd_div` does not take. [`FastAtanTables.v`](theories/FastAtanTables.v) shows that the table holds atan(i/64) for i in [1, 64] in double-double within 2^-104 (`fast_atan_table_correct`, by `vm_compute` in fixed point with 160 fractional bits, with atan(i/64) between partial sums of its series, `atan_tail_bounds`, for i <= 32, and as π/4 - atan((64 - i)/(64 + i)) above, with π from `PI_bounds` of `DyConstPi.v`), that `PIO2` is π/2 within 2^-107 (`fast_pio2_correct`), and bounds the coefficients of the polynomial (`fast_atan_consts`). `fast_atan_poly_correct` in [`FastAtanPoly.v`](theories/FastAtanPoly.v) shows that `fast_atan_poly` computes the odd Taylor polynomial of atan u to degree 11, with u^3/3 in double-double, within 2^-78 |u| of atan u, for a double-double u = uh + ul with 2^-82 <= |uh| <= 2^-7 and |ul| <= 2^-53 |uh|, and `fast_atan_core_correct` in [`FastAtanCore.v`](theories/FastAtanCore.v) that the core is within 2^-74 of atan t, relative, where it returns. `fast_atan_pos` takes the core at t up to 1, and π/2 - atan(1/t) above it, with 1/t from `dd_div`. `fast_atan2_pos` takes atan q for q the quotient of the smaller of `a` and `b` by the larger, from `dd_div` on their significands, and then atan q, π - atan q, π/2 - atan q or π/2 + atan q by the octant, as the kernel's `Atan2` does; it takes q itself, or RN(q) beside π/2 or π, where the exponents of `a` and `b` differ by more than 60, and declines the results below 2^-1021, as the rounding tests scale by 2^e only for e >= -1021. `pio2_add_correct`, `split_correct` and `octant_correct` in [`FastAtanAsm.v`](theories/FastAtanAsm.v) bound these steps, and [`FastAtanQuot.v`](theories/FastAtanQuot.v) the quotients. `fast_asin_pos` and `fast_acos_val` take atan(t/r) up to `ASIN_S` = 181/256 and atan(r/t) above it, which are asin t and acos t (`asin_q` in [`FastAsinAngle.v`](theories/FastAsinAngle.v)), for r = √(1 - t^2) from `fast_sqrt1m`, with `dd_sqrt` and `dd_sqrt_correct` above, and `fast_sqrt1m_correct` in [`FastAsinSqrt.v`](theories/FastAsinSqrt.v) that r in double-double is within 2^-99 of it, relative. `fast_acos_val` takes π/2 - x in double-double at |x| < 2^-27, where no rule settles `acos`, and declines 1. `fast_atan_pos_correct` and `fast_atan2_pos_correct` in [`FastAtan.v`](theories/FastAtan.v), and `fast_asin_pos_correct` and `fast_acos_val_correct` in [`FastAsin.v`](theories/FastAsin.v), show that the four functions return `err = 2^-p` for a p in [55, 105] and `e` in [-1021, 1022], the bounds that `round_dir_err_correct` and `round_near_err_correct` above take, and `fast_atan_dir_correct` and the others combine the two: where they return, the fast paths return the rounding in their direction, or to nearest, at 2^-27 <= |x| < 2^54 for `atan`, 2^-27 <= |x| <= 1 for `asin`, |x| <= 1 for `acos`, and finite `y` and `x` other than 0 for `atan2`, which include all the arguments that the rules leave. Each dir and near function first tries a first stage, `fast_atan_pos1(t)`, `fast_atan2_pos1(a, b, neg)`, `fast_asin_pos1(t)` or `fast_acos_val1(x)`, and its rounding test, and takes the path above, the second stage, only where one of them returns `None`. The first stages compute the same angles as the second by the same identities, with `fast_atan1(th, tl)` in place of the core: where the core's i is 0, it takes the odd Taylor polynomial of atan to degree 9 at th, plus tl (`poly9_err` in [`FastAtan1Poly.v`](theories/FastAtan1Poly.v)), and otherwise the core's c, n and d (`core_index`, `core_num` and `core_den` of `FastAtanCore.v`) and atan c from `ATAN_TABLE`, and then, in plain `f64`, uh = RN(nh / dh) and the rest of the quotient n / d from the remainder nh - uh dh (`atan1_div` in [`FastAtan1Div.v`](theories/FastAtan1Div.v)), and atan u from the same polynomial. `fast_atan1_correct` in [`FastAtan1.v`](theories/FastAtan1.v) shows that it is within 2^-64 of atan t, relative, where it returns, and it returns `None` where the high part nh of n is below 2^-80 in magnitude. `fast_atan_pos1` takes 1/t above 1 from `fast_recip(t)`, and `fast_atan2_pos1` the quotient of the significands from `fast_quot`, which `fast_recip_correct` and `fast_quot_correct` in [`FastAtan1Quot.v`](theories/FastAtan1Quot.v) show to be within 2^-104 of 1/t and 2^-105 of the quotient, relative. `fast_asin_atan1` takes the quotient of `fast_asin_atan`, from `fast_sqrt1m` and `dd_div`, and its angle is within 2^-64 + 2^-84 of asin t or acos t, relative (`fast_asin_atan1_correct`). `octant_bound` in `FastAtanAsm.v` shows that `octant`, for an input within d of a v in (0, 4/5], absolute where it returns π/2 ± v or π - v and relative, with v >= 2^-1020, where it returns v, and for 2^-100 <= d <= 2^-60, returns h + l within 3 d of the angle times 2^-e. The first stages return `err` = `ERR62` = 2^-62 where they call `fast_atan1`, and `ERR70` = 2^-70 where they do not: in `fast_atan2_pos1` where the exponents of `a` and `b` differ by more than 60, in `fast_asin_pos1` at t = 1, and in `fast_acos_val1` at |x| < 2^-27 and at x = -1. In the first of these arms, `fast_atan2_pos1` returns `Some None` for the results below 2^-1021, and the fast path of `atan2` then declines without the second stage. `fast_atan_pos1_correct`, `fast_atan2_pos1_correct`, `fast_asin_pos1_correct` and `fast_acos_val1_correct` show of the first stages what the four theorems above show of the second, and `fast_atan_dir_correct` and the others above are about the dir and near functions with both stages. As for e^x, `fast_atan_poly`, `fast_atan_core`, `fast_atan1`, `fast_quot`, `fast_recip`, `fast_sqrt1m`, `dd_div` and `dd_sqrt` compute the errors of their exact products with `two_prod_err::<HAS_FMA>`, the theorems hold for either value of `fma`, and `fast_atan_dir_fma` and the others show that both give the same result, so that `DyadicAtan.atan_dir_with` and the others pass `false`. The same holds of `exact_div`, which `tiny_quotient` and `midpoint_quotient` call before the fast path, and which computes the error of its product with `two_prod_err::<HAS_FMA>`: `exact_div_fma` shows that both give the same at finite `y` other than 0 with `|y| < x < 2^996`, where they call it, so that they pass `false`:

```coq
Theorem fast_atan_core_correct :
  forall fma th tl ah al,
  is_finite th = true -> is_finite tl = true ->
  bpow radix2 (-61) <= B2R th <= 129 / 128 -> Rabs (B2R tl) <= bpow radix2 (-53) * B2R th ->
  DyadicAtan.fast_atan_core fma th tl = Some (ah, al) ->
  is_finite ah = true /\ is_finite al = true /\ B2R ah = RN (B2R ah + B2R al) /\
  Rabs (B2R ah + B2R al - atan (B2R th + B2R tl)) <= bpow radix2 (-74) * atan (B2R th + B2R tl).

Theorem fast_atan1_correct :
  forall fma th tl ah al,
  is_finite th = true -> is_finite tl = true ->
  bpow radix2 (-61) <= B2R th <= 129 / 128 -> Rabs (B2R tl) <= bpow radix2 (-53) * B2R th ->
  DyadicAtan.fast_atan1 fma th tl = Some (ah, al) ->
  is_finite ah = true /\ is_finite al = true /\ B2R ah = RN (B2R ah + B2R al) /\
  Rabs (B2R ah + B2R al - atan (B2R th + B2R tl)) <= bpow radix2 (-64) * atan (B2R th + B2R tl).

Theorem fast_atan2_pos_correct :
  forall fma a b neg h l e err,
  is_finite a = true -> is_finite b = true -> 0 < B2R a -> 0 < B2R b ->
  DyadicAtan.fast_atan2_pos fma a b neg = Some (h, l, e, err) ->
  is_finite h = true /\ is_finite l = true /\ B2R h = RN (B2R h + B2R l) /\
  (-1021 <= e <= 1022)%Z /\
  exists p, (55 <= p <= 105)%Z /\ B2R err = bpow radix2 (- p) /\
    Rabs (Ratan2 (B2R a) (if neg then - B2R b else B2R b) * bpow radix2 (- e) - (B2R h + B2R l))
      <= bpow radix2 (- p).

Theorem fast_atan2_pos1_correct :
  forall fma a b neg h l e err,
  is_finite a = true -> is_finite b = true -> 0 < B2R a -> 0 < B2R b ->
  DyadicAtan.fast_atan2_pos1 fma a b neg = Some (Some (h, l, e, err)) ->
  is_finite h = true /\ is_finite l = true /\ B2R h = RN (B2R h + B2R l) /\
  (-1021 <= e <= 1022)%Z /\
  exists p, (55 <= p <= 105)%Z /\ B2R err = bpow radix2 (- p) /\
    Rabs (Ratan2 (B2R a) (if neg then - B2R b else B2R b) * bpow radix2 (- e) - (B2R h + B2R l))
      <= bpow radix2 (- p).

Theorem fast_acos_dir_correct :
  forall fma x up v,
  is_finite x = true -> Rabs (B2R x) <= 1 ->
  DyadicAtan.fast_acos_dir fma x up = Some v ->
  rounds up (acos (B2R x)) v.

Theorem fast_atan_dir_fma :
  forall x up,
  is_finite x = true -> bpow radix2 (-27) <= Rabs (B2R x) < bpow radix2 54 ->
  DyadicAtan.fast_atan_dir true x up = DyadicAtan.fast_atan_dir false x up.

Lemma exact_div_correct :
  forall fma y x q,
  is_finite y = true -> is_finite x = true ->
  Rabs (B2R y) < B2R x < bpow radix2 996 ->
  DyadicAtan.exact_div fma y x = Some q ->
  is_finite q = true /\ B2R q = B2R y / B2R x.

Theorem exact_div_fma :
  forall y x,
  is_finite y = true -> is_finite x = true -> B2R y <> 0 ->
  Rabs (B2R y) < B2R x < bpow radix2 996 ->
  DyadicAtan.exact_div true y x = DyadicAtan.exact_div false y x.
```

`k_atan_c`, `k_asin_c`, `k_acos_c` and `k_atan2_c` in `DyPoint.v` show that `k_atan`, `k_asin`, `k_acos` and `k_atan2`, the kernel's four functions as fields of a record `point`, satisfy `atan_c`, `asin_c`, `acos_c` and `atan2_c` of `Contract.v`, with `kernel_field_correct_dom` for `asin` and `acos`, and `kernel_field2_correct`, its counterpart for a function of two `f64`s, for `atan2`, so that `kernel_atan_correct` to `kernel_atan2_correct` give `atan_correct`, `asin_correct`, `acos_correct` and `atan2_correct` for a record `point` whose fields are these:

```coq
Theorem k_atan2_c : atan2_c k_atan2.

Theorem kernel_atan2_correct :
  forall y x, valid y -> valid x ->
  exists z, Interval.atan2 kernel_point y x = Some z /\
    is_tightest (image2_set atan2_dom Ratan2 y x) z.
```

The tests in `src/dyadic/atan/tests.rs` take these four functions, in both directions, at the special values, at each rule's threshold and the arguments next to it, at the edges of the cases of `atan_encl` and `asin_parts`, where `atan_series` starts to halve its argument, at arguments whose results are close to an `f64`, at the hardest arguments that searches found, and at random arguments, of any exponent, uniform in intervals and log-uniform over the exponents, for `atan2` in all four quadrants and with `|y / x|` from about 2^-2097 to 2^2097. They check that the two directions give the same `f64` or two adjacent ones, or NaN both, and, on 64-bit Linux and macOS, that they give those of `src/mpfr_calls/mpfr.rs`, which call MPFR, bit for bit, and that the table of π is `floor(π * 2^4222)`, from π rounded down and up by MPFR at 4,300 bits. Others there check that the four functions give the same `f64`s with the fast path as without it, and, on 64-bit Linux and macOS, as MPFR, at random arguments of any exponent and of each branch of the fast path, at the edges of its table, of the index into it, of its reductions and of its ranges, `acos` at 0 and at the powers of 2 below 2^-27 and the arguments of each rule of `atan2` among them, and at arguments where its rounding tests pass but would fail with a bound 2^6 times as large, and, on 64-bit Linux and macOS, that its table and its constants hold against MPFR at 300 bits. Those in `src/dyadic/atan/tests/mpfr_cases.rs` take the arguments of MPFR's own tests of these functions, with MPFR's results at 53 bits where those tests give them, and those in `src/dyadic/atan/tests/levels.rs` force each level from 1 to 64 limbs at a few arguments, and for π itself, as for the exponentials above.

`sin_dir_correct` in [`DyTrig.v`](theories/DyTrig.v) shows that `sin(x, up)` of `src/dyadic/trig.rs`, which computes sin x with a fast path in double-double and with the kernel, returns, at a finite `x` and wherever it returns by a rule, by the fast path or by a rounding test of `ziv` that passes, sin x rounded toward +∞ if `up` and toward -∞ otherwise, as `mpfr_round` says, and `sin_dir_side` that it returns there, wherever it returns rather than panics, a value on the side of sin x that the rounding is on, as for e^x above. `cos_dir_correct` and `tan_dir_correct`, and `cos_dir_side` and `tan_dir_side`, state the same of `cos` and `tan`, at every finite `x`: `tan_dir_correct` and `tan_dir_side` need no hypothesis on the domain of tan, as `ev_tan` returns only where the divisor of its quotient excludes 0. `DyadicTrig.sin_dir x up` in `Model.v` is `sin(x, up)`, with `None` where the Rust panics, and `sin_dir_with`, `sin_dir_strict` and `sin_dir_post` are as for e^x. Each function first settles some arguments by rules: NaN and the infinities, at which it returns NaN and of which `Contract.v` asks nothing, those of magnitude below 2^-26, whose results are within an ulp of `x` for sin and tan and of 1 for cos, and some of those of magnitude in [2^-26, 2^-13) for sin and [2^-26, 2^-14) for cos and tan, at which the first two terms of the series and a bound on the rest put the result strictly between two adjacent `f64`s, as `sin_small`, `cos_small` and `tan_small` compute in integers from the bits of `|x|`. At the finite arguments, the theorems above meet the rules `sin_tiny_rule`, `cos_tiny_rule` and `tan_tiny_rule` in [`DyTrigTiny.v`](theories/DyTrigTiny.v), each of which shows that its rule returns the rounding in its direction, and `sin_small_rule`, `cos_small_rule` and `tan_small_rule` in [`DySinSmall.v`](theories/DySinSmall.v), [`DyCosSmall.v`](theories/DyCosSmall.v) and [`DyTanSmall.v`](theories/DyTanSmall.v), each of which shows the same wherever its rule returns an `f64` rather than leaving the argument to `ziv`. The functions then try the fast path, which returns, where it returns, the rounding in its direction (`fast_sin_dir_correct` below). The other arguments go to the driver, with the enclosures that `ev_sin_correct`, `ev_cos_correct` and `ev_tan_correct` show hold the result at each level. Each evaluation takes `t = |x|`, as sin and tan are odd and cos is even, and reduces it: `reduce_correct` in [`DyTrigReduce.v`](theories/DyTrigReduce.v) shows that `t = k π/2 + ρ`, or `t = k π/2 - ρ`, for an integer `k` whose `k mod 4` is the quadrant that `reduce` returns and a `ρ` in the enclosure that it returns, with `|ρ| <= 1` from its `assert!`. `reduce` divides `t 2^(s+1)`, an integer, by the top `l` limbs `P` of the table of π (`div_rem_correct` of [`Knuth.v`](theories/Knuth.v)), and `PI_top` bounds `t - k π/2` for whatever integer `k` the division gives, so that `k` is the nearest integer to `t / (π/2)` is not needed and not proved. `DyTrigCore.series_correct` in [`DyTrigCore.v`](theories/DyTrigCore.v) shows that `series`, where it returns, encloses sin or cos of every point of its argument in `[-1, 1]`, by Horner's scheme on the scaled remainders of the series of sin and cos (`trig_tail_bounds` and `trig_horner` in [`TrigSeries.v`](theories/TrigSeries.v)), from the enclosure `[0, 1]` of the remainder after the `terms n z` terms that it sums, at most 389 (`tc_terms`), and `sin_k_correct` that `sin_k` encloses `sin(k π/2 + ρ)` or `sin(k π/2 - ρ)` for every `k` with the `k mod 4` that it takes (`sin_quad`). `ev_cos` takes cos t as sin(t + π/2), with the quadrant plus 1, and `ev_tan` divides sin ρ by cos ρ for an even `k` and cos ρ by sin ρ for an odd one (`tan_quad`), with cos ρ as `√((1 - sin ρ)(1 + sin ρ))` (`cos_sqrt`):

```coq
Theorem sin_dir_correct :
  forall x up v,
  is_finite x = true -> DyadicTrig.sin_dir_with ziv_strict x up = Some v ->
  mpfr_round f64_format (if up then RNDU else RNDD) (sin (B2R x)) (with_val v).

Lemma reduce_correct :
  forall n t quad neg r,
  level_ok n -> is_finite t = true -> (0 <= B2R t)%R ->
  DyadicTrig.reduce n t = Some (quad, neg, r) ->
  encl_ok n r /\ encl_le (2 ^ 15) r /\
  exists k rho, k mod 4 = quad /\ encl_has r rho /\ (-1 <= rho <= 1)%R /\
    B2R t = (IZR k * (PI / 2) + (if neg then - rho else rho))%R.
```

The fast path of `sin`, `cos` and `tan`, after their rules and before `ziv` and `ziv_near`, is `fast_sin(x)`, `fast_cos(t)` and `fast_tan(x)` of `src/dyadic/trig.rs`, which compute sin x, cos t and tan x as an unevaluated sum `h + l` of two `f64`s with `h = RN(h + l)`, or return `None`, and the rounding tests `fast_round_dir` and `fast_round_near`, which scale `h` and `l` by a power of 2 so that |`h`| lies in [1, 2) and call `round_dir_signed` and `round_near_signed` of `src/dyadic/fast.rs`. For |x| < 2^16, `fast_reduce` writes x as n u + ρ for u = π/4096 and an integer n, with u in three parts as Cody and Waite do, and [`FastTrigReduce.v`](theories/FastTrigReduce.v) shows that |n| < 2^27, |ρ| <= u/2 (1 + 2^-24) and that the double-double `rh + rl` that it returns is within 2^-90 of ρ (`fast_reduce_correct`). For a finite x with |x| >= 2^16, `fast_reduce_big` writes x as N u + ρ for an integer N and returns only n = N mod 8192, in [0, 8191], as 8192 u = 2π: as Payne and Hanek do, it writes |x| as m 2^e for an integer m < 2^53, multiplies m by the 192 bits of 1/(2π) that start at bit e + 64 from the top of `INV2PI`, 20 limbs of 64 bits, in three products of two 64-bit integers, splits the product into n and a fraction F within 2^-126 of ρ/u, rounds F down to a multiple of 2^-106 as two `f64`s `fh + fl`, negates n modulo 8192, `fh` and `fl` for x < 0, and multiplies `fh + fl` by u as `U1 + U2` and `U3`. [`FastTrigBigInt.v`](theories/FastTrigBigInt.v) shows that `INV2PI`, read as one integer T, satisfies T <= 2^1215 / π <= T + 1 (`inv2pi_bounds`, from `PI_bounds` by `km_t_bounds_gen` of `KahanMcDonald.v` at N = 1215 and `vm_compute`), and that the window, the products and the shifts give n and F, and [`FastTrigBig.v`](theories/FastTrigBig.v), from those, [`FastTrigBigMul.v`](theories/FastTrigBigMul.v), [`FastTrigBigDec.v`](theories/FastTrigBigDec.v) and [`FastTrigBigSum.v`](theories/FastTrigBigSum.v), that there is an N with N mod 8192 = n for which ρ = x - N u, `rh` and `rl` meet the bounds of `fast_reduce_correct`, with 2^-65 <= |`rh`| (`fast_reduce_big_correct`). It declines x where n mod 2048 = 0, which `fast_reduce_band_big` takes (below), and where |`rh`| < 2^-65. With n = 2048 q + i and 0 <= i < 2048, x is q π/2 + i u + ρ, up to a multiple of 2π above 2^16, so that sin x and cos x are ±sin(i u + ρ) or ±cos(i u + ρ) = ±sin((2048 - i) u - ρ) by q mod 4. Where i = 0, other than at n = 0 below 2^16, x is within u/2 (1 + 2^-24) of a multiple of π/2 other than 0, where sin x, cos x or tan x is close to 0 or has a pole, and ρ can be as small as 2^-61 (`km_distance` below), so that the error of 2^-90 of `fast_reduce_correct` is only 2^-29 of ρ. The fast path takes all of these x, with the reductions `fast_band_reduce`, below 2^16, and `fast_reduce_band_big`, above, which give ρ to a relative error (below). It declines x where i is not 0 and `rh` is 0, the x with |x| >= 2^16 and |`rh`| < 2^-65, which `fast_reduce_big` declines and `fast_reduce_band_big` too, as n mod 2048 is not 0 there, and NaN and the infinities. [`FastTrigTables.v`](theories/FastTrigTables.v) shows that the table `SC` holds sin and cos of i π/4096 for i in [0, 1024] in double-double within 2^-104, with each low part at most 2^-53 times its high part (`sc_correct`, by evaluation in Rocq's virtual machine (`vm_cast_no_check`) in fixed point with 160 fractional bits, with π from `PI_bounds` of `DyConstPi.v` and sin and cos between partial sums of their series, `trig_bounds_even` of [`TrigSeries.v`](theories/TrigSeries.v)), and bounds the constants (`fast_trig_consts`, in fixed point with 200 fractional bits). `fast_sc_correct` in [`FastTrigSC.v`](theories/FastTrigSC.v) shows that `fast_sc(j)`, which returns the row of `SC` at j for j <= 1024 and above it the row at 2048 - j with its sine and cosine swapped, as 2048 u = π/2, by reading the row at j up to 1024 and at 2048 - j above it and picking its halves by an index, 2 above 1024 and 0 up to it, is sin(j u) and cos(j u) within 2^-88 of them, relative, for j in [1, 2047], and `fast_eval_correct` in [`FastTrigEval.v`](theories/FastTrigEval.v) that `fast_eval` computes sin(j u + ρ) = sin(j u) cos ρ + cos(j u) sin ρ, with the Taylor polynomials of cos ρ - 1 to degree 4 and of sin ρ - ρ to degree 5, within 2^-71 of it, relative, with the round-off of these two polynomials, `cm` and `s3`, bounded by Gappa (`poly_cm_gappa` and `poly_s3_gappa` of [`FastTrigGappa.v`](theories/FastTrigGappa.v), from `cm_gappa` and `s3_gappa`), and `fast_sin0_correct` and `fast_cos0_correct` the same of the Taylor polynomials of sin x and cos x at n = 0, for |x| at least 2^-27. `fast_div_correct` in [`FastTrigDiv.v`](theories/FastTrigDiv.v) bounds the relative error of the quotient that `fast_tan` takes by 3 2^-71, and `fast_sin_correct`, `fast_cos_correct` and `fast_tan_correct` in [`FastTrigCore.v`](theories/FastTrigCore.v) combine these into relative bounds of 2^-71, 2^-71 and 3 2^-71. `fast_round_dir_correct` and `fast_round_near_correct` in [`FastTrigRound.v`](theories/FastTrigRound.v) show, from `round_dir_signed_correct` above and `round_near_signed_correct`, that the tests return a rounding of every real `y`, of either sign, of which `h + l` is within 3 2^-71 |y|, for 2^-1000 <= |`h`| <= 2^1000, which `fast_sin_correct` and the others show of the `h` of the fast path, and `fast_sin_dir_correct` and the others in [`FastTrig.v`](theories/FastTrig.v) combine the two at the arguments of magnitude at least 2^-27, which include all that the rules leave. As for e^x, `fast_reduce_big`, `fast_reduce_band_big`, `fast_band_reduce`, `fast_eval` and `fast_div` compute the errors of their exact products with `two_prod_err::<HAS_FMA>`, the theorems hold for either value of `fma`, and `fast_sin_dir_fma` and the others show that both give the same result, so that `DyadicTrig.sin_dir_with` and the others pass `false`:

```coq
Theorem fast_sin_correct :
  forall x h l,
  is_finite x = true -> bpow radix2 (-27) <= Rabs (B2R x) ->
  DyadicTrig.fast_sin false x = Some (h, l) ->
  is_finite h = true /\ is_finite l = true /\ B2R h = RN (B2R h + B2R l) /\
  bpow radix2 (-1000) <= Rabs (B2R h) <= bpow radix2 1000 /\
  Rabs (sin (B2R x) - (B2R h + B2R l)) <= bpow radix2 (-71) * Rabs (sin (B2R x)).

Theorem fast_tan_correct :
  forall x h l,
  is_finite x = true -> bpow radix2 (-27) <= Rabs (B2R x) ->
  DyadicTrig.fast_tan false x = Some (h, l) ->
  is_finite h = true /\ is_finite l = true /\ B2R h = RN (B2R h + B2R l) /\
  bpow radix2 (-1000) <= Rabs (B2R h) <= bpow radix2 1000 /\
  Rabs (tan (B2R x) - (B2R h + B2R l)) <= 3 * bpow radix2 (-71) * Rabs (tan (B2R x)).

Theorem fast_round_dir_correct :
  forall h l up v y,
  is_finite h = true -> is_finite l = true -> B2R h = RN (B2R h + B2R l) ->
  bpow radix2 (-1000) <= Rabs (B2R h) <= bpow radix2 1000 ->
  Rabs (y - (B2R h + B2R l)) <= 3 * bpow radix2 (-71) * Rabs y ->
  DyadicTrig.fast_round_dir h l up = Some v ->
  rounds up y v.

Theorem fast_sin_dir_correct :
  forall fma x up v,
  is_finite x = true -> bpow radix2 (-27) <= Rabs (B2R x) ->
  DyadicTrig.fast_sin_dir fma x up = Some v ->
  rounds up (sin (B2R x)) v.

Theorem fast_sin_dir_fma :
  forall x up, bpow radix2 (-27) <= Rabs (B2R x) ->
  DyadicTrig.fast_sin_dir true x up = DyadicTrig.fast_sin_dir false x up.
```

Near the multiples of π/2 other than 0, where i = 0, other than at n = 0 below 2^16, `fast_quad` and `fast_tan` pass n, `rh` and `rl` of `fast_reduce` to `fast_band_reduce` below 2^16, through `fast_band` and `fast_tan_band`, and above 2^16, where `fast_reduce_any` returns `None`, `fast_sin`, `fast_cos` and `fast_tan` call `fast_reduce_band_big`, through `fast_band_big` and `fast_tan_band_big`. `fast_band_reduce` splits `rh + rl` into `sh` = RN(`rh` + `rl`) and its error, subtracts from that error the error of the product n `U3`, from `two_prod_err::<HAS_FMA>`, and n `U4` rounded, for a fourth part `U4` of u, to give `sl`, and returns `sh`, `sl` and `sr` = RN(`sh` + `sl`). [`FastTrigBandRed.v`](theories/FastTrigBandRed.v) shows that `U4` is finite, with |`U4`| <= 2^-120, and that `U1 + U2 + U3 + U4` is within 2^-174 of π/4096 (`band_consts`), that where `fast_reduce` returns an n with n mod 2048 = 0 other than 0, ρ = x - n u lies in [2^-61, u/2 (1 + 2^-24)] in magnitude, `sh + sl` is within 2^-74 |ρ| of ρ and |`sl`| <= 2^-24 |`sh`| (`fast_band_reduce_correct`), and that `fast_band_reduce` returns the same with and without an FMA at the n, `rh` and `rl` of every x at which `fast_reduce` returns (`fast_band_reduce_fma`). `fast_reduce_band_big` takes the steps of `fast_reduce_big` as far as n and returns `None` where n mod 2048 is not 0. It then reads the fraction to 128 bits, as an integer A with |x|/u - N in [A 2^-128, (A + 5) 2^-128) for an integer N with N mod 8192 = n, with 13 bits from the lowest limb of the product, which `fast_reduce_big` does not read (`band_int` of [`FastTrigBandInt.v`](theories/FastTrigBandInt.v)), shifts A to the left past its leading bits that only repeat its sign, by `leading_zeros`, and takes the top 106 bits of the result as `fh + fl` (`band_norm`), so that these hold the leading bits of the fraction, not a fixed 106 bits after the point. [`FastTrigBandBig.v`](theories/FastTrigBandBig.v) shows, from these and the products of [`FastTrigBandMul.v`](theories/FastTrigBandMul.v), that it returns only for a finite x with |x| >= 2^16 and an n in [0, 8191] with n mod 2048 = 0, and that there is then an N with N mod 8192 = n for which ρ = x - N u, `rh` and `rl` meet the same bounds (`fast_reduce_band_big_correct`), and that it returns the same with and without an FMA at every x (`fast_reduce_band_big_fma`). `fast_band` and `fast_band_big` pass q = (n >> 11) + s, with s = 0 for sin and 1 for cos, as `fast_quad` takes it, to `fast_band_eval`, which returns ±sin ρ by `fast_band_sin`, `rh` plus `rl` and the Taylor polynomial of sin ρ - ρ to degree 5, where q is even, and ±cos ρ by `fast_cos0` at `rr` where q is odd, negated where q & 2 is not 0. [`FastTrigBandEval.v`](theories/FastTrigBandEval.v) shows that for finite `rh`, `rl` and `rr`, a ρ with |ρ| in [2^-61, u/2 (1 + 2^-24)], `rh + rl` within 2^-74 |ρ| of ρ, |`rl`| <= 2^-24 |`rh`| and `rr` = RN(`rh` + `rl`), the bounds that the two reductions give, `fast_band_sin` and `fast_cos0` give sin ρ and cos ρ within 2^-71 of them, relative (`band_sin_correct` and `band_cos_correct`, which, unlike `fast_cos0_correct`, covers |ρ| below 2^-27), and `fast_band_eval` its ±sin ρ or ±cos ρ within 2^-71 of it, relative, with `h` = RN(`h` + `l`) and 2^-1000 <= |`h`| <= 2^1000 (`band_eval_correct`). `fast_band_tan`, which `fast_tan_band` and `fast_tan_band_big` call, divides the sine of `fast_band_sin` by the cosine of `fast_cos0` with `fast_div`, or, where n & 2048 is not 0, the cosine by the sine, and negates the quotient. `band_div_correct` in [`FastTrigBandDiv.v`](theories/FastTrigBandDiv.v) bounds the relative error of `fast_div` by 3 2^-71 for a numerator and a denominator of magnitudes in [2^-64, 1], each within 2^-71 of the double-double that `fast_div` takes, relative, and `band_div_fma` shows that `fast_div` returns the same with and without an FMA where |`nh`| and |`dh`| lie in [2^-65, 2], so that `fast_band_tan` gives tan ρ or -cos ρ / sin ρ within 3 2^-71 of it, relative (`band_tan_correct`), and the same with and without an FMA under the same premises (`band_tan_fma`). The proofs of `fast_sin_correct`, `fast_cos_correct` and `fast_tan_correct` above take these x from these theorems. Where |ρ| is below about 2^-34, cos ρ is closer to 1 than the error bound of the rounding test, which then cannot round ±cos ρ in a direction, and `fast_sin_dir` and `fast_cos_dir` call `fast_one_dir(h, up)` where `fast_round_dir` fails, which returns `None` unless |`h`| = 1, and otherwise ±(1 - 2^-53) or ±1, with the sign of `h`, by `up`. [`FastTrigOne.v`](theories/FastTrigOne.v) shows that sin x is never ±1 at a finite x (`sin_not_one`), nor cos t at a finite t other than 0 (`cos_not_one`), as either would put x or t on a multiple of π/2 other than 0, which `km_distance` rules out, and that the `v` that `fast_one_dir` returns is a rounding, toward +∞ if `up` and toward -∞ otherwise, of every real y with |y| < 1 of which `h + l` is within 2^-71 |y|, with `h` and `l` finite and `h` = RN(`h` + `l`) (`fast_one_dir_correct`), as y then lies strictly between ±(1 - 2^-53) and ±1. `fast_sin_dir_correct` and `fast_cos_dir_correct` use these three, and `fast_tan_dir` has no such rule. The statements for the two reductions, for `fast_band_eval` and `fast_band_tan`, and for `fast_one_dir` are:

```coq
Theorem fast_band_reduce_correct :
  forall x n rh rl rr,
  DyadicTrig.fast_reduce x = Some (n, rh, rl, rr) -> Z.land n 2047 = 0%Z -> n <> 0%Z ->
  let '(sh, sl, sr) := DyadicTrig.fast_band_reduce false n rh rl in
  is_finite sh = true /\ is_finite sl = true /\ is_finite sr = true /\ sr = add sh sl /\
  bpow radix2 (-61) <= Rabs (B2R x - IZR n * (PI / 4096)) <=
    PI / 8192 * (1 + bpow radix2 (-24)) /\
  Rabs (B2R x - IZR n * (PI / 4096) - (B2R sh + B2R sl)) <=
    bpow radix2 (-74) * Rabs (B2R x - IZR n * (PI / 4096)) /\
  Rabs (B2R sl) <= bpow radix2 (-24) * Rabs (B2R sh).

Theorem fast_reduce_band_big_correct :
  forall x n rh rl rr,
  DyadicTrig.fast_reduce_band_big false x = Some (n, rh, rl, rr) ->
  is_finite x = true /\ 65536 <= Rabs (B2R x) /\ (0 <= n < 8192)%Z /\ Z.land n 2047 = 0%Z /\
  is_finite rh = true /\ is_finite rl = true /\ is_finite rr = true /\ rr = add rh rl /\
  exists N, (N mod 8192 = n)%Z /\
  bpow radix2 (-61) <= Rabs (B2R x - IZR N * (PI / 4096)) <=
    PI / 8192 * (1 + bpow radix2 (-24)) /\
  Rabs (B2R x - IZR N * (PI / 4096) - (B2R rh + B2R rl)) <=
    bpow radix2 (-74) * Rabs (B2R x - IZR N * (PI / 4096)) /\
  Rabs (B2R rl) <= bpow radix2 (-24) * Rabs (B2R rh).

Theorem band_eval_correct :
  forall q (rh rl rr : f64) rho,
  is_finite rh = true -> is_finite rl = true -> is_finite rr = true ->
  B2R rr = RN (B2R rh + B2R rl) ->
  bpow radix2 (-61) <= Rabs rho <= PI / 8192 * (1 + bpow radix2 (-24)) ->
  Rabs (rho - (B2R rh + B2R rl)) <= bpow radix2 (-74) * Rabs rho ->
  Rabs (B2R rl) <= bpow radix2 (-24) * Rabs (B2R rh) ->
  let w := if (Z.land q 1 =? 0)%Z then (if (Z.land q 2 =? 0)%Z then sin rho else - sin rho)
           else (if (Z.land q 2 =? 0)%Z then cos rho else - cos rho) in
  let '(h, l) := DyadicTrig.fast_band_eval q rh rl rr in
  is_finite h = true /\ is_finite l = true /\ B2R h = RN (B2R h + B2R l) /\
  bpow radix2 (-1000) <= Rabs (B2R h) <= bpow radix2 1000 /\
  Rabs (w - (B2R h + B2R l)) <= bpow radix2 (-71) * Rabs w.

Theorem band_tan_correct :
  forall n (rh rl rr : f64) rho,
  is_finite rh = true -> is_finite rl = true -> is_finite rr = true ->
  B2R rr = RN (B2R rh + B2R rl) ->
  bpow radix2 (-61) <= Rabs rho <= PI / 8192 * (1 + bpow radix2 (-24)) ->
  Rabs (rho - (B2R rh + B2R rl)) <= bpow radix2 (-74) * Rabs rho ->
  Rabs (B2R rl) <= bpow radix2 (-24) * Rabs (B2R rh) ->
  let w := if (Z.land n 2048 =? 0)%Z then sin rho / cos rho else - (cos rho / sin rho) in
  let '(h, l) := DyadicTrig.fast_band_tan false n rh rl rr in
  is_finite h = true /\ is_finite l = true /\ B2R h = RN (B2R h + B2R l) /\
  bpow radix2 (-1000) <= Rabs (B2R h) <= bpow radix2 1000 /\
  Rabs (w - (B2R h + B2R l)) <= 3 * bpow radix2 (-71) * Rabs w.

Theorem fast_one_dir_correct :
  forall (h l : f64) up v y,
  is_finite h = true -> is_finite l = true -> B2R h = RN (B2R h + B2R l) ->
  Rabs (y - (B2R h + B2R l)) <= bpow radix2 (-71) * Rabs y -> Rabs y < 1 ->
  DyadicTrig.fast_one_dir h up = Some v -> rounds up y v.
```

`k_sin_c`, `k_cos_c` and `k_tan_c` in `DyPoint.v` show that `k_sin`, `k_cos` and `k_tan`, the kernel's three functions as fields of a record `point`, satisfy `sin_c`, `cos_c` and `tan_c` of `Contract.v`, with `kernel_field_correct_dom` for `tan`, whose domain the proof does not use, so that `kernel_sin_correct` to `kernel_tan_correct` give `sin_correct`, `cos_correct` and `tan_correct` for a record `point` whose fields are these. Each of them uses both `k_sin_c` and `k_cos_c`, as `Interval::sin`, `Interval::cos` and `Interval::tan` call both functions through `quadrant`, and `kernel_tan_correct` also `k_tan_c`:

```coq
Theorem k_sin_c : sin_c k_sin.

Theorem kernel_sin_correct :
  forall has_fma first x, valid x ->
  exists y, Interval.sin kernel_point has_fma first x = Some y /\
    is_tightest (image_dom real_dom sin x) y.
```

The tests in `src/dyadic/trig/tests.rs` take these three functions, in both directions, at the special values, at each rule's threshold and the arguments next to it, at `NO_REDUCE` and π/4, between which `reduce` takes `k = 0`, at arguments whose results are close to an `f64`, at short dyadics from 2^-26 to 2^-12, `m 2^-26` for integers `m` up to 2^12 among them, at which cos is close to an `f64`, at `3 j 2^-e` from 2^-27 to 2^-12 for odd `j` up to 255, at some of which sin and tan are, at the 255 arguments on each side of `n 2^-26` for `n` up to 8, at arguments next to such centres in each binade of the ranges of the rules of small arguments, which a search found, among them some at which these rules leave the argument to `ziv`, at 6381956970095103 × 2^797, the case of Kahan and McDonald, which is close to a multiple of π/2, and the arguments next to it, at the hardest arguments that four searches found, and at random arguments, of any exponent, uniform in [-10, 10) and log-uniform from 2^60 up, and, on 64-bit Linux and macOS, at the `f64`s nearest to multiples of π/2 at every exponent from 0 to 1023 and the arguments next to them. They check that the two directions give the same `f64` or two adjacent ones, or NaN both, and, on 64-bit Linux and macOS, that they give those of `src/mpfr_calls/mpfr.rs`, which call MPFR, bit for bit. Others there check that the three functions give the same `f64`s with the fast path as without it, and, on 64-bit Linux and macOS, as MPFR, at random arguments of its range, log-uniform from 2^16 up among them, at the edges of its two reductions, every limb and every shift of the window of `INV2PI` among them, at arguments where its rounding tests pass but would fail with a bound 2^10 times as large, and, on 64-bit Linux and macOS, next to the steps of the reduction of `fast_reduce_big`, where x / u is close to an integer or to a midpoint between two, and that its tables, its constants and the bounds of `fast_reduce`, `fast_sin`, `fast_cos` and `fast_tan` hold against MPFR at 300 bits, that `INV2PI` and the bounds of `fast_reduce_big` hold against MPFR at 1,400 bits, and that its directed rounding tests fail at the hard cases. Others there check, in the same way, the band, where x is within u/2 (1 + 2^-24) of a multiple of π/2 other than 0: `near_multiples` takes sin, cos and tan at the `f64` nearest to k π/2, the two `f64`s on each side of it and the negations of all, for every k from 1 to 41,721, the last k with k π/2 below 2^16, and checks that the kernel computes fewer than 1 in 10,000 of the results of each function and that `fast_one_dir` gives every result ±cos ρ in a direction, which the rounding test leaves, and `near_one_edge` takes sin at an odd k and cos at an even k, for k from 1 to 16 and at 255, 256, 599 and 600, at that `f64` ± 2^-e and the negations, for e from 25 to 40, and checks that in a direction the rounding test gives the results for e from 27 to 33, `fast_one_dir` those for e from 34 to 40 and none for e = 25 and 26, and that to nearest the rounding test gives those for e from 27 to 40. Those in `src/dyadic/trig/tests/band.rs` take the three functions at random arguments in the band above 2^16 (`band_big_random`), at ±6381956970095103 × 2^797 (`km_argument`) and, on 64-bit Linux and macOS, at the ends of the band below and above 2^16 and the `f64`s next to them (`mpfr::band_edges`), all of which the fast paths take before any rounding test. On 64-bit Linux and macOS, `mpfr::band_reductions` checks the bounds of `fast_band_reduce_correct` at the arguments of `near_multiples`, and `mpfr::band_edges` at its own below 2^16. The tests of `band.rs` check those of `fast_reduce_band_big_correct` at their arguments above 2^16, the bounds that need π only on 64-bit Linux and macOS, where `mpfr::km_distance` also checks that 2^-61 <= |ρ| < 2^-60 at ±6381956970095103 × 2^797, so that the 2^-61 of `km_distance` is within a factor of 2 of |ρ| there. Those in `src/dyadic/trig/tests/mpfr_cases.rs` take the arguments of MPFR's own tests of these functions, with MPFR's results at 53 bits where those tests give them, and those in `src/dyadic/trig/tests/levels.rs` force each level from 1 to 64 limbs at a few arguments, near multiples of π/2 and huge ones among them, as for the exponentials above.

`km_distance` in [`KahanMcDonald.v`](theories/KahanMcDonald.v) shows that no finite `f64` lies within 2^-61 of a multiple of π/2 other than 0. The least distance is about 2^-60.9, at 6381956970095103 × 2^797, the case of Kahan and McDonald (J.-M. Muller, Elementary Functions, the chapter on range reduction), which is cited here, not proved: the proof shows only the bound 2^-61. For `|x| < 1` the distance is above π/2 - 1. Otherwise `x = m 2^e` with `2^52 <= m < 2^53` and `-52 <= e <= 971`, and `|x - k π/2| = (π/2) |m α - k|` with `α = 2^(e+1)/π`. For each `e`, `vm_compute` brackets the fractional part of `α` by two integers over a power of 2, from the bounds of π of `PI_bounds`, runs Euclid's algorithm on the bracket for two pairs `(q, p)` and `(q', p')` with `|q p' - p q'| = 1`, `q' >= 2^53` and residues `q α - p` and `q' α - p'` of opposite signs, and checks that `|q α - p|` is large enough at both ends of the bracket (`km_all_true`). Every `0 < m < q'` and integer `j` then have `|m α - j| >= |q α - p|` (`km_unimodular`), so that the proof needs no theory of continued fractions. With it, `reduce_pos` and `reduce_le` in [`DyTrigSign.v`](theories/DyTrigSign.v) show that the enclosure of ρ that `reduce` returns at a level lies in `[0, 4/5]`, and above 0 for `t > 0`: where `reduce` keeps a quotient `k >= 1`, `ρ = t - k π/2` is at least 2^-61, and the error of the reduction and the roundings at `n` limbs are far smaller. `series_pos` and `series_sin_lt1` in [`DyTrigSeriesPos.v`](theories/DyTrigSeriesPos.v) show that `series` then returns enclosures of sin ρ and cos ρ above 0, and one of sin ρ below 1, as each step of Horner's scheme keeps the enclosure in `[0, 1]`, and above 0 after the first. So `ev_sin_sign` and `ev_cos_sign` in [`DyTrigPos.v`](theories/DyTrigPos.v) show that the enclosures of sin x, for `x` other than 0, and of cos x exclude 0 at every level at which they return, and `ziv_sign` in [`DyTrigZivSign.v`](theories/DyTrigZivSign.v) that `ziv` then returns, rounding down, a value below 0 only where the result is below 0, and rounding up, one above 0 only where the result is above 0, whether a test passes or not, as it returns a bound of one of these enclosures rounded. With the rules and the fast path, which return correct roundings, and `sin_dir_side` and `cos_dir_side` for the other direction, `sin_dir_sign` and `cos_dir_sign` show that sin x rounded toward -∞ is below 0 exactly where sin x is, and cos x rounded toward +∞ is above 0 exactly where cos x is, at every finite `x` at which they return, the signs that `quadrant` of `src/elementary.rs` reads. `tan_div_pos` shows that the enclosure by which `tan` divides lies above 0 wherever the operations that compute it return, so that `Encl::div` does not panic there for a divisor that contains 0 (`encl_div_zero`):

```coq
Theorem km_distance :
  forall (x : f64) (k : Z), is_finite x = true -> k <> 0%Z ->
  bpow radix2 (-61) <= Rabs (B2R x - IZR k * (PI / 2)).

Theorem sin_dir_sign :
  forall x v,
  is_finite x = true -> DyadicTrig.sin_dir x false = Some v ->
  (Model.lt v Model.zero = true <-> sin (B2R x) < 0)%R.

Theorem cos_dir_sign :
  forall x v,
  is_finite x = true -> DyadicTrig.cos_dir x true = Some v ->
  (Model.lt Model.zero v = true <-> 0 < cos (B2R x))%R.

Theorem tan_div_pos :
  forall x n quad neg r one_e s a b p c,
  level_ok n -> is_finite x = true ->
  DyadicTrig.reduce n (fabs x) = Some (quad, neg, r) ->
  Dyadic.encl_point n DyadicExp.one = Some one_e ->
  DyadicTrig.series n r true = Some s ->
  Dyadic.encl_sub n one_e s = Some a -> Dyadic.encl_add n one_e s = Some b ->
  Dyadic.encl_mul n a b = Some p -> Dyadic.encl_sqrt n p = Some c ->
  (quad mod 2 = 1 -> (0 < lo_R s)%R) /\ (0 < lo_R c)%R.
```

`pow_dir_correct` in [`DyPow.v`](theories/DyPow.v) shows that `pow(x, y, up)` of `src/dyadic/pow.rs`, which computes x^y with a fast path in double-double and with the kernel, returns, at finite `x` and `y` in `pow_dom`, that is, with `x > 0`, or `x = ±0` and `y > 0`, and wherever it returns by a rule, by `exact`, by the fast path or by a rounding test of `ziv` that passes, `Rpow x y` rounded toward +∞ if `up` and toward -∞ otherwise, as `mpfr_round` says, and `pow_dir_side` that it returns there, wherever it returns rather than panics, a value on the side of `Rpow x y` that the rounding is on, as for e^x above. `pown_dir_correct` and `pown_dir_side` in [`DyPown.v`](theories/DyPown.v) state the same of `pown(x, n, up)`, with `powerRZ x n` in place of `Rpow x y`, at a finite `x` and an `n` with `is_i32 n` and `pown_dom n x`, that is, with `n >= 0` or `x` other than 0. `DyadicPow.pow_dir x y up` and `DyadicPow.pown_dir x n up` in `Model.v` are `pow(x, y, up)` and `pown(x, n, up)`, with `None` where the Rust panics, and `pow_dir_with`, `pown_dir_with`, `pow_dir_strict`, `pown_dir_strict`, `pow_dir_post` and `pown_dir_post` are as for e^x, except that `pown_dir_post` also asks of `post` that it carry over from `-r` in one direction to `r` in the other for an odd `n` (`odd_post`). Each function first settles some arguments by rules. `pow` returns 1 at `y = ±0`, NaN `x` included, and at `x = 1`, NaN `y` included, NaN at the other arguments with a NaN or with `x < 0`, -∞ included, of which `Contract.v` asks nothing, and, at `y = ±∞`, +∞ for `x > 1` and `y = +∞` and for `x < 1` and `y = -∞`, and +0 otherwise; at `x = ±0` it returns +∞ for `y < 0` and +0 for `y > 0`, with the sign of `x` for an odd integer `y`, and at `x = +∞`, +∞ for `y > 0` and +0 for `y < 0`. `pow_dir_zero_neg`, `pow_dir_one_inf`, `pow_dir_lt1_inf`, `pow_dir_gt1_inf`, `pow_dir_inf_neg` and `pow_dir_inf_pos` give these values at +0 with a finite `y < 0`, at a finite `x > 0` with `y = ±∞`, and at +∞ with a finite `y` other than 0. At a finite `x > 0` other than 1 and a finite `y` other than 0, `pow` computes from the bits of `x` and `y` an integer `s` with `2^s <= |y ln x| < 2^(s+4)`, and the sign of `y ln x` (`pow_rule_bounds` in [`DyPowRules.v`](theories/DyPowRules.v)): `s` is the exponent of `y` plus an integer `l` with `2^l <= |ln x| < 2^(l+3)` (`ln_expo_spec`), which `ln_expo` computes from the exponent of `x - 1` for `x` in `[1/2, 2)` and from that of `x` otherwise. For `s >= 10`, `|y ln x| >= 1024`, and the result overflows or is below 2^-1075 in magnitude (`pow_rule_overflow` and `pow_rule_underflow`), and for `s <= -58`, `|y ln x| < 2^-54`, and the result is within an ulp of 1 (`pow_rule_near_one`). Each of these lemmas shows that its rule returns the rounding in its direction, and `pow_rule_not_exact` that no finite `f64` is x^y at the arguments of these rules. As for the exponentials above, Ziv's strategy could not settle an argument whose result is an `f64` unless an enclosure has that `f64` as its bound in the direction of the rounding, and `pow` next calls `exact`, which computes such results in integers, and the fast path below: `exact` first where `maybe_exact` says that x^y may be an `f64`, and after the fast path otherwise, a choice that no proof unfolds, as `pow_dir_post` holds in both orders. It writes `x = m 2^e` with an odd `m` (`odd_parts_spec`) and `|y| = p / 2^k` with `k >= 0` and `p` odd if `k > 0`, leaves the argument to `ziv` if `y` is a multiple of 2^12, at which no finite `f64` is x^y (`exact_ey_small`), takes `k` integer square roots of `m`, each checked by squaring, halving `e` each time (`roots_loop_sound` and `roots_loop_complete`), so that `x = r^(2^k) 2^(e' 2^k)`, sets `q = 1` for `r = 1` and, for `y > 0`, `q = r^p` where it is below 2^53 (`small_power_spec`), and returns `q 2^(e' p)` for `y > 0` and `2^(-e' p)` for `y < 0` where it is in the range of the `f64`s. `exact_sound` in [`DyPowExact.v`](theories/DyPowExact.v) shows that an `f64` that `exact` returns is finite and is x^y, and `exact_complete` that `exact` returns `v` at every finite `x > 0` other than 1 and finite `y` other than 0 at which x^y is a finite `f64` `v`. Both rest on [`DyPowArith.v`](theories/DyPowArith.v), in integers and reals and with no kernel: `pow_dyadic_sound` shows that `(r^(2^k) 2^(e 2^k))^(p / 2^k) = r^p 2^(e p)`, and `pow_dyadic_complete` that `(m 2^e)^(±p / 2^k) = m' 2^e'`, for odd `m, m' > 0`, `p > 0` and `k >= 0`, with `p` odd if `k > 0`, gives an `r > 0` and an `e_r` with `m = r^(2^k)` and `e = e_r 2^k` and `m' = r^p`, and with `e' = e_r p` for `+p`, or `r = 1` and `e' = -e_r p` for `-p`. For `+p`, raising both sides to the power `2^k` and comparing the odd parts and the powers of 2 gives `m^p = m'^(2^k)` and `e p = e' 2^k` (`pow_dyadic_eq`), then Gauss's lemma (`Gauss` of Stdlib's `Znumtheory`) that `2^k` divides `e`, and `odd_pow_root` that `m` is a `2^k`-th power, which rests on `square_of_mul_square`: `n q^2 = r^2`, for `q, r > 0`, makes `n` a square, by Gauss's lemma again. For `-p`, `pow_dyadic_neg` gives `m = 1`. Of the arguments that `exact` and the fast path leave, those with an integer `y` from -2^31 to 2^31 - 1, at which `eq (of_i64 (to_i32 y)) y`, go to `pown_pos_ziv`, which is `pown_pos` below without the fast path that `pow` has already tried, at `k = to_i32 y`: there `is_i32 k` (`to_i32_range` in `DyPow.v`), so that `of_i64 k` is the `f64` `k` (`of_i64_exact` of `DyPowCore.v`) and `y = k`, `k` is not 0, and x^y is `powerRZ x k` for `x > 0` (`powerRZ_Rpower` of Stdlib), so that `pown_pos_ziv_post` in `DyPown.v`, which shows of `pown_pos_ziv` what `pown_pos_post` below shows of `pown_pos`, from its rules and its driver, gives `pow_dir_post` there. The fast path returns, where it returns, the rounding in its direction (`fast_pow_dir_correct` below), as |y ln x| >= 2^-57 there by the rules, and the others go to the driver, from level 1 or 2 by a threshold on the exponent of `y` and on `s` that no proof depends on, with the enclosures that `ev_pow_correct` in [`DyPowCore.v`](theories/DyPowCore.v) shows hold x^y at each level. `ev_pow` writes `ln x = e ln 2 ± a` with the `e` and the enclosure `a` of `reduce` (`reduce_correct` of `DyLogCore.v`), takes an integer `k` near `y log2 x` from an `f64` estimate, and returns `2^k e^-w` for `w = (k - y e) ln 2 ∓ y a` by `scaled`, as e^x does (`scaled_gen` of `DyExp.v`): both signs give `w = k ln 2 - y ln x`, and so `2^k e^-w = x^y` (`pow_w_value`). For the enclosure, `k` need not be near `y log2 x`: it needs only `|k| <= 2^14`, which `to_k` checks (`pow_to_k_bound`). `pown` returns 1 at `n = 0`, NaN `x` included, NaN at the other NaN arguments, and, at ±0 and ±∞, +∞ at ±0 for `n < 0` and at ±∞ for `n > 0`, and +0 otherwise, with the sign of `x` for an odd `n` (`pown_dir_zero_pos`, `pown_dir_zero_neg` and `pown_dir_inf`).
