- [`TwoSum.v`](theories/TwoSum.v): `two_sum_err_correct`. If `|x|, |y| < 2^1023` and `x + y` is finite, `two_sum_err` computes the exact error `(x + y) - s` without overflowing. It uses Flocq's proof of TwoSum (`Pff2Flocq.TwoSum_correct`), which allows underflow.
- [`StepUp.v`](theories/StepUp.v): `step_up_rounds_up`. If `p = RN(z)` is nonzero and `e = z - p` exactly, then `if e > 0 { step_up(p) } else { p }` is `z` rounded toward +∞, including the sign of the result and overflow. `mul1_ru_fast_correct` relies on this. `step_up_rounds_up_iff` is the same with any `up` that is true exactly when `p < z` in place of `e > 0`. `step_up_if_false` and `step_up_if_eq`: `step_up_if(x, up)`, with which `mul1_ru_fast` rounds without an FMA, is `x` if `up` is false, whatever `x` is, and `if up { step_up(x) } else { x }` if `x` is finite and nonzero.
- [`TwoProduct.v`](theories/TwoProduct.v): `two_prod_err_correct`. If `two_prod_is_exact` holds for `p = x * y`, then `p` is finite and nonzero, and `two_prod_err` computes the exact error `x * y - p` without overflowing, with or without an FMA. It uses Flocq's `mult_error_FLT` for the FMA, and Boldo's proofs of Veltkamp's `split` and Dekker's TwoProduct (`Pff2Flocq.Veltkamp`, `Pff2Flocq.Dekker`) without one. They hold with subnormals, but `mult_error_FLT` and `Dekker` need `|x * y| >= 2^-969`, which `two_prod_is_exact` ensures by checking `|p| >= 2^-968`.
- [`MulRuTiny.v`](theories/MulRuTiny.v): the steps of `mul1_ru_tiny_correct` that are about real numbers and single floats. `RU_scaled`: rounding `z * 2^128` upward, multiplying it by `2^-128` and rounding the result upward rounds `z` upward, for every real `z`, as Flocq's `round` has no overflow (`mul1_ru_tiny` uses it where nothing overflows). `step_up_rounds_up_iff0` is `step_up_rounds_up_iff` for a nonzero `w` and a `q = RN(w)` that may be zero, provided that a zero `q` has the sign of `w`. `zero_rule_rounds_up`: if `p = RN(z)` is a zero with the sign of a nonzero `z`, then `z` rounded upward is `p` if `z < 0`, and `2^-1074`, whose bit pattern is 1, if `z > 0`. `rounds_up_to_ext` shows that whether a float is `z` rounded upward depends on `z` only through `RU(z)` and the sign of `z`. The other lemmas bound magnitudes and exponents, such as `RN_nonzero_ge` (if `RN(z)` is nonzero, `|z| >= 2^-1075`), give the results of `*` and `Bmult mode_UP` where the exact product is at most `2^1023` in magnitude, or give the values of the constants `2^128` and `2^-128`.
- [`Decompose.v`](theories/Decompose.v): `decompose_correct`, i.e. `decompose` gives the sign, the integral significand and the exponent of a finite nonzero float. `pow2_correct`, i.e. `pow2(e)` is `2^e` for `e ∈ [-1074, 1023]`. `of_u64_exact`, i.e. `k as f64` is exact for `k <= 2^53`. `add_special`: where `add1_ru_slow` returns `x + y`, rounding to nearest gives the same result as toward +∞.
- [`RoundUpExact.v`](theories/RoundUpExact.v): `round_up_exact_correct`. `round_up_exact(neg, m, e, sticky)` rounds `(-1)^neg * (m + δ) * 2^e` toward +∞, under the precondition `round_up_exact_pre` in [`Spec.v`](theories/Spec.v), which bounds `m` and `e` and requires `m >= 2^52` if `sticky`.
- [`SumRoundUp.v`](theories/SumRoundUp.v): `sum_round_up_reduce`. For significands in `(0, 2^106)` and exponents in `[-2^19, 2^19]`, `sum_round_up` returns +0 if the sum is zero, and otherwise calls `round_up_exact` with arguments that meet its precondition.
- [`DivRemainder.v`](theories/DivRemainder.v): `div_remainder`. If `q = RN(x / y)`, `|q| >= 2^-1022` and `|x| >= 2^-967`, the remainder `x - q * y` is a float, and `|x - q * y| <= 2^-52 |x|`. This is a case of [BD03] (see `generic.rs`) for binary64, with subnormals. Flocq proves it only without them (`div_error_FLX`).
- [`DivRuFast.v`](theories/DivRuFast.v): `div_exact`, i.e. the exponent checks in `div1_ru_fast` imply the hypotheses of `div_remainder`. Given its conclusions, `div_fma_remainder` and `div_dekker_remainder` show that `(-q).mul_add(y, x)`, and `(x - p) - two_prod_err(q, y, p)` with `p = q * y`, compute `x - q * y` exactly without overflowing, the latter if `div1_ru_fast`'s checks for Dekker's TwoProduct hold.
- [`DivRuSlow.v`](theories/DivRuSlow.v): `div1_ru_slow_rounds_up`. If `x` and `y` are finite and nonzero, `div1_ru_slow` does not panic and returns `x / y` rounded toward +∞, as it calls `round_up_exact` with arguments that meet its precondition. `div_special`: where `div1_ru_slow` returns `x / y`, the rounding mode does not matter.
- [`SqrtRem.v`](theories/SqrtRem.v): `sqrt_rem_format`. If `x >= 2^-966`, the remainder `x - RN(√x)^2` is a float. This is a case of [BD03] for the square root, with subnormals. Flocq proves it only without them (`sqrt_error_FLX_N`). `sqrt_rem_sign`: the sign of `x - s * s` tells whether `s` is below or above `√x`.
- [`SqrtFast.v`](theories/SqrtFast.v): `sqrt1_fast_rem`. If `sqrt1_fast` accepts a positive `x` and the remainder is a float, then `s = RN(√x)` is positive, and `r` is `x - s * s` exactly, computed without overflowing.
- [`SqrtSlow.v`](theories/SqrtSlow.v): `sqrt1_slow_correct`. If `f` returns `Bsqrt m y` for every `y` that `sqrt1_fast` accepts, `sqrt1_slow x f` returns `Bsqrt m x`, for any rounding mode `m`. `round_scale`: rounding a normal number commutes with a scaling by a power of two that keeps it normal.
- [`AddOdd.v`](theories/AddOdd.v): `add_odd_correct`. If `|x|, |y| < 2^1023` and `RN(x + y)` is finite, `add_odd(x, y)` is `x + y` rounded to odd [BM08] (see `generic.rs`).
- [`EmulatedFma.v`](theories/EmulatedFma.v): `mul_add1_rn_emulated_correct`. Where the fast path of `mul_add_ru` is valid without an FMA and does not return `mul_add1_special(a, b, c)`, `mul_add1_rn_emulated` returns `Bfma mode_NE a b c`, the FMA rounded to nearest, including the sign of a zero. The algorithm is [BM08, Algorithm 1], and the proof follows that of [BM08, Theorem 4], using Flocq's `round_N_odd`. Of the checks in `mul_add1_ru_fast`, the proof uses only `two_prod_is_exact` and the bounds on `exponent(u1)` and `exponent(c)`. The `not_tiny` checks are ErrFma's.
- [`MulAddRuFast.v`](theories/MulAddRuFast.v): `mul_add1_ru_fast_rounds_up`. If `r1 = Bfma mode_NE a b c`, and the fast path is valid and does not return `mul_add1_special(a, b, c)`, its result is `a * b + c` rounded toward +∞. It uses Flocq's proof of ErrFma (`Pff2Flocq.ErrFMA_correct`), whose underflow hypotheses `two_prod_is_exact` and the `not_tiny` checks ensure.
- [`MulAddRuSlow.v`](theories/MulAddRuSlow.v): `mul_add1_ru_slow_correct` above. `mul_add1_special_correct`: where the fast and slow paths return `mul_add1_special(a, b, c)`, rounding to nearest gives the same result as toward +∞.
- [`RoundTiesEven.v`](theories/RoundTiesEven.v): `round_ties_even1_correct`, i.e. `round_ties_even1`, which `round_ties_even` calls on each lane, is Flocq's `Bnearbyint mode_NE`, IEEE 754's roundToIntegralTiesToEven, including the sign of a zero result, the infinities and NaN. For `|x| < 2^52`, `|x| + 2^52` is in `[2^52, 2^53)`, where the ulp is 1, so the addition rounds `|x|` to an integer, ties to even. Subtracting `2^52` is exact, and `copysign` gives the result the sign of `x`, which is right because rounding to nearest, ties to even, is odd.
- [`Nearest.v`](theories/Nearest.v): what `round` and `round_ties_even` share, proved once for any way of breaking a tie. `Rnearest_le` and `Rnearest_near`: rounding to the nearest integer is nondecreasing and moves a real by less than 1. `nearest_strongest`: on an interval with bounds `a` and `b`, the strongest decoration is `com` if both are finite, round to the same integer and neither is halfway between two integers, `dac` if one of them is halfway instead, and `def` otherwise. `is_half_test`: for a finite `a`, `|a| - trunc(|a|) == 0.5` holds exactly when `a` is halfway between two integers.
- [`Classify2.v`](theories/Classify2.v): `classify2_pair`. If `classify` returns `c1` for `x` and `c2` for `y`, `classify2` returns `(c1, c2)`. Every discriminant of an `IntervalClass` is below 16, so the shift by 4 drops no bits, and `transmute_class2` decodes the two nibbles back into the two classes.
- [`Classes.v`](theories/Classes.v): `classify_lanes` and `classify_mem`, what each class says about the lanes of a `valid` interval and about its reals. In `M`, both lanes are above 0, a positive float or +∞ (`pos_lane` in `AddSub.v`). In `N0` and `N1`, the first lane is above 0, and the second is finite and 0 or below 0, and in `P0` and `P1` the other way round. In `Z`, both lanes are finite and 0, and in `E`, both are NaN.
- [`MulLane.v`](theories/MulLane.v): `mul_lane_finite`. `Bmult mode_UP` of two finite lanes is the least float or +∞ at or above their product: the product rounded up if that does not overflow, +∞ if it overflows above, and `-f64::MAX` if it overflows below, as `add_lane` shows for a sum. `mul_lane_pos`: the same for two lanes above 0, where the product is +∞ if either lane is (`umult` in `AddSub.v`). `max_least_lane`: if two lanes are the least floats or +∞ at or above two upper bounds, their `max1` is the least at or above the larger bound.
- [`MulTight.v`](theories/MulTight.v): the largest product of an element of a set of reals `A` and one of a set `B`, with the largest element of a set, or +∞ if there is none, as in `tight` in `AddSub.v`. `tight_Some`: a set has the largest element `m` exactly when `m` is in it and every element is at most `m`. `tight_mult_pos_r` and `tight_mult_pos_l`: if the elements of `B`, or those of `A`, are at least 0, and `A` and `B` each have an element above 0, the largest product is the product of the largest elements, or +∞ if either is. `tight_mult_min`: if the least elements `a` and `c` of `A` and `B` are at least 0, the largest negated product is `-(a * c)`. `tight_mult_mixed`: if `A` and `B` each have elements below and above 0, the largest product is the larger of the product of the largest negations and the product of the largest elements. `image2_mult_opp_l`, `image2_mult_opp_r` and `image2_mult_opp_lr`: the negated products of reals of `x` and `y` are the products with the factor from `x`, or that from `y`, negated, and the products are those with both negated, as `-(r * t) = (-r) * t = r * (-t)` and `r * t = (-r) * (-t)`.
- [`MulZero.v`](theories/MulZero.v): `mul_empty` and `mul_zero` above. `zero_tightest`: `Interval::zero()`, `[+0; +0]`, holds exactly the real 0, as its lower bound is `-(+0)`, and so is the tightest interval that contains `{0}`.
- [`MulMM.v`](theories/MulMM.v): `mul_MM` above.
- [`MulMRow.v`](theories/MulMRow.v): `mul_MN`, `mul_MP`, `mul_NM` and `mul_PM` above. `mul_M_lanes`, `mul_N_lanes` and `mul_P_lanes` collect what the proofs need of each class, so that the tightness arguments treat `N0` and `N1`, and `P0` and `P1`, alike.
- [`MulNP.v`](theories/MulNP.v): `mul_NN`, `mul_NP`, `mul_PN` and `mul_PP` above. `mul_NN_eq`, `mul_NP_eq`, `mul_PN_eq` and `mul_PP_eq` give the lanes that `Interval::mul` returns, as `Bmult mode_UP` of two lanes each. `mul_fin_pos` and `mul_pos_fin` put a lane rounded up from two finite lanes and one from two lanes above 0 together into the tightest interval. A bound that is 0 may be stored as +0 or -0, which need no cases of their own, as both are finite with the value 0.
- [`MulAddLane.v`](theories/MulAddLane.v): `image3_fma`, `tightest_mul_add`, `fma_lane_finite`, `fma_lane_pos` and `uplus_umax` above. If the sum that `fma_lane_finite` rounds up overflows, `Bfma mode_UP` gives +∞ for a sum at or above 0, and `-f64::MAX` for one below 0, as `add_lane` shows for a sum of two lanes.
- [`MulAddZero.v`](theories/MulAddZero.v): `mul_add_addend_empty`, `mul_add_empty`, `mul_add_zero` and `tightest_self` above.
- [`MulAddMM.v`](theories/MulAddMM.v): `mul_add_MM` above.
- [`MulAddMRow.v`](theories/MulAddMRow.v): `mul_add_MN`, `mul_add_MP`, `mul_add_NM` and `mul_add_PM` above. `tightest_mul_add_pos` is `tightest_mul_pos` of `MulMRow.v` with the lanes of `z` added: if the products and the negated products have the largest elements that the products of two pairs of lanes above 0 stand for, `Bfma mode_UP` of those pairs and the lanes of `z` gives the tightest interval.
- [`MulAddNP.v`](theories/MulAddNP.v): `mul_add_NN`, `mul_add_NP`, `mul_add_PN` and `mul_add_PP` above. `mul_add_NN_eq`, `mul_add_NP_eq`, `mul_add_PN_eq` and `mul_add_PP_eq` give the lanes that `Interval::mul_add` returns, as `Bfma mode_UP` of the two lanes that `mul_NN_eq` and the others multiply and a lane of `z`. `mul_add_fin_pos` and `mul_add_pos_fin` are `mul_fin_pos` and `mul_pos_fin` with the lanes of `z` added.
- [`DecBinary.v`](theories/DecBinary.v): `set_dec_correct`, `elem2_set_dec_correct` and `dec_holds` above. `image2_set_all`, `p2_set_all` and `strongest2_set_all`: for all of R² as the domain, `image2_set`, `p2_set` and `strongest2_set` are `image2`, `p2` and `strongest2`. `d2_continue_in2_sub`: a function that is continuous at a point relative to `D` is continuous there relative to any box in `D`. `set_dec_empty_any`, `set_dec_keep` and `set_dec_lower`: `set_dec` with a decoration other than `ill` returns `EMPTY` for an empty interval, and otherwise keeps the decoration if it is `com` only for a common interval, and lowers `com` to `dac` for an interval that is not common.
- [`DecTernary.v`](theories/DecTernary.v): `set_dec_correct3` and `dec_holds3` above. `strongest3_Dac` and `strongest3_Com` give the strongest decoration on a box: `com` if `p3 Com` holds, and `dac` if `p3 Dac` holds but `p3 Com` does not. `p3_Dac_continuous`: `p3 Dac` holds on every nonempty box for a function of three reals that is continuous at each point of R³, as its restriction to the box is continuous too.
- [`DivTight.v`](theories/DivTight.v): `tightest_lanes_lub`, `quot_tight_a`, `quot_unbounded` and `quot_lub_c` above. `tight_lub`: a largest element, or none for a set with no upper bound, is a least upper bound in the sense of `lub`.
- [`DivLane.v`](theories/DivLane.v): `div_lane_pos`, `div_lane_neg`, `div_lane_inf` and `div_lane_nonpos` above. `div_lane_finite`: `Bdiv mode_UP` of two finite lanes, the second not 0, is the least float or +∞ at or above their quotient, as `mul_lane_finite` shows for a product.
- [`DivSets.v`](theories/DivSets.v): `div_image_P`, `div_image_N`, `recip_image_P` and `recip_image_N` above, and the sets of numerators and denominators of each class. `div_tightest_P`, `div_tightest_N`, `recip_tightest_P` and `recip_tightest_N` put the rewritten image together with `tightest_lanes_lub`.
- [`DivZero.v`](theories/DivZero.v): `div_empty`, `div_zero`, `div_entire`, `recip_empty`, `recip_entire` and `contains_zero` above. `tightest_entire`: `ENTIRE` is the tightest interval that contains a set with no upper and no lower bound.
- [`DivCases.v`](theories/DivCases.v): the lemmas from `div_MN1` to `div_PP1`, and `recip_N0`, `recip_N1`, `recip_P0` and `recip_P1` above. `div_MN1_eq` and the other lemmas named `_eq` give the lanes that each helper and each branch of `Interval::recip` returns, as `Bdiv mode_UP` of two lanes, or +∞.
- [`MulRevSets.v`](theories/MulRevSets.v): `mul_rev_one_component`, `mul_rev_split`, `quot_convex`, `mul_rev_div`, `mul_rev_convex` and `two_output_two` above. `component_self`: a nonempty convex set is its only component. `two_output_empty` and `two_output_one`: the other two forms of `two_output`, for an empty set and for a nonempty convex one. In `quot_convex`, `z` between `z1 = u1 / q1` and `z2 = u2 / q2` is `u / q`, where `u` and `q` are weighted means of `u1` and `u2`, and of `q1` and `q2`, with the same weights.
- [`MulRevTwo.v`](theories/MulRevTwo.v): `mul_rev_N1M`, `mul_rev_P1M` and `yset_M` above. `mul_rev_N1M_eq` and `mul_rev_P1M_eq` give the lanes that the two branches return, as `Bdiv mode_UP` of two lanes, or +∞.
- [`MulRevCases.v`](theories/MulRevCases.v): `mul_rev_empty`, `mul_rev_entire`, `mul_rev_nz` and `mul_rev_div_eq` above. `classify_mem0`, `classify_not_mem0` and `classify_nonzero`: 0 is in an interval in `M`, `N0`, `P0` or `Z`, and not in one in `N1` or `P1`, and an interval in `N0`, `N1`, `P0` or `P1` holds a real other than 0.
- [`CancelSpec.v`](theories/CancelSpec.v): `cancel_result_empty`, `cancel_result_hull`, `superset_iff`, `not_defined`, `cancel_result_ext` and `cancel_result_valid` above. `cancel_empty`, `cancel_undefined` and `cancel_common` give `cancel_result` in each case: the empty interval for an empty `X` and a bounded `Y`, `ENTIRE` where cancelMinus has no value, and for `X = [a, b]` and `Y = [c, d]` the tightest interval that contains `[a - c, b - d]` if `d - c <= b - a`, and `ENTIRE` otherwise. `no_real_empty`: a `valid` interval with no reals is `EMPTY`.
- [`CancelLanes.v`](theories/CancelLanes.v): `not_common_unbounded`, `width_lane`, `halve`, `max_min_lanes` and `fast_two_sum` above. `common_lanes`: the lanes `-a` and `b` of a common interval are finite, `a <= b`, and its reals are those of `[a, b]`. `partial_cmp_finite`: `partial_cmp` of finite floats is `Some` of the order of their values. `RN_le_MAX`: a real below `2^1024 - 2^970`, the midpoint of `f64::MAX` and `2^1024`, rounds to nearest to at most `f64::MAX`.
- [`CancelWidths.v`](theories/CancelWidths.v): `cancel_common_eq` and `add_inf_ge` above. `cancel_tail_correct`: the comparison of the widths, from `hadd_rn` on, for finite lanes of which at least one width does not overflow.
- [`CancelHull.v`](theories/CancelHull.v): `sub_lanes_tightest` above.
- [`MpfrRound.v`](theories/MpfrRound.v): `mpfr_fn_round` and `f64_mpfr_format` above. `mpfr_fn_correct`: a function that `mpfr_fn!` defines rounds `f` of a finite argument in the domain once, in its direction. `mpfr_fn_inf`: it takes an infinity to the infinity that the MPFR function gives. `round_up_lane` and `round_dn_lane`: a float rounded up from `r`, or the negation of one rounded down, is the least float or +∞ at or above `r`, or `-r`.
- [`RealBounds.v`](theories/RealBounds.v): the greatest lower bound and the least upper bound of the values of a function on R (`glb_of`, `lub_of`), and what it means to have none (`unbounded_below`, `unbounded_above`), which `low_end` and `high_end` use.
- [`ExpReal.v`](theories/ExpReal.v), [`LogReal.v`](theories/LogReal.v) and [`TrigReal.v`](theories/TrigReal.v): the facts about the point functions that the instances use: each is monotone and continuous on its domain, relative to it for `asin` and `acos` at -1 and 1, and has the bounds, or no bounds, at the ends of its domain that the special values in `Mpfr.v` round, such as `tanh_glb` and `tanh_lub`, -1 and 1, and `ln_near0` and `ln_big`, no bound near 0 or near +∞.
- [`Interior.v`](theories/Interior.v): `intersection_mem`, `interior_mem` and `subset_mem` above: for `valid` intervals, the reals of `intersection` are those of both, `interior` holds exactly when each real of the first has a neighbourhood in the second, and `subset` exactly when each real of the first is a real of the second. It also shows that the domains `[0, +∞]`, `[-1, 1]` and `[1, +∞]` that `src/elementary.rs` intersects with are `valid`, and what their reals are.
- [`ElemDec.v`](theories/ElemDec.v): `elem_set_dec_correct`, `dec_holds_dom`, `elem_set_dec_trv_com`, `elem_dec_correct` and `elem_dec_impl_correct` above. `elem_set_dec_trv_com` allows `com` for an empty `x`, for which `dec_holds_dom` describes `trv`, as `def` asks for a nonempty `x`. The image and the result are then empty, and `set_dec` returns `EMPTY` for `trv` and `com` alike, so it applies `elem_set_dec_correct` with `trv` there. `continuity_pt_continue_in`: a function that is continuous at a point is continuous there relative to any set. `strongest_dom_Trv`, `strongest_dom_Dac` and `strongest_dom_Com`: `trv` is the strongest decoration if `def` does not hold, `dac` if `dac` holds and `com` does not, and `com` if `com` holds. `image_dom_all`, `hull_tightest_all`, `p_dom_all` and `strongest_dom_all`: on all of R as the domain, `image_dom` is `image`, the hull of the image is the tightest interval that contains it, and for that hull, `p_dom` and `strongest_dom` are `p` and `strongest`.
- [`MonoInc.v`](theories/MonoInc.v), [`LogDom.v`](theories/LogDom.v) and [`ClosedDom.v`](theories/ClosedDom.v): the generic theorems above, each in a `Section` whose hypotheses are what the instances in `Elem.v` prove of their point functions and assume of MPFR. `closed_gen_correct` and `dec_closed_gen_correct` cover `closed_inc` and `Interval::acos_impl`, which differ only in how they compute the result from the intersection.
- [`Atanh.v`](theories/Atanh.v) and [`Cosh.v`](theories/Cosh.v): `atanh_correct`, `dec_atanh_correct`, `cosh_correct` and `dec_cosh_correct` above, with the bounds of an interval from `LogDom.v` and `MonoInc.v`. `ch_fmax_fin`: `fmax` of two finite floats is the larger real, whichever zero it returns.
- [`PiBounds.v`](theories/PiBounds.v): `PI_Q` and `PI_bounds` above. `PI_const` and `FRAC_PI_2_const`: `Interval::PI` and `Interval::FRAC_PI_2` satisfy `trig_const` for π and π/2.
- [`NivenPoly.v`](theories/NivenPoly.v): polynomials with integer coefficients, as lists of their coefficients, lowest degree first, evaluated by Horner's rule (`zp_ev`), and their formal derivatives (`zp_d`). `zp_ev_d`: `zp_ev (zp_d l)` is the derivative of `zp_ev l`. `zp_ev_dn_0`: the `k`-th derivative at 0 is `k!` times the coefficient of `x^k`. `zp_ev_dn_high`: the `k`-th derivative is 0 once `k` reaches the length of the list. `zp_ev_qpow`: `zp_qpow a b n` is `(a - b x)^n`.
- [`NivenAlt.v`](theories/NivenAlt.v): chains of derivatives, sequences of functions of which each is the derivative of the one before (`ch_chain`). `ch_unique`: two chains that start with the same function agree everywhere, by the uniqueness of derivatives. `ch_reflect`: for a chain `d`, `x => (-1)^k d_k(c - x)` is the chain that starts with `x => d_0(c - x)`. `ch_alt_deriv`: for a chain `d`, the derivative of the alternating sum `d_s - d_(s+2) + ... + (-1)^m d_(s+2m)` is the same sum from `d_(s+1)`. `ch_alt_rec`: the sum plus the one from `d_(s+2)` is `d_s + (-1)^m d_(s+2m+2)`.
- [`NivenSym.v`](theories/NivenSym.v): `nv_d_sym`, `nv_d_int` and `nv_d_high` above. `nv_d_sym` follows from `ch_unique` and `ch_reflect`, as `f(π - x) = f(x)`, and the other two from `zp_ev_dn_0` and `zp_ev_dn_high`.
- [`PiIrrational.v`](theories/PiIrrational.v): `pi_irrational`, `nv_G_deriv`, `nv_G_ends` and `nv_bound` above.
- [`TrigPieces.v`](theories/TrigPieces.v): the facts about `sin`, `cos` and `tan` that the branches use: the periods, monotonicity on each piece (`cos_piece_dec`, `cos_piece_inc`, `sin_piece_inc`, `sin_piece_dec`, `tan_piece_inc`), the values at the ends of the pieces (`cos_kPI`, `sin_kPI2_1`, `sin_kPI2_3`), that an interval at least `2 * PI` wide holds a real where `cos`, or `sin`, is 1 and one where it is -1 (`cos_wide`, `sin_wide`), that `tan` has no bound on either side of a pole (`tan_pole_unbounded`) or on an interval at least `PI` wide (`tan_wide`), that it is continuous on its domain (`tan_dom_cont`), and `tan_dom_f64` above.
- [`TrigQuot.v`](theories/TrigQuot.v): `trig_quot_lo` and `trig_quot_hi` above.
- [`TrigReduce.v`](theories/TrigReduce.v): `trig_reduce`, `trig_reduce_exact`, `n_small`, `n_inf`, `rem_euclid_2_int` and `rem_euclid_4_int` above. `floor_div_bounds` and `floor_div_eq`: `k = floor(r / c)` is the integer with `k * c <= r < (k + 1) * c`, for `c > 0`.
- [`TrigBranch.v`](theories/TrigBranch.v): for each of the six forms that the branches return, when it contains the image (`br_inc_encl` and the others) and when it is the tightest interval that does (`br_inc_tight` and the others).
- [`TrigQuad.v`](theories/TrigQuad.v): `quadrant_correct` and `qd_not_kpi` above. `qd_sin_neg` and `qd_cos_pos`: for a finite float `r`, `sin_rd(r) < 0.0` holds exactly when `sin r < 0`, and `cos_ru(r) > 0.0` exactly when `cos r > 0`.
- [`TrigCount.v`](theories/TrigCount.v): `quadrants_correct` and `int_tests` above. `count_f_val`: for the quadrants `ja` and `jb` of `a` and `b`, the count that `quadrants` returns, `d` or `d + 4` for `d = (jb - ja) mod 4`, is a finite float that holds that integer.
- [`TrigCert.v`](theories/TrigCert.v): `floors_exact_ok` and `trig_wide` above. `tc_with_valid`: the singleton `[q, q]` of a finite `q`, which `floors_exact` divides, is `valid`.
- [`Cos.v`](theories/Cos.v), [`Sin.v`](theories/Sin.v) and [`Tan.v`](theories/Tan.v): the theorems above, from a case analysis of the branches (`cs_main`, `sn_main` and `tn_impl`), and, in `Tan.v`, `tn_sub_wide` above.
- [`Dom2.v`](theories/Dom2.v): `elem_dec2_impl_correct` above, from `elem2_set_dec_correct` in `DecBinary.v`. `image2_set_prod`, `p2_set_prod` and `strongest2_set_prod`: for a domain that is a product `D1 × D2`, `image2_set`, `p2_set` and `strongest2_set` are `image2_dom`, `p2_dom` and `strongest2_dom`, and `continue_in2_set` is `continue_in2` (`continue_in2_set_prod`). `dec_div_correct` uses `image2_set_prod`, `continue_in2_set_prod` and `strongest2_set_prod` to apply `elem2_set_dec_correct` to `/`.
- [`PowReal.v`](theories/PowReal.v) and [`PownReal.v`](theories/PownReal.v): the facts about `Rpow` and `powerRZ` that the proofs use: the values of `Rpow` at a base 0 or 1 and at an exponent 0, and of `powerRZ` at `n = 0`, their signs, their monotonicity (`Rpow_le_base_pos`, `Rpow_le_base_neg`, `Rpow_le_exp_ge1`, `Rpow_le_exp_le1`, `pown_le_pos`, `pown_le_neg`, `pown_le_odd`), their symmetry for an even or odd `n` (`pown_even_opp`, `pown_odd_opp`), the limits that the special values round (such as `Rpow_base_large_neg`, `Rpow_exp_large_gt1` and `pown_small_neg`), and `Rpow_continuous` and `pown_cont` above.
- [`PowMpfr.v`](theories/PowMpfr.v): what `pow_rd`, `pow_ru`, `pown_rd` and `pown_ru` return: at finite arguments in the domain, the value of the point function rounded once (`pow_fin`, `pown_fin`, from `mpfr_fn_round`), and at the special arguments that the code passes, a zero, 1 or an infinity (`pow_zero_neg` and the others). `pl_lo`, `pl_hi`, `pl_lo_inf`, `pl_lo_fmin` and `pl_hi_fmax` above.
- [`PowCases.v`](theories/PowCases.v): `pw_image_inter` and `pw_xok_inter` above. `pw_interior` and `pw_subset`: what the tests `interior` and `subset` of `[0, +∞]` that `Interval::pow` makes say of the reals of `x`.
- [`PowNeg.v`](theories/PowNeg.v), [`PowPos.v`](theories/PowPos.v), [`PowMixLo.v`](theories/PowMixLo.v), [`PowMixHi.v`](theories/PowMixHi.v) and [`PowMix.v`](theories/PowMix.v): the branches of `Interval::pow` above (`pw_neg_empty`, `pw_neg_lt1`, `pw_neg_gt1`, `pw_neg_mid`, `pw_pos_lt1`, `pw_pos_gt1`, `pw_pos_mid`, `pw_mix_zero`, `pw_mix_lo`, `pw_mix_hi` and `pw_mix`).
- [`Pow.v`](theories/Pow.v) and [`Powi.v`](theories/Powi.v): the theorems above, from a case analysis of the branches (`pow_correct` and `pwi_impl`), and `pw_dec_cases`, the decoration that `Interval::pow_impl` returns.
- [`AtanReal.v`](theories/AtanReal.v): the facts about `Ratan2` that the proofs use, all from Stdlib's `atan`: its values on the axes and by the formula (`Ratan2_pos_x`, `Ratan2_neg_x_ge`, `Ratan2_neg_x_lt`, `Ratan2_zero_x_pos` and the others), `Ratan2_range`, `Ratan2_polar` and `Ratan2_polar_unique` above, its ranges on the half-planes and the closed quadrants (`Ratan2_upper`, `Ratan2_q1` and the others), its monotonicity above, its limits as `x` or `y` goes to an infinity and as `y` goes to 0 from below (`Ratan2_x_pinf`, `Ratan2_y_0neg` and the others), and `Ratan2_cont`, `Ratan2_cont_upper`, `Ratan2_not_cont` and `Ratan2_not_cont_in2` above.
- [`PiTight.v`](theories/PiTight.v): `PI_up`, `PI_dn`, `FRAC_PI_2_up` and `FRAC_PI_2_dn` above: the upper bound of `Interval::PI` is the least float at or above π, and the negation of its lower bound the least float at or above -π, by `least_lane` in `AddSub.v`, and the same for `Interval::FRAC_PI_2` and π/2. `pt_PI_facts` and `pt_FRAC_PI_2_facts` compute that the upper bound of each constant is Flocq's `Bsucc` of its lower bound, and `pt_succ_le` shows that no float lies strictly between a float and its `Bsucc`, so with `PI_bounds`, the bounds are the floats next to π and π/2. `PI_tightest` and `FRAC_PI_2_tightest` in `ConstPi.v` use `pt_PI_facts` and `pt_FRAC_PI_2_facts` too.
- [`Atan2Mpfr.v`](theories/Atan2Mpfr.v): `at_val`, `at_args`, `atan2_round`, `at_hi_lane` and `at_lo_lane` above. `at_val` is `Ratan2` of the reals of two finite floats, and at an infinity the limit that the special value of `atan2_ok` rounds, and `atan2_round` shows that `atan2_rd` and `atan2_ru` round it in their directions at every point of `at_args`.
- [`Atan2Dec.v`](theories/Atan2Dec.v): `ad_empty`, `ad_trv`, `ad_com`, `ad_dac` and `ad_def`, the conditions on the box under which `dec_holds` holds for each decoration, which `at2_impl_dec` in `Atan2.v` applies.
- [`Atan2Const.v`](theories/Atan2Const.v), [`Atan2Right.v`](theories/Atan2Right.v), [`Atan2Lower.v`](theories/Atan2Lower.v) and [`Atan2Left.v`](theories/Atan2Left.v): the cases of `Interval::atan2` above (`at2_empty`, `at2_full`, `at2_p0p0`, `at2_mp0`, `at2_n0p0`, `at2_p0n0`, `at2_p0m`, `at2_pi`, `at2_zero`, `at2_neg_half_pi`, `at2_half_pi`, `at2_q1`, `at2_mp1`, `at2_p1m`, `at2_q4`, `at2_mn1`, `at2_q2`, `at2_q3` and `at2_n1p0`).
- [`Atan2.v`](theories/Atan2.v): the theorems above, from a case analysis of the 49 pairs of classes, and `at2_impl_dec`, the decoration that `Interval::atan2_impl` returns.
- [`Cbrt.v`](theories/Cbrt.v): `cbrt_correct`, `dec_cbrt_correct`, `Rcbrt_continuous`, `cbrt_unbounded_below` and `cbrt_unbounded_above` above. `cbrt_inc`: `Rcbrt` is nondecreasing, the form of `Rcbrt_lt` that `MonoInc.v` asks for. `Rcbrt_continuous` takes, at `r0` with `c = Rcbrt r0`, the reals between `(c - ε)^3` and `(c + ε)^3`, whose cube roots are within ε of `c`.
- [`Hypot.v`](theories/Hypot.v): `hypot_correct`, `dec_hypot_correct`, `Rhypot_continuous`, `Rhypot_abs`, `Rhypot_le`, `Rhypot_lipschitz`, `Rabs_le_Rhypot_l` and `Rabs_le_Rhypot_r` above. `Rhypot_nonneg` and `Rhypot_sqr`: `Rhypot r t` is at least 0 and its square is `r * r + t * t`. `hull_nonempty`, `hull_sup`, `hull_inf`, `hull_up` and `hull_inf_finite`: a nonempty hull of a set of reals, such as the interval that `abs` returns, has a nonempty set, a finite bound of it is an element of the set and bounds the set, an upper bound +∞ leaves the set without an upper bound, and the lower bound is finite if the set has only reals `>= 0`, which `image_Rabs_nonneg` gives for the absolute values. `mpfr_hypot_inf_l` and `mpfr_hypot_inf_r`: `hypot_ru` is +∞ if one argument is +∞, from the special values of `hypot_ok`.
- [`Limbs.v`](theories/Limbs.v): `bit_len_correct`, `cmp_correct`, `add_assign_correct`, `sub_assign_correct`, `mul_add_limb_correct`, `mul_add_row_correct`, `mul_into_correct`, `shl_into_correct`, `shr_into_correct` and `div_rem_limb_correct`: each loop of `src/limbs.rs` but `div_rem` and the steps of Algorithm D, which `KnuthStep.v` and `Knuth.v` prove, computes, for limbs with `limbs_ok` and the lengths that its comment asks for, the operation on integers that its comment gives, with the carry or the borrow out of the top limb and limbs with `limbs_ok`. `val_bounds`, `val_app`, `val_firstn` and `val_skipn`: the value of a list of limbs, of two lists one after the other, and of its low and its high limbs.
- [`KnuthStep.v`](theories/KnuthStep.v): `knuth_step_correct`, `est_upper` and `est_lower` above. `qhat_loop`: the loop of D3 ends with `q <= qhat <= q + 1` for the limb `q` of the quotient. `submul_correct`: the loop of D4 subtracts `qhat` times the divisor, with the carry of the products and the borrow of the subtractions.
- [`Knuth.v`](theories/Knuth.v): `div_rem_correct` above, `div_rem_knuth_correct`, for Algorithm D with its normalization (D1) and unnormalization (D8), and `div_rem_zero`: `div_rem` by 0 panics. `knuth_loop_correct`: the loop over the steps (D2, D7), with the quotient limbs computed so far and the rest of the dividend below the divisor.
- [`NatOps.v`](theories/NatOps.v): the theorems above, and `from_limbs_correct`: `from_limbs` drops the zero limbs at the top and keeps the value. `nat_ok`: limbs with `limbs_ok` and no zero limb at the top, of which `nat_ok_unique` shows that each value has at most one list. `nat_mul_correct`, `nat_shl_correct`, `nat_div_rem_correct`, `nat_pow5_correct` and `nat_digits10_correct`: the loops of `NatLimbs` on limbs with `nat_ok`, which return limbs with `nat_ok`, from which the theorems on `Z` follow by `to_limbs_correct`.
- [`ParseScale.v`](theories/ParseScale.v): `scale_correct` above. `scaled_ok s a`: the `Scaled` `s` describes the real `a > 0`: `Huge` says that `a > 2^1024`, `Tiny` that `0 < a < 2^-1074`, and `Mid q e sticky` that `a = (q + f) * 2^e`, with `2^55 <= q < 2^57`, `-2^20 <= e <= 2^20`, within the bounds that `round_up_exact_pre` in `Spec.v` asks for, and a `0 <= f < 1` that is 0 exactly when `sticky` is false. `nat_bits_spec`: a positive integer of `k` bits lies in `[2^(k - 1), 2^k)`. `ratio_bounds`: so `2^(t - 1) < n / d < 2^(t + 1)` for the difference `t` of the bit lengths of `n` and `d`. `pow5_bounds_nonneg` and `pow5_bounds_neg`: `5^k` lies between `4^k` and `8^k`. `coarse_bounds`: with these, the bounds of `|x|` before `5^|e5|` is computed, from which `scale` returns `Huge` or `Tiny` when they imply it. `mid_ok`: the quotient and the remainder of the integer division describe `|x|` as `Mid` says. `nat_bits_le` and `nat_bits_mul_pow5`: a positive integer below `2^k` has at most `k` bits, and multiplying it by `5^k` adds at most `3 k` bits, so that every `i128`, `u64` and `i32` operation of `scale` stays in range for a `Rat` with `rat_ok` and a numerator other than 0.
- [`ParseRound.v`](theories/ParseRound.v): `rational_to_f64_correct`, `number_to_f64_correct` and `number_neg_correct` above. `round_up_scaled_correct`: for a real `v` other than 0 whose magnitude `s` describes (`scaled_ok s |v|`), `round_up_scaled` of the sign of `v` and `s` returns `v` rounded up (`rounds_up_to v` of `Spec.v`). `rounds_up_to_mpfr_round`: that is `v` rounded toward +∞ to `f64_format`, as `mpfr_round` says. `rounds_up_to_opp`: the negation of `-v` rounded up is `v` rounded down. `rounds_up_to_eq`: `v` rounded up and `v` rounded down are equal exactly when `v` is a finite `f64`, which gives `inexact`. `round_overflow`: a rounded `f64` is infinite exactly when it is not finite, which gives `overflow`.
- [`ParseHull.v`](theories/ParseHull.v): `bounds_tightest` above. `ninterval_ok_set`: an `NInterval` with `ninterval_ok` other than `NInterval::EMPTY` denotes some real. `lower_lane` and `upper_lane`: the converted bounds are the lanes of the least upper bounds of the negated reals and of the reals of the literal.
- [`ParseExact.v`](theories/ParseExact.v): `try_from_ninterval_exact_correct` above. `lo_mem_exact` and `hi_mem_exact`: an exact bound rounded in its direction bounds the same reals as the bound itself.
- [`ParseDec.v`](theories/ParseDec.v): the theorems above. `dn_NAI_ok`: `DecNInterval::NAI` has `dec_ninterval_ok`. `dn_new_ok`: `DecNInterval::new` of an `NInterval` with `ninterval_ok` has `dec_ninterval_ok`. `dn_set_dec_ok`: so has `DecNInterval::set_dec` of one, with any decoration, `ill` included. `dn_decorated_ok`: what `dn_decorated` returns for a literal whose `NInterval` has `ninterval_ok`, with a decoration `d`, if it accepts the literal, has `dec_ninterval_ok`, the decoration `d`, and the literal's `NInterval` unless `d` is `ill`. `from_dec_ninterval_set_dec`: what `From<DecNInterval>` returns for any `x` with `ninterval_ok (nx x)`, with the decoration as `set_dec` gives it. `from_dec_ninterval_not_common`: for a literal with `ninterval_ok` whose bounds are both rationals and a decoration other than `ill`, the result is not common exactly when the conversion of a bound overflowed. `nis_common_bounded`: a literal whose bounds are both rationals denotes a bounded set. `overflow_not_common`: if a bound overflowed, the interval that `From<DecNInterval>` passes to `set_dec` is not common.
- [`FormatCut.v`](theories/FormatCut.v): `format_cut_dec_correct`, `format_cut_bin_correct` and `round_sign` above. `format_decompose_correct` and `format_exact_dec_correct`: `decompose` and `exact_dec` give the magnitude of a finite `x` other than 0 exactly, as `m * 2^e` with `0 < m < 2^53` and `-1074 <= e <= 971`, and as `n * 10^s` with `0 < n < 10^767` and `-1074 <= s <= 0`. `round_FIX_cut`, for any radix `β`: rounding `n * β^s`, for `n >= 0`, to a multiple of `β^(s + j)` toward zero gives `n / β^j` times `β^(s + j)`, and away from zero one more unless `β^j` divides `n`. `round_FLX_FIX`: rounding to `prec` significant digits is rounding to a multiple of `β^(mag y - prec)`.
- [`FormatDec.v`](theories/FormatDec.v): `format_fixed_correct` and `format_sci_correct` above. `round_F2R_exact`: rounding a number that the format holds keeps it, which shows that `fixed` and `sci`, which cap the precision at 1100, round as with the precision itself above that.
- [`FormatHex.v`](theories/FormatHex.v): the theorems above. `bpow_radix16` and `round_radix16_FIX`: `16^e = 2^(4e)`, so rounding to a multiple of `16^e` is rounding to a multiple of `2^(4e)`. `mag_hex2` and `mag_hex16`: the leading bit of `m53 * 2^(ex - 52)`, for `2^52 <= m53 < 2^53`, is at the exponent `ex` of 2, and its leading hex digit at the exponent `ex / 4` of 16, rounded down. `format_hex_cases`: what `hex` computes from these, for each precision.
- [`DyRound.v`](theories/DyRound.v): `round_correct`, `dround_width` and `encl_round_width` above, and `is_zero_correct`, `neg_correct`, `shl_correct`, `cmp_abs_correct`, `cmp_correct`, `dmin_correct` and `dmax_correct`: `is_zero`, `-x` and the exact `shl` of a `Dyadic`, the order of their magnitudes and of their values, and `min` and `max`. `round_tail`: the last step of `round`, which adds 1 to the limbs that it keeps where it dropped bits other than 0 and rounds away from 0. `low_bits_nonzero_correct`: the test of the dropped bits. `dy_format`: a `Dyadic` with `dy_ok` is in the format of `dprec n` bits.
- [`DyConv.v`](theories/DyConv.v): `from_f64_correct` and `to_f64_correct` above. `round_up_window`: the 128 bits and the sticky bit that `round_up` passes to `round_up_exact` describe the magnitude of the `Dyadic`. `round_up_correct`: so `round_up` rounds it toward +∞, as `rounds_up_to` in `ParseRound.v` says, by `round_up_exact_correct` in `RoundUpExact.v`, and without it for the magnitudes at or above `2^1024` and below `2^-1074`.
- [`DyIsqrt.v`](theories/DyIsqrt.v): `isqrt_correct` above. `seed_bounds`: the 32 bits of the seed are at least 2^31 and below 2^32, whatever `f64::sqrt` returns, as they come from the bits of its fraction with the implicit bit set. `newton_ge`, `newton_lt` and `newton_le`: from any `s > 0`, a step of Newton's iteration is at least `floor(sqrt(x))`, by the inequality of the arithmetic and geometric means, lowers an `s` above it, and stays at most `x`. `isqrt_loop_correct`: so the loop stops at `floor(sqrt(x))`, within its fuel.
- [`DyOps.v`](theories/DyOps.v): the theorems of the operations above. `add_window_correct`: the limbs into which `add` shifts the operand of the smaller magnitude, with the sticky bit of the bits shifted out, hold that operand but for a fraction of their last limb. `add_body_correct`: `add` of two numbers other than 0, the larger in magnitude first. `sqrt_frac`: the square root of an integer between `s^2` and `(s + 1)^2` is `s` and a fraction strictly between 0 and 1, so the sticky bit of `sqrt` is right.
- [`DyDriver.v`](theories/DyDriver.v): the theorems of the enclosures and of Ziv's strategy above, and `encl_sub_width`. `mul_corners` and `div_corners`: a product or a quotient of points of two enclosures lies between the least and the greatest of the four products or quotients of their bounds. `round_between`: if both bounds of an enclosure round to equal `f64`s, every point of it rounds to the first, which gives `ziv_levels_rounds`: a level of a list of levels that passes gives the correct rounding, and so `ziv_strict_correct`. `ziv_levels_cap`: where a list of levels `ls` and then 64 limbs passes, `ls` and then `ziv_cap` give the same, which gives `ziv_strict_ziv`. `rounds_side`: a correct rounding is on the correct side, and `f64_side_le`: a value on the correct side of `r'` is on the correct side of every `r` beyond `r'` in the other direction, which with `ziv_levels_rounds` and `to_f64_correct` gives `ziv_side`. `f64_gap`: a real number that is not an `f64` is at a positive distance from every `f64`, which gives `ziv_at_passes`. `encl_mul_correct` covers both paths of `Encl::mul`: where neither lower bound is negative, it takes only the product of the lower bounds and that of the upper bounds, which are then the least and the greatest of the four. `encl_div_correct` covers both paths of `Encl::div`: where the lower bound of the divisor is not negative, and so positive, it takes only the quotient of the lower bound of the dividend by the upper bound of the divisor, or by its lower bound if that of the dividend is negative, and that of the upper bound of the dividend by the lower bound of the divisor, or by its upper bound if that of the dividend is negative, which are then the least and the greatest of the four (`div_pos_min` and `div_pos_max`).
- [`ConstTight.v`](theories/ConstTight.v): `const_tightest`, `const_tightest_Q`, `com_strongest0`, `const_strongest0`, `ENTIRE_dec_correct`, `EMPTY_dec_correct` and `NAI_dec_correct` above. `B2Q_correct`: `B2Q x`, which is `m * 2^e` for a finite float `x` and 0 otherwise, has the real value of `x` that `B2R` gives.
- [`ExpSeries.v`](theories/ExpSeries.v): `exp_Q_bounds` above. `exp_alt_bounds`: for `0 <= x <= 1`, the partial sums of `Σ (-1)^n x^n / n!` with an even number of terms bound `exp(-x)` below, and those with an odd number above. `es_sumQ_R`: `es_sumQ` computes these partial sums for a rational `x`.
- [`ConstPi.v`](theories/ConstPi.v), [`ConstInv.v`](theories/ConstInv.v), [`ConstLn.v`](theories/ConstLn.v) and [`ConstLog.v`](theories/ConstLog.v): the theorems above, with `const_tightest_sqrt`, `LN_2_Q` and `LN_10_Q`.
- [`DyGrow.v`](theories/DyGrow.v): `encl_le` above. `point_step`, `neg_step`, `add_step`, `sub_step`, `mul_step`, `div_step`, `mul_u64_step`, `div_u64_step` and `shl_step`: the theorems of `DyDriver.v` for an operation on enclosures that returned, for operands with `encl_le E` for `E` up to 2^60, with a bound on the exponents of the result, such as `Z.max Ea Eb + 2^13` for a sum. `encl_le_small`: `encl_le E` gives `encl_small` for `E <= 2^61`.
- [`AtanhSeries.v`](theories/AtanhSeries.v): `atanh_sum_bounds`: for `0 <= x < 1`, `atanh x` minus the sum of the first `n` terms of its series is between 0 and `x^(2n+1) / (1 - x^2)`, by the mean value theorem. `ln2_atanh` and `ln10_atanh`: `ln 2 = 2 atanh(1/3)` and `ln 10 = 3 ln 2 + 2 atanh(1/9)`. `zsum_bounds`: the sum `zsum` of the terms floored in integer fixed point, at `P` bits, bounds `2^P atanh(1/q)`.
- [`DyConst.v`](theories/DyConst.v): `ln2_correct` and `ln10_correct` above. `LN2_bounds` and `LN10_bounds`: the tables are `floor(ln 2 * 2^4224)` and `floor(ln 10 * 2^4222)`, from `zsum_bounds` at 4,288 bits, which `vm_compute` evaluates. `dc_split_correct` and `dc_level`: `split` gives a normalized `h` and an enclosure of the rest at each of the 64 levels, which `vm_compute` checks.
- [`DyExpCore.v`](theories/DyExpCore.v): `series_correct`: `series` encloses `1 - e^-s` for `0 <= s <= 1`, by Horner's scheme on the scaled remainders of the Taylor series of `e^-s`, which lie in `[0, 1]` (`exp_alt_bounds` of `ExpSeries.v`). `exp_neg_correct` and `expm1_neg_correct`: `exp_neg` and `expm1_neg` enclose `e^-y` and `1 - e^-y` for `0 <= y <= 2`. `params_bounds`: `params` gives `1 <= j <= 16` halvings and `1 <= K <= 256` terms, for which the exponents stay bounded.
- [`DyExpRules.v`](theories/DyExpRules.v): `exp_overflow_rule`, `exp_underflow_rule`, `exp_tiny_rule`, `exp2_overflow_rule`, `exp2_underflow_rule`, `exp2_tiny_rule`, `exp10_overflow_rule`, `exp10_underflow_rule`, `exp10_tiny_rule` and `exp10_int_rule`, the rules of e^x, 2^x and 10^x above, with thresholds that follow from the bounds of `ln 2` and `ln 10` of `ConstLn.v`. `pow10_exact`: the literals `1e1` to `1e22` are 10^1 to 10^22 exactly.
- [`DyHypRules.v`](theories/DyHypRules.v), [`DySinhTiny.v`](theories/DySinhTiny.v) and [`DyTanhTiny.v`](theories/DyTanhTiny.v): `sinh_overflow_rule`, `sinh_overflow_neg_rule`, `cosh_overflow_rule`, `cosh_tiny_rule`, `tanh_big_rule`, `tanh_big_neg_rule`, `sinh_tiny_rule` and `tanh_tiny_rule`, the rules of the hyperbolic functions above.
- [`DyExp.v`](theories/DyExp.v) and [`DyHyp.v`](theories/DyHyp.v): the theorems above. `exp_encl_correct`: `exp_encl`, the reduction of e^x, encloses e^x. `scaled_gen`: `scaled`, where it returns, at an enclosure `y`, an integer `k` with `|k| <= 2^14`, a dyadic `h` and an enclosure `l` of `ln 2 - h`, both with exponents of at most 2^14 in magnitude, encloses `2^k e^-v` for every point `v` of `y`, and bounds the exponents of the result by `2^25 (E + 2^17) + 2^20` for any bound `E` on those of `y` with `0 <= E <= 2^29 + 2^19`. `ev_pow_correct` of `DyPowCore.v` uses its enclosure at `E = 2^29 + 2^19`, the bound of its `w`, beyond that of `scaled_correct`. `scaled_correct` is its case `E <= 2^20`, with the bound 2^48, which `exp_encl_correct`, `ev_exp2_correct` and `ev_exp10_correct` use. `ev_exp_correct`, `ev_exp2_correct`, `ev_exp10_correct`, `ev_cosh_correct`, `ev_sinh_correct` and `ev_tanh_correct`: each evaluation, where it returns, encloses the result at its level, as `driver_post` asks.
- [`FastRound.v`](theories/FastRound.v): `round_dir_correct` and `round_near_correct` above, the rounding tests of the fast paths, the lemmas `dir_step_eq` and `round_dir_eq`, which `Assumptions.v` does not print, that the step from `h` by the sign bit of `l` in `round_dir` and `round_dir_err` is the one that the comparisons of `l` with the bound and its negative choose, and `two_prod_err_fma_eq`: where `two_prod_is_exact` holds with and without an FMA, `two_prod_err` gives the same `f64` with both, zeros included, as both are the exact error.
- [`FastExpTables.v`](theories/FastExpTables.v): `t1_correct` and `t2_correct` above, and the bounds of the constants of `fast_exp`: `c1_exact`, that `C1` is an integer below 2^31 times 2^-43, so that `k C1` is exact for `|k| < 2^22`, `c12_correct`, that `C1 + C2` is within 2^-98 of ln 2 / 4096, `inv_correct`, that `INV` is within 2^-41 of 4096 / ln 2, `p3_correct` and `p4_correct`, that `P3` and `P4` are within 2^-56 and 2^-58 of 1/6 and 1/24, and `ln2_dd_correct` and `ln10_dd_correct`, that `LN2` and `LN10` are within 2^-106 and 2^-105 of ln 2 and ln 10. The bounds on ln 2 and ln 10 come from those of `DyConst.v`.
- [`FastExpReduce.v`](theories/FastExpReduce.v): `fast_exp_reduce` above, and `shifter_bits`, a lemma that `Assumptions.v` does not print, that `ks.to_bits() as i32 as i64` is the integer `ks - SHIFTER` where that difference rounds to an integer below 2^31 in magnitude, which `fast_exp`, `fast_reduce` of `DyadicTrig`, `fast_atan_core` and `fast_atan1` take as their index.
- [`FastExpErr.v`](theories/FastExpErr.v): lemmas, none of which `Assumptions.v` prints: the error of one rounded `+`, `-` or `*` from a bound on its exact result, and bounds of e^r near 0, among them that of its Taylor polynomial of degree 4.
- [`FastExpPoly.v`](theories/FastExpPoly.v), [`FastExpTbl.v`](theories/FastExpTbl.v) and [`FastExpProd.v`](theories/FastExpProd.v): lemmas, none of which `Assumptions.v` prints, for the three steps of `fast_exp` after its reduction: `fast_exp_poly`, that `s + pl` is e^(rh + rl) within 2^-71, `fast_exp_tbl`, that `th + tl` is 2^(i/4096) 2^(j/64) within 2^-100, and `fast_exp_prod`, that `h + l` is `(s + pl) (th + tl)` within 2^-69, each with the bounds the next step needs, and, in `fast_exp_tbl` and in `prod_exact` of `FastExpProd.v`, that the products whose errors `two_prod_err` computes are exact.
- [`FastExpCore.v`](theories/FastExpCore.v): `fast_exp_correct` above, from the four files before it, and `fast_exp_fma`, that `fast_exp` returns the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastRoundSubBits.v`](theories/FastRoundSubBits.v) and [`FastRoundSubCommon.v`](theories/FastRoundSubCommon.v): lemmas, none of which `Assumptions.v` prints, for the tests of `FastRoundSub.v` and `FastRoundWideErr.v`: the bits of the `f64`s from 0 to 2^-1022 and of those of a binade (`to_from_bits`, `small_bits` and `bits_binade`), those of C = 2^(-1022 - e) (`sub_unit_spec`), the rounding to nearest of a number within 2^-1075 of a multiple of 2^-1074 (`rn_sub_grid`), the values of the bounds of the tests (`sub_consts` and `sub_far_consts`), the directed roundings of a number between two adjacent `f64`s from 0 to 2^-1022 (`sub_bracket`), the rounding to nearest of a `y 2^e` with `y` > 0 closer than g/2 to K g, for g = 2^(-1074 - e) and K in [0, 2^52], to the `f64` whose bits are K (`sub_near_grid`), and the steps that both tests share, from Fast2Sum of C and `h` to the bound on the distance of `y` - N g from the `u` that the tests compare, where N g is `s` - C (`sub_common`), and the same steps for a `y` within 2^-p of `h + l`, for an integer p in [55, 105], in place of 2^-68 (`sub_common_err`).
- [`FastRoundSub.v`](theories/FastRoundSub.v): `round_dir_sub_correct` and `round_near_sub_correct`, the rounding tests of the fast paths for results below 2^-1021, at an `e` in [-1085, -1022].
- [`FastRoundWide.v`](theories/FastRoundWide.v): `round_dir_wide_correct` above and `round_near_wide_correct`, the tests for an `e` in [-1085, 1023], `round_dir_err_wide_correct` and `round_near_err_wide_correct`, the tests of `FastRoundErr.v` for an `e` in [-1021, 1023], and `round_dir_err_normal_correct` and `round_near_err_normal_correct`, the same for an `e` in [-1022, 1023], with `h` > 1 at -1022.
- [`FastRoundWideErr.v`](theories/FastRoundWideErr.v): `round_dir_sub_err_correct` and `round_near_sub_err_correct`, the tests of `FastRoundSub.v` with the bound `err` = 2^-p of `FastRoundErr.v`, at an `e` in [-1085, -1022], `round_dir_wide_err_correct` and `round_near_wide_err_correct`, the tests of the fast path of x^y for an `e` of at least -1085, and the lemmas `sub_err_consts` and `sub_err_far_consts`, which `Assumptions.v` does not print, the values of the bounds of the tests.
- [`FastExpEdge.v`](theories/FastExpEdge.v): `fast_exp_edge_correct` above, `fast_exp_edge_fma`, that `fast_exp_edge` returns the same with and without an FMA, and `fast_exp_edge_in`, that it returns `None` unless `zh` is finite and |`zh`| <= 760.
- [`FastExpByEdge.v`](theories/FastExpByEdge.v): `fe_by_edge_correct`, the bound of `fast_exp_edge_correct` for 2^x and 10^x, from copies of the bounds of `fast_exp_by` in `FastExp.v` for |`zh`| up to 1000, and `fe_by_edge_fma`, that `fast_exp_by_edge` returns the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastExp.v`](theories/FastExp.v): `fast_exp_dir_correct`, `fast_exp_near_correct` and `fast_exp_dir_fma` above, and the same for `exp2` and `exp10`, from the bounds of `FastExpCore.v`, `FastExpEdge.v` and `FastExpByEdge.v`, the tests of `FastRound.v` and `FastRoundWide.v` and, for `exp2` and `exp10`, a bound of 2^-92 on the distance of `zh + zl` from `x c`.
- [`FastRoundErr.v`](theories/FastRoundErr.v): `round_dir_err_correct` above and `round_near_err_correct`, the rounding tests with an error bound as an argument, the lemma `round_dir_err_eq`, which `Assumptions.v` does not print, the same as `round_dir_eq` for `round_dir_err` and a finite `err` >= 0, `two_sum_correct`, that TwoSum returns the rounded sum and its exact error, and `normalize_correct`, that `normalize` scales a double-double with 2^-1021 <= `h` < 2 by a power of 2 to 1 <= `h` < 2, exactly.
- [`FastDiv.v`](theories/FastDiv.v): `dd_div_correct` above, and `dd_div_fma`, that `dd_div` returns the same with and without an FMA where `dd_div_correct`'s hypotheses hold.
- [`HypTaylor.v`](theories/HypTaylor.v), [`FastSinhHorner.v`](theories/FastSinhHorner.v) and [`FastSinhSteps.v`](theories/FastSinhSteps.v): lemmas, none of which `Assumptions.v` prints: the truncation errors of the Taylor polynomials of sinh of degree 15 and of cosh of degree 12 on [0, 1] (`sinh_taylor` and `cosh_taylor`), and the steps of `fast_sinh_poly`: the Horner scheme and 1/3! + u A (`sh_horner`, `sh_b`), t^2, t^3 and the product t^3 (1/3! + u A) (`sh_t3`, `sh_c`), and the final sum t + (ch + cl) (`sh_final`, `dd_out`).
- [`FastHypPoly.v`](theories/FastHypPoly.v) and [`FastCoshPoly.v`](theories/FastCoshPoly.v): `sinh_poly_correct` and `cosh_poly_correct`, the bounds of `fast_sinh_poly` and `fast_cosh_poly` given above, and `sinh_poly_fma` and `cosh_poly_fma`, that they return the same with and without an FMA on the same ranges of t.
- [`FastHypPos.v`](theories/FastHypPos.v), [`FastCoshPos.v`](theories/FastCoshPos.v) and [`FastTanhPos.v`](theories/FastTanhPos.v): `fast_sinh_pos_correct`, `fast_cosh_pos_correct` and `fast_tanh_pos_correct`, that the three functions return a double-double `h + l` with `h = RN(h + l)`, an `e` in [-1021, 1023] ([-1021, 1022] for `fast_tanh_pos`) and an `err` = 2^-p with p in [55, 105] that bounds the distance of `h + l` from f(t) 2^-e, and `fast_sinh_pos_fma` and the two others, that they return the same with and without an FMA, at every finite t >= 0 for both kinds of theorem. `FastHypPos.v` and `FastTanhPos.v` also export lemmas that `Assumptions.v` does not print, for the literals and the steps (`lit_26`, `rn_err`, `quot_split`, `th_mid_core` and the others).
- [`FastHyp.v`](theories/FastHyp.v): `fast_sinh_dir_correct` and `fast_sinh_dir_fma` above, and the same of `fast_sinh_near`, `fast_cosh_dir`, `fast_cosh_near`, `fast_tanh_dir` and `fast_tanh_near`, from the bounds of the three files before it, of `FastExpEdge.v` above 709, and the tests of `FastRoundErr.v` and `FastRoundWide.v`.
- [`RoundOpp.v`](theories/RoundOpp.v): `mpfr_round_down_opp` and `pown_rnd_opp`, lemmas, none of which `Assumptions.v` prints, moved out of `DyPown.v`, which restates them, so that `FastSign.v` does not depend on `DyPown.v`.
- [`FastSign.v`](theories/FastSign.v): `round_dir_signed_correct` above and `round_near_signed_correct`, the rounding tests of `FastRound.v` for an `h` of either sign.
- [`FastLogTables.v`](theories/FastLogTables.v): `ltbl_correct` above, by `vm_compute` on integers, and the bounds of the constants of the fast path of the logarithms: `ln2h_exact` and `lg2h_exact`, that `LN2H` and `LG2H` are integers below 2^42 times a power of 2, so that `e LN2H` and `e LG2H` are exact for |e| < 2^11, `ln2_split_correct` and `lg2_split_correct`, that `LN2H + LN2L` and `LG2` are ln 2 and log10(2) within 2^-100, and `c2_correct` and `c10_correct`, that `C2` and `C10` are 1/ln 2 and 1/ln 10 within 2^-104.
- [`FastLogTaylor.v`](theories/FastLogTaylor.v): `log1p_taylor` and `log1p_shift` above, the first from the derivative of its remainder, in which the derivative of the partial sum is a geometric sum, as for `atanh_sum_bounds`.
- [`FastLogHorner.v`](theories/FastLogHorner.v) and [`FastLogPoly.v`](theories/FastLogPoly.v): `log1p_horner_correct`, the round-off of Horner's scheme for the terms of degree 3 to 8, and `log1p_poly_correct` and `log1p_poly_fma`, the bound of `log1p_poly` and that it returns the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastLogReduce.v`](theories/FastLogReduce.v): `ln_split_correct` and `ln_z_exact` above, and `ln_z_fma`, that `z1` and `z2` are the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastLogCore.v`](theories/FastLogCore.v): `ln_core_correct` above, from the four files before it, and `ln_core_fma`, that `ln_core` returns the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastLogNorm.v`](theories/FastLogNorm.v): `normalize_correct` and `ln_f64_ne1` above (this `normalize_correct` is that of the `normalize` of `src/dyadic/log.rs`, which takes an `h` of either sign, not that of `FastRoundErr.v`).
- [`FastLogFin.v`](theories/FastLogFin.v) and [`FastLogByProd.v`](theories/FastLogByProd.v): lemmas, none of which `Assumptions.v` prints, for the last Fast2Sum and the normalisation shared by `fast_ln` and `fast_log_by`, and for the product of `ah + al` by `c` in `fast_log_by`, with and without an FMA.
- [`FastLogLn.v`](theories/FastLogLn.v) and [`FastLogBy.v`](theories/FastLogBy.v): `fast_ln_correct`, `fast_log2_correct` and `fast_log10_correct` above.
- [`FastLog.v`](theories/FastLog.v): `fast_ln_dir_correct`, `fast_ln_near_correct` and `fast_ln_dir_fma` above, the same for `log2` and `log10`, `fast_ln_near_fma` and the others for rounding to nearest, and `fast_ln_one` and `fast_log2_one`, that the fast paths of `ln` and `log2` rounded in a direction return `None` at 1.
- [`FastTrigTables.v`](theories/FastTrigTables.v): `sc_correct` above, and `fast_trig_consts`: `INV` times π/4096 is within 2^-52 of 1, and `U1 + U2 + U3` within 2^-119 of π/4096.
- [`FastTrigReduce.v`](theories/FastTrigReduce.v): `fast_reduce_correct` above.
- [`FastTrigBigInt.v`](theories/FastTrigBigInt.v): `inv2pi_bounds` above, and lemmas, none of which `Assumptions.v` prints, for the integers of `fast_reduce_big`: `big_w_window`, that for each k in [28, 1035] its three limbs `w(j)` are the 192 bits of `INV2PI` from bit k from the top, by `vm_compute`; `big_frac`, that m 2^e / (π/4096) is m times those bits over 2^179 up to a multiple of 8192 and a term in [0, m 2^-179]; `big_prod`, that the three products give the top 128 bits of that product modulo 2^192; and `big_split`, that the shifts split it into n and the two parts of the fraction.
- [`FastTrigBigMul.v`](theories/FastTrigBigMul.v), [`FastTrigBigDec.v`](theories/FastTrigBigDec.v) and [`FastTrigBigSum.v`](theories/FastTrigBigSum.v): lemmas, none of which `Assumptions.v` prints, for the floats of `fast_reduce_big`: `big_mul_correct`, that its product of `fh + fl` by `U1 + U2` and `U3` is (`fh` + `fl`) π/4096 within 2^-100 for |`fh`| <= 1/2 and |`fl`| <= 2^-53 where |`rh`| >= 2^-65, and `big_mul_fma`, the same with and without an FMA; `big_decode`, that x is finite with |x| >= 2^16 where the test of its exponent passes, and is ±m 2^e for the m and e read from its bits; `big_fh` and `big_fl`, that `fh` and `fl` are their integers times 2^-53 and 2^-106, not rounded; `big_guard`, that the test |`rh`| >= 2^-65 holds of the real `rh` where it passes; `big_int`, that n is N mod 8192 for an integer N and `fh + fl` is the fraction up to less than 2^-105; `big_err`, the bounds on x - N π/4096 from those, for either sign of x; and `big_neg_mod`, that (-n) & 8191 is -N mod 8192.
- [`FastTrigBig.v`](theories/FastTrigBig.v): `fast_reduce_big_correct` above, and `fast_reduce_big_fma`, that `fast_reduce_big` returns the same with and without an FMA, and two lemmas that `Assumptions.v` does not print: `fast_reduce_any_correct`, that `fast_reduce_any`, which `fast_sin`, `fast_cos` and `fast_tan` call, gives the bounds of `fast_reduce_correct` on `rh`, `rl`, `rr` and x - N π/4096 at an N with N - n a multiple of 8192, and n = 0 only from `fast_reduce`, and `fast_reduce_any_fma`, that it returns the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastTrigSC.v`](theories/FastTrigSC.v): `fast_sc_correct` above, and `fast_sc_fma`, that `fast_sc(j)` returns the same with and without an FMA for j in [1, 2047], by `reflexivity`, as `DyadicTrig.fast_sc` takes the argument `fma` as the other steps do and does not read it, and a lemma that `Assumptions.v` does not print, `fast_sc_row`, that reading the row at j up to 1024 and at 2048 - j above it and picking its halves by an index, 2 above 1024 and 0 up to it, gives the row at j up to 1024 and the row at 2048 - j with its halves swapped above it.
- [`FastTrigGappa.v`](theories/FastTrigGappa.v): `poly_cm_gappa` and `poly_s3_gappa` above, that `cm` and `s3` of `fast_eval` are finite and as close to the polynomials with 1/24, 1/120 and 1/6 as the hand proofs `poly_cm` and `poly_s3` of `FastTrigEval.v` show at |`rr`| <= 3836e-7, from `cm_gappa` and `s3_gappa`, which the `gappa` tactic proves: with each operation rounded to nearest (`RN` of `TwoSum.v`), the two polynomials are within 21 2^-80 and 5 2^-90 of those with 1/24, 1/120 and 1/6 for |r| <= 6436 2^-24, so that the bounds include the errors of the constants `C4`, `S5` and `S3`. `fast_sin0_correct`, `fast_cos0_correct`, `band_sin_correct` and `band_cos_correct` still use `poly_cm` and `poly_s3`.
- [`FastTrigEval.v`](theories/FastTrigEval.v): `fast_eval_correct`, `fast_sin0_correct` and `fast_cos0_correct` above, and `fast_eval_fma`, that `fast_eval` returns the same with and without an FMA where `ch` lies in [2^-12, 1] and `|rh|` in [2^-65, 1].
- [`FastTrigDiv.v`](theories/FastTrigDiv.v): `fast_div_correct` above, for a numerator of magnitude in [2^-28, 1] and a denominator in [2^-13, 1], each within 2^-71 of the double-double that `fast_div` takes, relative, and `fast_div_fma`, that `fast_div` returns the same with and without an FMA where `|nh|` lies in [2^-29, 2] and `dh` in [2^-14, 2].
- [`FastTrigRound.v`](theories/FastTrigRound.v): `fast_round_dir_correct` and `fast_round_near_correct` above, from `unscale`, a lemma that `Assumptions.v` does not print, that the scaling of `fast_unscale` gives a pair within 2^-68 of the scaled result and an exponent, as the tests of `FastSign.v` take them, and from those tests.
- [`FastTrigBandRed.v`](theories/FastTrigBandRed.v): `band_consts`, `fast_band_reduce_correct` and `fast_band_reduce_fma` above, the second from `band_consts`, `fast_reduce_correct` and `km_distance`.
- [`FastTrigBandInt.v`](theories/FastTrigBandInt.v): lemmas, none of which `Assumptions.v` prints, for the integers of `fast_reduce_band_big`: `band_split`, that the shifts read, from the 192 bits R of the product of `fast_reduce_big`, n as `fast_reduce_big` reads it, the 64 bits `a` below n, as a signed integer, and the 64 bits `lo` below `a`, with n = N0 mod 8192 and R = N0 2^179 + (`a` 2^64 + `lo`) 2^51 + (R mod 2^51) for N0 = R 2^-179 + 1/2 rounded down; `band_int` above, from `band_split` and `big_w_window`, `big_frac` and `big_prod`; `band_norm` above, that where |`a`| >= 2^13 the shift s is at most 50 and the integers `zh` and `zl` of `fh` and `fl` give `zh` 2^53 + `zl` = A 2^s / 2^22 rounded down, with 2^51 <= |`zh`| <= 2^52 and 0 <= `zl` < 2^53.
- [`FastTrigBandMul.v`](theories/FastTrigBandMul.v): lemmas, none of which `Assumptions.v` prints, for the floats of `fast_reduce_band_big`: `band_pow`, that the `f64` of bits E << 52 is 2^(E - 1023) for E in [1, 2046]; `band_scale`, that its product by an integer z with |z| < 2^53, as an `f64`, is exact for E in [1, 1023]; `band_fh` and `band_fl`, that `fh` and `fl` are `zh` 2^(-53 - s) and `zl` 2^(-106 - s), not rounded, for s in [0, 50]; `band_f_bounds`, that S/8 <= |`fh`| <= S/2 and |`fl`| <= 2^-53 S for S = 2^-s and either sign of `fh` and `fl`, where 2^51 - 1 <= |`zh`| <= 2^52 and 0 <= `zl` < 2^53; `band_guard`, that |`fh`| <= 1/2 and the rounded product of `fh` by `uh` = RN(`U1` + `U2`) is at least 2^-15 S and 2^-65 in magnitude, where S/8 <= |`fh`| <= S/2 for an S in [2^-50, 1]; `band_mul_rel`, that `fast_mul` of `fh + fl` by `uh` and `U3` gives `rh + rl` within 2^-110 S of (`fh` + `fl`) π/4096, with |`rh`| >= 2^-15 S and |`rl`| <= 2^-24 |`rh`|, where moreover |`fl`| <= 2^-53 S; and `band_mul_fma`, that `fast_mul` returns the same with and without an FMA where S/8 <= |`fh`| <= S/2.
- [`FastTrigBandBig.v`](theories/FastTrigBandBig.v): `fast_reduce_band_big_correct` and `fast_reduce_band_big_fma` above, from the lemmas of `FastTrigBandInt.v` and `FastTrigBandMul.v`, `big_decode`, `big_neg_mod` and `km_distance`.
- [`FastTrigBandDiv.v`](theories/FastTrigBandDiv.v): `band_div_correct` and `band_div_fma` above, which, unlike `fast_div_correct` and `fast_div_fma`, take a numerator and a denominator of either sign down to 2^-64 in magnitude (2^-65 for `band_div_fma`).
- [`FastTrigBandEval.v`](theories/FastTrigBandEval.v): `band_sin_correct`, `band_cos_correct`, `band_eval_correct`, `band_tan_correct` and `band_tan_fma` above, the last two from `band_div_correct` and `band_div_fma`.
- [`FastTrigCore.v`](theories/FastTrigCore.v): `fast_sin_correct`, `fast_cos_correct` and `fast_tan_correct` above, from the theorems of `FastTrigTables.v`, `FastTrigSC.v`, `FastTrigEval.v` and `FastTrigDiv.v`, from `fast_reduce_any_correct` of `FastTrigBig.v`, which `quad_setup` turns into the bounds at ρ = x - 2π j - n π/4096 for an integer j, and from `quad_correct`, the bound of 2^-71 on sin(x + s π/2), relative, from which the sign and the choice between sin and cos by q mod 4 follow, and, in the band, from `fast_band_reduce_correct`, `fast_reduce_band_big_correct`, `band_eval_correct` and `band_tan_correct`, which give that bound for `fast_band` and `fast_band_big` (`band_quad`, which `quad_correct` uses below 2^16, and `band_big_quad`) and that of 3 2^-71 on tan x for `fast_band_tan` (`band_tan_out`), and `fast_sin_fma`, `fast_cos_fma` and `fast_tan_fma`, that the three return the same with and without an FMA at arguments of magnitude at least 2^-27, in the band from `fast_band_reduce_fma`, `fast_reduce_band_big_fma` and `band_tan_fma`.
- [`FastTrigOne.v`](theories/FastTrigOne.v): `sin_not_one`, `cos_not_one` and `fast_one_dir_correct` above, the first two from `km_distance`.
- [`FastTrig.v`](theories/FastTrig.v): `fast_sin_dir_correct` and `fast_sin_dir_fma` above, `fast_sin_near_correct` and `fast_sin_near_fma`, the same for nearest, and the same four for cos and tan, from the bounds of `FastTrigCore.v` and the tests of `FastTrigRound.v` and, for `fast_sin_dir` and `fast_cos_dir`, the three theorems of `FastTrigOne.v`.
- [`FastPowTaylor.v`](theories/FastPowTaylor.v): `log1p_shift2` above.
- [`FastPowHorner.v`](theories/FastPowHorner.v): `log1p_horner4_correct` above.
- [`FastPowCube.v`](theories/FastPowCube.v): `log1p_cube_correct` above, and `log1p_cube_fma`, that the errors of its three products are the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastPowPoly.v`](theories/FastPowPoly.v): `log1p_poly_acc_correct` above, from the three files before it, and `log1p_poly_acc_fma`, that `log1p_poly_acc` returns the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastPowCore.v`](theories/FastPowCore.v): `ln_core_acc_correct` above, and `ln_core_acc_fma`, that `ln_core_acc` returns the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastPowLn.v`](theories/FastPowLn.v): `ln_acc_correct` above, and `ln_acc_fma`, that `ln_acc` returns the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastPowMul.v`](theories/FastPowMul.v): `fast_pow_mul`, `fast_pow_mul_wide` and `fast_pow_mul_one` above.
- [`FastPowRules.v`](theories/FastPowRules.v): `fast_pow_overflow` above and `fast_pow_underflow`, the rules on `th` of `fast_pow_wide_dir` and `fast_pow_wide_near`.
- [`FastPow.v`](theories/FastPow.v): `fast_pow_correct`, `fast_pow_edge_correct`, `fast_pow_dir_correct`, `fast_pow_near_correct`, `fast_pow_dir_fma` and `fast_pow_near_fma` above, from `fast_exp_correct`, `fast_exp_edge_correct`, `fast_pow_mul`, `fast_pow_mul_wide`, the rules of `FastPowRules.v` and the tests of `FastRoundErr.v` and `FastRoundWideErr.v`.
- [`FastDdSqrt.v`](theories/FastDdSqrt.v): `dd_sqrt_correct` above, with the exact residual of the square root rounded to nearest, and `dd_sqrt_fma`, that `dd_sqrt` returns the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastLogDd.v`](theories/FastLogDd.v): `fast_ln_dd_correct` above, and `fast_ln_dd_fma`, that `fast_ln_dd` returns the same with and without an FMA.
- [`AsinhTaylor.v`](theories/AsinhTaylor.v): `asinh_taylor9` and `asinh_taylor15` above, the remainders of the odd Taylor polynomials of degrees 9 and 15 of asinh on [0, 1/16], by the mean value theorem (`log1p_dominate` of `FastLogTaylor.v`): the derivative of asinh at c is 1/√(1 + c^2), and that of the polynomial is the binomial series of (1 + v)^(-1/2) at v = c^2, cut after v^4 or v^7, and squaring turns the bound on their difference into polynomial inequalities on [0, 1/256], which `lra` proves, and `nra` for one of those of degree 15. Its other lemmas, such as `asinh_taylor_gen`, the same for a degree and a bound on the derivative given as hypotheses, are steps, and `Assumptions.v` prints none of them.
- [`FastAhypOdd.v`](theories/FastAhypOdd.v): `asinh_odd_correct` and `atanh_odd_correct` above, from a bound of `odd_series` for any coefficients with 1/8 <= |c0| <= 1/2 and |c1|, |c2|, |c3| <= 1/4, `asinh_taylor9` and `atanh_sum_bounds`.
- [`FastAhypNear1Poly.v`](theories/FastAhypNear1Poly.v) and [`FastAhypNear1Sum.v`](theories/FastAhypNear1Sum.v): the steps of `fast_acosh_near1` above. `near1_u`: u = (x - 1)/2 exactly, with 2^-53 <= u < 2^-9; `near1_w`: mh and wl, with wl - mh within 2^-71 of w; `near1_sum`: `h + l` within 2^-70 sh of (sh + sl)(1 - mh + wl); and `near1_ml_fma` and `near1_pe_fma`, that the two errors of products by `two_prod_err` are the same with and without an FMA. `Assumptions.v` prints none of them, as `acosh_near1_correct` and `acosh_near1_fma` use them all.
- [`FastAhypNear1.v`](theories/FastAhypNear1.v): `acosh_asinh_half`, `acosh_near1_correct` and `acosh_near1_fma` above, from the two files before it, `dd_sqrt_correct` and `asinh_taylor15`.
- [`FastAhypAsinh.v`](theories/FastAhypAsinh.v): `fast_asinh_pos_correct` above, with ln 2 + ln(t + RN(1/(4t))) within 2^-80 of asinh t, relative, from 2^26 on, and `fast_asinh_pos_fma`, that `fast_asinh_pos` returns the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastAhypAcosh.v`](theories/FastAhypAcosh.v): `fast_acosh_correct` above, with ln 2 + ln(x + RN(-1/(4x))) within 2^-80 of acosh x, relative, from 2^26 on, and `fast_acosh_fma`, that `fast_acosh` returns the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastAhypAtanh.v`](theories/FastAhypAtanh.v): `fast_atanh_pos_correct` above, and `fast_atanh_pos_fma`, that `fast_atanh_pos` returns the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastAhyp.v`](theories/FastAhyp.v): `fast_asinh_correct` and `fast_atanh_correct`, the signed results of `fast_asinh` and `fast_atanh`, `fast_asinh_dir_correct`, `fast_asinh_near_correct`, `fast_asinh_dir_fma` and `fast_asinh_near_fma` above and the same for `acosh` and `atanh`, from the tests of `FastSign.v` and `FastRoundErr.v`, and `fast_acosh_one`.
- [`FastAtanTables.v`](theories/FastAtanTables.v): `fast_atan_table_correct`, `fast_pio2_correct` and `fast_atan_consts` above, by `vm_compute` on integers; `fast_atan_consts` shows that `A3` is 1/3 within 2^-109 in double-double and that `A5` to `A11` are 1/5 to 1/11 rounded to nearest.
- [`FastAtanIdent.v`](theories/FastAtanIdent.v): `atan_sub_table` above, from Stdlib's `Machin.atan_sub_correct`, and `atan_lipschitz`, `atan_upper` and `atan_lower`: |atan a - atan b| <= |a - b|, atan q <= q for q >= 0 and q/2 <= atan q for 0 <= q <= 2, which turn absolute errors of an argument into relative errors of atan. `Assumptions.v` prints none of them.
- [`FastAtanPoly.v`](theories/FastAtanPoly.v): `fast_atan_poly_correct` above, and `fast_atan_poly_fma`, that `fast_atan_poly` returns the same with and without an FMA on the same range of u.
- [`FastAtanCore.v`](theories/FastAtanCore.v): `fast_atan_core_correct` above, from the three files before it and `dd_div_correct`, and `fast_atan_core_fma`, that `fast_atan_core` returns the same with and without an FMA on the same range of t, and `core_kf`, `core_index` and `core_bits`, lemmas that `Assumptions.v` does not print, that `kf` is the integer i nearest 64 th, with 0 <= i <= 64, that c = i/64 exactly, and that the index `ks.to_bits() as i32 as i64` of `fast_atan_core` and `fast_atan1` is i (from `shifter_bits`).
- [`FastAtan1Poly.v`](theories/FastAtan1Poly.v): `poly9_err`, a lemma that `Assumptions.v` does not print, that x - `poly9 x`, the odd Taylor polynomial of atan x to degree 9 that `fast_atan1` evaluates in plain `f64`, with `poly9 x` its rounded x^3 term, is within 2^-52 |x|^3 of atan x for 2^-82 <= |x| <= 2^-7.
- [`FastAtan1Rem.v`](theories/FastAtan1Rem.v): `atan1_rem`, a lemma that `Assumptions.v` does not print, that for `f64`s n and d with 2^-80 <= |n| <= 4 and 1/2 <= d <= 2^54, the remainder n - q d of q = RN(n / d), computed as `(n - p) - pe` from the product q d = p + pe of `two_prod_err`, is exact, from `div_remainder` and `div_dekker_remainder`, and that pe is the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastAtan1Mid.v`](theories/FastAtan1Mid.v): `quot_mid`, a lemma that `Assumptions.v` does not print, that q = RN(n / d) for `f64`s n and d in [1, 2) is the rounding of q + RN(n / d - q), with which `fast_quot_correct` shows that the high part of `fast_quot` is the rounding of its sum.
- [`FastAtan1Div.v`](theories/FastAtan1Div.v): `atan1_div` and `atan1_div_fma`, lemmas that `Assumptions.v` does not print, that the quotient u = uh + ul that `fast_atan1` takes, with uh = RN(nh / dh) and ul from the remainder nh - uh dh, plus nl and minus uh dl, divided by dh, has 2^-82 <= |uh| <= 2^-7 and is within 2^-97 |n| of n / D for any D >= 1 + 2^-14 within 2^-103 of d, for the n = nh + nl and d = dh + dl of the core, and that the error of the product uh dh is the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastAtan1Quot.v`](theories/FastAtan1Quot.v): `fast_quot_correct` and `fast_recip_correct` above, from `atan1_rem` and `quot_mid`, and `fast_quot_fma` and `fast_recip_fma`, that `fast_quot` and `fast_recip` return the same with and without an FMA on the same ranges.
- [`FastAtan1.v`](theories/FastAtan1.v): `fast_atan1_correct` above, from `FastAtan1Poly.v`, `FastAtan1Div.v` and the lemmas `core_index`, `core_bits`, `core_num` and `core_den` of `FastAtanCore.v`, and `fast_atan1_fma`, that `fast_atan1` returns the same with and without an FMA on the same range of t, and `atan1_abs_err` and `atan1_rel_err`, lemmas that `Assumptions.v` does not print, that a value within 2^-64 of atan q', relative, is within 2^-64 of atan q, absolute, for q' in [0, 129/128] and |q' - q| <= 2^-90, and within 2^-64 + 2^-84 of it, relative, for 0 < q <= 2 and |q' - q| <= 2^-96 q.
- [`FastAtanAsm.v`](theories/FastAtanAsm.v): `pio2_add_correct`, `split_correct` and `octant_correct` above, lemmas that `Assumptions.v` does not print, and `octant_bound` above, a lemma that it prints.
- [`FastAtanQuot.v`](theories/FastAtanQuot.v): lemmas, none of which `Assumptions.v` prints, for the inputs of the core that `fast_atan_pos` and `fast_atan2_pos` build: the quotient of two significands in [1, 2) by `dd_div` lies in [1/2, 2) and within 2^-98 of theirs, relative (`quot_facts`), a power of 2 scales it exactly into the core's range (`scaled_input`), and 1/t from `dd_div`, for 1 < t < 2^54, is an input of the core (`big_input`).
- [`FastAtan.v`](theories/FastAtan.v): `fast_atan_pos_correct`, `fast_atan2_pos_correct`, `fast_atan_dir_correct` and `fast_atan_dir_fma` above, `fast_atan_pos_fma` and `fast_atan2_pos_fma`, that these two functions return the same with and without an FMA, `fast_atan_near_correct` and `fast_atan_near_fma`, the same for nearest, the same four for `atan2`, and `fast_atan_pos1_correct` and `fast_atan2_pos1_correct` above and `fast_atan_pos1_fma` and `fast_atan2_pos1_fma`, the same for the first stages `fast_atan_pos1` and `fast_atan2_pos1`, from the theorems of `FastAtanCore.v`, `FastAtan1.v`, `FastAtan1Quot.v`, `FastAtanAsm.v` and `FastAtanQuot.v` and the tests of `FastRoundErr.v`.
- [`FastAsinReal.v`](theories/FastAsinReal.v), [`FastAsinAngle.v`](theories/FastAsinAngle.v) and [`FastAsinQuot.v`](theories/FastAsinQuot.v): lemmas, none of which `Assumptions.v` prints: bounds of quotients whose numerator or denominator errs relatively (`div_num`, `div_den`), `asin_q` above, and `asin_quot`, that the quotient that `fast_asin_atan` takes by `dd_div`, t / r up to the split 181/256 and r / t above it, is within 2^-96 of that of `asin_q`, relative, and an input of the core.
- [`FastAsinSqrt.v`](theories/FastAsinSqrt.v): `fast_sqrt1m_correct` above, from `dd_sqrt_correct`, and `sqrt1m_fma` and `sqrt1m_facts`, that `fast_sqrt1m` returns the same with and without an FMA and that its result lies in the range that `dd_div` takes, for t in [2^-27, 1). `Assumptions.v` prints none of them.
- [`FastAsin.v`](theories/FastAsin.v): `fast_asin_pos_correct`, `fast_acos_val_correct` and `fast_acos_dir_correct` above, `fast_asin_pos_fma` and `fast_acos_val_fma`, that these two functions return the same with and without an FMA, the dir and near theorems of `asin` and `acos` and their forms with an FMA, as in `FastAtan.v`, and `fast_asin_pos1_correct` and `fast_acos_val1_correct` above and `fast_asin_pos1_fma` and `fast_acos_val1_fma`, the same for the first stages `fast_asin_pos1` and `fast_acos_val1`, from `fast_asin_atan_correct` and `fast_asin_atan1_correct`, that the angles that `fast_asin_atan` and `fast_asin_atan1` compute are within 2^-73 and 2^-64 + 2^-84 of asin t or acos t, relative, and the tests of `FastRoundErr.v`.
- [`DyLogRules.v`](theories/DyLogRules.v): `atanh_tail` and `atanh_horner`: for `0 <= x <= 1/2`, the remainder of the series of atanh after `k` terms is `x^(2k+1)` times a number in `[0, 2]`, by `atanh_sum_bounds`, and a step of Horner's scheme from one for `k + 1` gives one for `k`. `ln_atanh` and `ln_atanh_inv`: `ln m = 2 atanh((m - 1) / (m + 1))` and `-ln m = 2 atanh((1 - m) / (m + 1))` for `m > 0`. `Ratanh_odd` and `arcsinh_odd`. `arcsinh_ln`, `arcsinh_atanh`, `acosh_ln`, `acosh_atanh` and `atanh_ln`: asinh, acosh and atanh as `ln` or `atanh` of the expressions that the evaluations take. `ln_le_sub`: `ln y <= ln x + (y - x) / x` for `0 < x <= y`. `log10_pow10_rule` and `log_rules_finite_pos`: the rule of log10 at `1e0` to `1e22`, and that `log_rules` settles no positive finite argument.
- [`DyLogTiny.v`](theories/DyLogTiny.v): `asinh_tiny_rule` and `atanh_tiny_rule`, the rules of asinh and atanh for `|x| < 2^-26`: asinh x lies strictly between `x` and the next `f64` toward 0, and atanh x strictly between `x` and the next `f64` away from 0, for `x` other than 0 (`asinh_tiny_between`, `atanh_tiny_real` and `atanh_tiny_succ`).
- [`DyLogCore.v`](theories/DyLogCore.v): `atanh_series_correct`: `atanh_series`, where it returns, encloses atanh of every point of its argument, which its assertion keeps in `[0, 1/2]`, by Horner's scheme from `[0, 2]` after `terms n` terms, at most 821, and bounds the exponents of the result by 2^43 where those of its argument are at most 2^30. `atanh_series_gen`: the same, with the bound `2^11 (E + 2^15)` for any bound `E` up to 2^30 on the exponents of the argument, which `reduce_correct` uses to bound the exponents of its enclosure by 2^29. `reduce_correct`: where `reduce` returns at a `Dyadic` `d`, `d` is positive, and `reduce` gives an `e` with `|e| <= 2048` and an enclosure of `ln d - e ln 2`, negated if its flag `less` is set. `ln_dy_correct` and `ln_encl_correct`: `ln_dy` encloses the logarithm of a positive `Dyadic`, and `ln_encl` that of every point of an enclosure of positive numbers, with the bound of `ln_le_sub`.
- [`DyLog.v`](theories/DyLog.v) and [`DyLogHyp.v`](theories/DyLogHyp.v): the theorems above. `ev_ln_correct`, `ev_log2_correct`, `ev_log10_correct`, `ev_asinh_correct`, `ev_acosh_correct` and `ev_atanh_correct`: each evaluation, where it returns, encloses the result at its level, as `driver_post` asks, at every argument for the first four, and at a finite argument in its domain for `ev_acosh_correct` and `ev_atanh_correct`. `sqrt_step`, in `DyLogHyp.v`: `encl_sqrt_correct` with a bound on the exponents of the result, as the steps of `DyGrow.v` give for the other operations.
