- [`AtanSeries.v`](theories/AtanSeries.v): `atan_tail_bounds`: for every real `x`, the remainder of the series of atan after `n` terms, times `(-1)^n`, is `x^(2n+1)` times a number in `[0, 1/(2n+1)]`, by the mean value theorem. `atan_horner`: if `T x^(2k+3)` is that remainder after `k + 1` terms, then `(1/(2k+1) - x^2 T) x^(2k+1)` is the one after `k`, a step of Horner's scheme in `x^2`. `atan_halve`: `atan u = 2 atan(u / (1 + √(1 + u^2)))` for every real `u`.
- [`AtanIdent.v`](theories/AtanIdent.v): `atan_quarter_plus` and `atan_quarter_minus` above, for `w > -1`, and `atan_half_inv`, for `w > 0`. `asin_as_atan` and `acos_as_atan`: `asin t = atan(t / √((1 - t)(1 + t)))` for `0 <= t < 1`, and `acos t = atan(√((1 - t)(1 + t)) / t)` for `0 < t <= 1`. `asin_plus_acos`: `asin t + acos t = π/2` on `[-1, 1]`. `Ratan2_opp_y`: `Ratan2 (-y) x = -Ratan2 y x` for `y <> 0`.
- [`DyConstPi.v`](theories/DyConstPi.v): `pi_correct` above. `azsum_bounds`: the sum `azsum` of the terms of the series of `2^P atan(1/q)`, each floored in integer fixed point, after `n` terms with `2^P < q^(2n+1)`, is within `n + 1` of `2^P atan(1/q)`. `DyConstPi.PI_bounds`: the table is `floor(π * 2^4222)`, from `azsum_bounds` at 4,288 bits with 925 terms for `q = 5` and 272 for `q = 239`, which `vm_compute` evaluates. `dp_levels_ok_true`: the check of `DyConst.v` that `split` gives a normalized `h` and an enclosure of the rest, at each of the 64 levels for this table, which `vm_compute` evaluates.
- [`KahanMcDonald.v`](theories/KahanMcDonald.v): `km_distance`, `km_all_true` and `km_unimodular` above. `km_core`: the bound at `m 2^e` for `0 < m < 2^53` and `-52 <= e <= 971`, from `km_cert_sound`, which turns the check of one exponent into the bound. `km_t_bounds`: the bracket of `2^N/π` from `PI_bounds`, of which each exponent takes a window. `km_t_bounds_gen`, of which `km_t_bounds` is an instance, gives that bracket for any N >= 0, and `inv2pi_bounds` of `FastTrigBigInt.v` takes it at N = 1215.
- [`AtanExactDiv.v`](theories/AtanExactDiv.v): `exact_div_correct` and `exact_div_fma` above, from `two_prod_err_correct` and `two_prod_err_fma_eq`. `ge_mul_false` and `ge_false`: the real bounds `|y| c < x` and `x < c` that `ge (mul (fabs y) c) x = false` and `ge x c = false` give, for finite `y`, `x` and `c`, and `c > 0` for the first. `two_25_R` and the others: the values of the constants of `tiny_quotient`, `midpoint_quotient` and `exact_div`.
- [`DyAtanTiny.v`](theories/DyAtanTiny.v): `atan_tiny_rule` and `asin_tiny_rule`, the rules of atan for `|x| < 2^-26` and of asin for `|x| < 2^-27`: atan x lies strictly between `x` and the next `f64` toward 0, and asin x strictly between `x` and the next `f64` away from 0, for `x` other than 0 (`atan_tiny_between` and `asin_tiny_succ`). `tiny_quotient_correct`: for `x > 0`, `tiny_quotient y x` returns an `f64` only if it is `y / x` exactly and of magnitude below 2^-26. `tiny_quotient_dyadic_correct`: the same of `tiny_quotient_dyadic`, its path for `x >= 2^996`.
- [`DyAtanCore.v`](theories/DyAtanCore.v): `DyAtanCore.series_correct`, `atan_series_correct`, `atan_encl_correct` and `asin_parts_correct` above. `ac_terms`: `0 <= terms n z <= 898` at every level `n`.
- [`AtanPiConst.v`](theories/AtanPiConst.v): `pi_bounds_correct`: for a finite `k`, each pair of bits that `DyadicAtan.pi_bounds k` gives is two adjacent positive `f64`s, the second `Bsucc` of the first, with `k π` strictly between them and above the first by at most 2^-54 times the first, from `PI_Q` in [`PiBounds.v`](theories/PiBounds.v) and `vm_compute` on the bits. `pi_const_rounds`: for a finite `c` at which `pi_bounds |c|` gives a pair, that pair with the sign of `c` gives `c π` rounded toward -∞ and toward +∞, as `rounds` says, and `pi_const_near`: its first `f64` with the sign of `c` is `c π` rounded to nearest, as `near_round` says.
- [`DyAtan.v`](theories/DyAtan.v) and [`DyAsin.v`](theories/DyAsin.v): the theorems above. `ev_atan_correct`, `ev_atan2_correct`, `ev_asin_correct` and `ev_acos_correct`: each evaluation, where it returns, encloses the result at its level, as `driver_post` asks, at a finite argument, with `y` and `x` other than 0 for `ev_atan2_correct` and `x` in `[-1, 1]` for the last two. `pi_times_post`: `pi_times_with z c up`, where it returns, gives `c π`, for a finite `c`, the property `post` of every driver `z` with `driver_post z post`, for a `post` that the correct rounding also has (`rounds_post post`): at `|c|` = 1/4, 1/2, 3/4 and 1 it returns the constant of `pi_bounds`, with the sign of `c`, that rounds `c π` in its direction (`pi_const_rounds`), and at any other `c` what `z` returns, so that with `ziv_strict` it rounds `c π` toward +∞ if `up` and toward -∞ otherwise (`pi_times_correct`), and `pi_times c up` returns the same value there (`pi_times_strict`). `atan_huge_post` above, for a property `post` that the correct rounding has (`rounds_post`), as its rule returns `pi_times` of ±1/2, and that carries over between two reals with no `f64` between them (`gap_post`), as the correct rounding does (`rounds_gap`, from `atn_round_gap`) and the side does (`side_gap`), with `atn_huge_between`: for `t >= 2^54`, atan t and π/2 lie strictly between the bounds of `Interval::FRAC_PI_2`, between which no `f64` lies (`atn_pi2_gap`).
- [`TrigSeries.v`](theories/TrigSeries.v): `trig_tail_bounds`: for `|a| <= 1`, the remainder of the series of sin a (if `odd`) or cos a after `k` terms is `T` times the next term, `trig_term odd a k`, for some `T` in `[0, 1]`, as the partial sums bracket the function for `0 <= a <= 1`, by `sin_bound` and `cos_bound` of Stdlib's `Rtrigo1.v` from two terms on and by `0 <= sin a <= a` and `0 <= cos a <= 1` below two terms, and by parity for `a < 0`. `trig_horner`: if `T` is the factor of the remainder after `k + 1` terms, `1 - a^2 T / ((2k + 1 + o)(2k + 2 + o))`, with `o` 1 for sin and 0 for cos, is the one after `k`, a step of Horner's scheme in `a^2`. `cos_sqrt`: `cos a = √((1 - sin a)(1 + sin a))` for `|a| <= 1`. `sin_quad` and `tan_quad`: sin and tan of `k π/2 + ρ` and of `k π/2 - ρ`, for every integer `k`, are ±sin ρ or ±cos ρ, and ±sin ρ / cos ρ or ±cos ρ / sin ρ, by `k mod 4`, from `quad_shift`, which replaces `k` by `k mod 4`, and `quad_cases`.
- [`DyTrigTiny.v`](theories/DyTrigTiny.v): `sin_tiny_rule`, `cos_tiny_rule` and `tan_tiny_rule`, the rules of sin, cos and tan for `|x| < 2^-26`: for `x` other than 0, sin x lies strictly between `x` and the next `f64` toward 0 (`sin_tiny_between`), tan x strictly between `x` and the next `f64` away from 0 (`tan_tiny_succ`, from `x < tan x <= x + x^3/2` for `x > 0`, `tan_tiny_real`), and cos x strictly between `1 - 2^-53`, the `f64` below 1, and 1 (`cos_tiny_real`), from the brackets of sin and cos by their series to two and to three terms (`sin_bounds_3` and `cos_bounds_3`, which are `sin_bound` and `cos_bound` with `n = 0`).
- [`DyTrigSmall.v`](theories/DyTrigSmall.v): the helpers of the rules of sin, cos and tan for small arguments. `magnitude_normal`: the value of the bit pattern of a normal `f64`, from its biased exponent and its stored significand. `small_bits`: the pattern of `|x|` of a finite `x`, and its fields as the rules read them. `bits_pair`: the `f64`s of two consecutive patterns below that of +∞ are finite and adjacent. `bits_bracket` and `bits_bracket_opp`: a real strictly between them rounds toward -∞ to the first and toward +∞ to the second, and its opposite to their opposites the other way round. `cube_correct`: `cube m` is `m^3` split at bit 64, for `0 <= m < 2^53`.
- [`DyCosSmall.v`](theories/DyCosSmall.v): `cos_small_rule`, the rule of cos for `|x|` in [2^-26, 2^-14): where `cos_small` returns an `f64`, it is cos x rounded in its direction, from `1 - a^2/2 < cos a <= 1 - a^2/2 + a^4/24` for `0 < a < 2^-14` (`cos_small_real`, from `cos_bound` with `n = 1` and `cos_bounds_3`) and the exact square of the significand of `|x|`.
- [`DySinSmall.v`](theories/DySinSmall.v): `sin_small_rule`, the same for sin and `sin_small`, for `|x|` in [2^-26, 2^-13), from `a - a^3/6 < sin a <= a - a^3/6 + a^5/120` for `0 < a < 2^-13` (`sin_small_real`, from `sin_bound` with `n = 1` and `sin_bounds_3`), the cube of the significand of `|x|` (`cube_correct`) and the bounds on its quotients that `sin_small_int` gives.
- [`DyTanSmall.v`](theories/DyTanSmall.v): `tan_small_rule`, the same for tan and `tan_small`, for `|x|` in [2^-26, 2^-14), from `a + a^3/3 < tan a < a + a^3/3 + a^5/4` for `0 < a < 2^-14` (`tan_small_real`, from `sin_small_real` and `cos_small_real`), and `tan_small_bracket`, which places tan t strictly between `t + j g` and `t + (j + 1) g`, for `t = |x|`, its ulp `g` and the `j` that `tan_small` computes from the cube of its significand, where the first of the two tests of `tan_small` passes.
- [`DyTrigReduce.v`](theories/DyTrigReduce.v): `reduce_correct` above. `PI_top`: for `l <= 66`, the top `l` limbs of the table of π, as an integer `P`, satisfy `P 2^(1 - 64 l) <= π/2 < (P + 1) 2^(1 - 64 l)`, from `DyConstPi.PI_bounds`. `round_int`: `round` of the limbs of an integer `x` at an exponent `e`, with no sticky bit, is `x 2^e` rounded at the level in the direction asked, for `|e|` and a bit length of `x` of at most 2^14.
- [`DyTrigCore.v`](theories/DyTrigCore.v): `DyTrigCore.series_correct` and `sin_k_correct` above, with `tc_series_loop_correct` for the loop of `series`. `tc_terms`: `0 <= terms n z <= 389` at every level `n`.
- [`DyTrigSign.v`](theories/DyTrigSign.v): `reduce_pos` and `reduce_le` above, from `reduce_bounds`, which states both at once.
- [`DyTrig.v`](theories/DyTrig.v): the theorems above. `ev_sin_correct`, `ev_cos_correct` and `ev_tan_correct`: each evaluation, where it returns, encloses the result at its level, as `driver_post` asks, at a finite argument, with `t >= 0` for `ev_cos_correct`.
- [`DyTrigSeriesPos.v`](theories/DyTrigSeriesPos.v): `series_pos` and `series_sin_lt1` above, from `sp_loop`, the bounds of the loop of `series`, and `sp_terms`, that it takes at least one step.
- [`DyTrigZivSign.v`](theories/DyTrigZivSign.v): `ziv_sign` above. `ziv_bound`: `Dyadic.ziv` returns a bound of the enclosure at some level rounded in its direction, whether a test passes or not. `rnd_dn_neg` and `rnd_up_pos`: a real rounded toward -∞ is below 0 exactly where the real is, and one rounded toward +∞ above 0 exactly where the real is.
- [`DyTrigPos.v`](theories/DyTrigPos.v): `ev_sin_sign`, `ev_cos_sign`, `tan_div_pos`, `sin_dir_sign` and `cos_dir_sign` above.
- [`DyPowArith.v`](theories/DyPowArith.v): the arithmetic of the exact powers, on integers and reals, with no function of `Model.v`. `odd_pow2_inj`: an odd `m` times `2^e` determines `m` and `e`. `square_of_mul_square`: `n q^2 = r^2` with `q, r > 0` makes `n` the square of a positive integer, by Gauss's lemma on `q` and `r` divided by their gcd. `odd_pow_square` and `odd_pow_root`: `a^p = b^2`, and `a^p = b^(2^k)` with `k >= 0`, of `a, b > 0` and an odd `p > 0`, make `a` a square, and the `2^k`-th power of a positive integer. `Rpower_IZR_pow`, `Rpower_bpow_IZR` and `Rpower_dyadic_IZR`: `Rpower` at an integer `n` of a positive integer and of a positive integer times a power of 2, for `n >= 0`, and of a power of 2, for every `n`. `pow_dyadic_eq`: where `(mx 2^ex)^(p/2^k)`, for an odd `mx > 0`, `p > 0` and `k >= 0`, is a dyadic `mv 2^ev` with an odd `mv > 0`, `mx^p = mv^(2^k)` and `ex p = ev 2^k`. `pow_dyadic_neg`: where `(mx 2^ex)^(-p/2^k)` is such a dyadic, `mx = 1`. `pow_dyadic_sound`: `((mr 2^er)^(2^k))^(p/2^k) = mr^p 2^(er p)` for `mr > 0`, `p > 0` and `k >= 0`. `pow_dyadic_root`: `mx^p = mv^(2^k)` and `ex p = ev 2^k`, of `mx, mv > 0`, `p > 0` and `k >= 0`, with `p` odd unless `k = 0`, give an `r > 0` and an `er` with `mx = r^(2^k)`, `ex = er 2^k`, `mv = r^p` and `ev = er p`, by `odd_pow_root` and by Gauss's lemma for `2^k` dividing `ex`. `pow_dyadic_complete`: the three together: where `(mx 2^ex)^(±p/2^k)` is the dyadic `mv 2^ev`, with odd `mx, mv > 0`, `p > 0`, `k >= 0` and `p` odd unless `k = 0`, `mx 2^ex` is the `2^k`-th power of a dyadic `r 2^er` with `r > 0`, and `mv 2^ev` is `(r 2^er)^(±p)`, where `r = 1` for the negative exponent.
- [`DyPowExact.v`](theories/DyPowExact.v): `exact_sound` and `exact_complete`: for a finite `x > 0` and a finite `y` other than 0, where `exact x y` returns an `f64`, that `f64` is finite and is x^y, and for `x` other than 1, `exact` returns `v` wherever x^y is a finite `f64` `v`. `exact` writes `x = mx 2^ex` and `|y| = p / 2^k` with an odd `mx`, and with `p` the odd mantissa of `y` if `k > 0` (`pk_spec`), takes `k` square roots of `mx` and halves `ex` `k` times, raises the root to the power `p`, and builds the `f64` `q 2^e` (`exact_tail`, after its range check, `range_check`). `roots_loop_sound`: where the loop of square roots of an `mx > 0` returns `(mr, er)`, `mr > 0`, `mx = mr^(2^k)` and `ex = er 2^k`. `roots_loop_complete`: it returns `(r, er)` at `r^(2^k)` and `er 2^k` for `0 < r` and `r^(2^k) < 2^53`, as `sqrt` of an `f64` that holds the square of a positive integer below 2^53 is exact (`fsqrt_exact`), and the integers below 2^53 convert to and from `f64` exactly through `i64` (`of_u64_exact`, as `of_i64` and `of_u64` are the same function, and `to_i64_int` of `FastExpReduce.v`). `small_power_spec`: `small_power r p`, for `r >= 1` and `p >= 0`, returns `r^p` exactly when it is below 2^53. `exact_root`: where x^y is a finite `f64` `v`, `pow_dyadic_complete` writes `x` as the `2^k`-th power of a dyadic `r 2^er` and `v` as `(r 2^er)^(±p)`, with `r = 1` for `y < 0`. `exact_ey_small`: there, for `x` other than 1, `|y| = my 2^ey` with an odd `my` has `ey <= 11`, so the first check of `exact`, which gives up at `ey > 11`, passes every such argument.
- [`DyPowRules.v`](theories/DyPowRules.v): the rules of x^y before the loop. `odd_parts_spec`: for a finite `x` other than 0, `odd_parts x` is the odd `m < 2^53` and the `e >= -1074` of `|x| = m 2^e`, from the bits of `x` (`trailing_zeros_odd`). `expo_odd_parts`: for such an `x`, `expo x`, which reads the biased exponent `be` of a normal `x` as `be - 1023`, is `e + 63 - leading_zeros 64 m` for its odd parts `(m, e)`. `expo_spec` and `expo_range`: for such an `x`, `expo x` is the `g` of `2^g <= |x| < 2^(g+1)`, in [-1074, 1023]. `ln_expo_spec`: for a finite `x > 0` other than 1, `ln_expo x` is an `A` with `2^A <= |ln x| < 2^(A+3)`, from the exponent of `x` (`ln_far_bounds`) or, for `x` in [1/2, 2), from that of `x - 1`, with `|x - 1| / 2 <= |ln x| <= 2 |x - 1|` (`ln_near_bounds`). The rest is at a finite `x > 0` other than 1 and a finite `y` other than 0. `pow_rule_bounds`: `2^s <= |y ln x| < 2^(s+4)` for `s = expo y + ln_expo x`, and `y ln x` is positive where `y > 0` and `x > 1` are both true or both false, and negative otherwise. `pow_rule_overflow`, `pow_rule_underflow` and `pow_rule_near_one`: for `s >= 10`, x^y rounded in the direction `up` is `overflow up` of `DyadicExp` where `y ln x` is positive and `underflow up` where it is negative, and for `s <= -58`, it is `near_one` of 1 or of -1, by that sign. `pow_rule_not_exact`: where `s >= 10` or `s <= -58`, no finite `f64` is x^y.
- [`DyPowCore.v`](theories/DyPowCore.v): `ev_pow_correct`: `ev_pow`, where it returns, encloses x^y at its level, as `driver_post` asks, at a finite `x > 0` and a finite `y`. It writes ln x as `e ln 2` plus or minus the enclosure of `reduce` (`reduce_correct` of `DyLogCore.v`), takes an integer `k` from an `f64` estimate of `y log2 x`, which `to_k` checks for `|k| <= 2^14` (`pow_to_k_bound`), so that `k as f64` is `k` (`of_i64_exact`, for `|k| <= 2^53`), and encloses `2^k e^-w`, for `w = (k - y e) ln 2` minus or plus `y` times that enclosure, by `scaled` (`scaled_gen` of `DyExp.v`). `pow_w_value`: both signs give `w = k ln 2 - y ln x`, and so `2^k e^-w = x^y`, whatever `k` is.
- [`DyPown.v`](theories/DyPown.v): the theorems above for x^n. `powi_spec`: for a bound `E >= 0` on the exponents of an enclosure `a` with `p (E + 2^13) <= 2^60`, binary powering, `powi`, of `a` to a positive `p` returns an enclosure of `y^p` for every `y` in `a`, with the bound `p (E + 2^13) - 2^13` on its exponents, and the point `y^p` where `a` is the point `y` and every `y^j` for `j` from 1 to `p` has at most the `64 n` bits of the level (`pown_mul` for one product). `ev_pown_correct`: `ev_pown`, where it returns, encloses x^n at its level, at a finite `x > 0` and an `i32` `n` other than 0. `pown_exact_odd`: at a finite `x > 0` and an `i32` `n` other than 0, where x^n is a finite `f64`, the odd part `mx` of `x` has `mx^|n| < 2^53`, and `mx = 1` for `n < 0` (`odd_pow2_inj`). `ev_pown_exact`: there, `ev_pown` returns the point x^n at every level, as no product and no quotient rounds. `ziv_strict_exact` and `ziv_exact`: where an evaluation gives the point of a nonzero `f64` `v` at every level, `ziv_strict` and `ziv` return `v`, from a start of at most 64 (`driver_exact`, from `ziv_at_exact`). `pown_rule_overflow`, `pown_rule_underflow` and `pown_rule_not_exact`: the rules of `pown_pos`, at a finite `x > 0`, for `g = expo x`: x^n is at least 2^1024 where `g n`, or `(g + 1) n` for `n < 0`, is at least 1024 (`pown_over_bound`), and in (0, 2^-1074) where `(g + 1) n`, or `g n` for `n < 0`, is at most -1075 (`pown_under_bound`), so that x^n rounded in the direction `up` is `overflow up` of `DyadicExp` in the first case and `underflow up` in the second, and no finite `f64` is x^n in either. `pown_pos_post` and `pown_pos_exact`: `pown_pos_with z x n up`, for a finite `x > 0` and an `i32` `n` other than 0, where it returns, gives x^n with the property `post` in the direction `up` of every driver `z` with `driver_post z post`, and returns the `f64` x^n wherever x^n is a finite `f64`, for every `z` with `driver_exact z`. `pown_pos_ziv_post` and `pown_pos_ziv_strict`: the same as `pown_pos_post` of `pown_pos_ziv_with`, which is `pown_pos_with` without the fast path and which `pow` calls at an integer `y`, and that where `pown_pos_ziv_with ziv_strict` returns, `pown_pos_ziv_with Dyadic.ziv` returns the same. `pown_dir_nonzero`: at a finite `x` other than 0 and an `n` other than 0, `pown_dir` is `pown_pos` of `|x|`, or, for `x < 0` and an odd `n`, the negation of `pown_pos` of `-x` in the other direction. `pown_rnd_opp`: where `w` is `-r` rounded in the other direction, `-w` is `r` rounded in the direction `up`, which gives `rounds_odd`, and `side_odd` the same of the side (`odd_post`), which `pown_dir_post` asks of `post`. `pown_dir_zero_pos`, `pown_dir_zero_neg` and `pown_dir_inf`: the zeros and infinities that `pown_dir` returns at ±0 and ±∞ for `n` other than 0, negative for a negative `x` and an odd `n`.
- [`DyPow.v`](theories/DyPow.v): the theorems above for x^y. `pow_dir_zero_neg`, `pow_dir_one_inf`, `pow_dir_lt1_inf`, `pow_dir_gt1_inf`, `pow_dir_inf_neg` and `pow_dir_inf_pos`: the values of `pow_dir` at the special arguments of `pow_c` that have one nonzero finite argument: +∞ at +0 and a finite `y < 0`; 1 at a finite `x = 1` and ±∞; at -∞ and +∞, +∞ and +0 for a finite `0 < x < 1`, and +0 and +∞ for a finite `x > 1`; and +0 and +∞ at +∞ and a finite `y < 0` and `y > 0`. `f64_one`: a finite `f64` of value 1 is the literal `1.0`. `to_i32_range`: `to_i32` of any `f64` is in the range of an `i32` (`is_i32`). `fast_pow_dir_exact`: where x^y is a finite `f64` `v` and |y ln x| >= 2^-60, the fast path of `pow`, where it returns, returns `v`.
- [`NearCells.v`](theories/NearCells.v): `near_zero` and `near_inf` above, and the other cells of rounding to nearest that the rules use: `near_exact`, that a finite `f64` rounds to itself, `near_opp`, that the rounding is symmetric, `near_x`, `near_one`, `near_m_one` and `near_ninf`, the numbers that round to `x`, 1, -1 and -∞, and `near_unique`, that a number other than 0 has one rounding.
- [`NearRound.v`](theories/NearRound.v) and [`DyConvNear.v`](theories/DyConvNear.v): `to_f64_near_correct` above, from the rounding to nearest of the window of bits and the sticky bit that `round_near` takes (`near_window`).
- [`DyDriverNear.v`](theories/DyDriverNear.v): the theorems of Ziv's strategy rounded to nearest above, and `ziv_near_strict_ziv`.
- [`DyExpNear.v`](theories/DyExpNear.v), [`DyLogNear.v`](theories/DyLogNear.v), [`DyAtanNear.v`](theories/DyAtanNear.v), [`DyTrigNear.v`](theories/DyTrigNear.v) and [`DyPowNear.v`](theories/DyPowNear.v): the theorems above of the functions rounded to nearest, `<f>_near_correct` and `<f>_near_strict`, and `pow_near_midpoint`.
- [`NearAtanMid.v`](theories/NearAtanMid.v), [`MidQuot.v`](theories/MidQuot.v), [`MidRound.v`](theories/MidRound.v) and [`DyAtanMidQ.v`](theories/DyAtanMidQ.v): `midpoint_quotient_correct`, `div_mid` and `to_f64_mid` above, `midpoint_quotient_dyadic_correct`, the same of `midpoint_quotient_dyadic`, its path for `x >= 2^996`, and `near_atan_mid` and `near_atan_mid_neg`, that atan q rounds to nearest to the `f64` next to q toward 0 at a midpoint q of two `f64`s below 2^-1021 in magnitude.
- [`DyPowNearExact.v`](theories/DyPowNearExact.v): `exact_near_sound` and `exact_near_complete` above.
- [`CrPow.v`](theories/CrPow.v): the theorems above of `pow` of `inari::cr`.
- [`RootReal.v`](theories/RootReal.v): `hypot_cut` above, and the real cube root: `Rcbrt_cube`, that the cube of `Rcbrt r` is `r`, `Rcbrt_unique`, that `Rcbrt r` is the only real whose cube is `r`, `Rcbrt_lt`, that `Rcbrt` is strictly increasing, and `Rcbrt_opp`, that it is odd.
- [`RootRound.v`](theories/RootRound.v): `odd_correct`, `odd_window_dir` and `odd_window_near` above.
- [`DyIcbrt.v`](theories/DyIcbrt.v): `icbrt_correct` above, from the step of Newton's iteration for the cube root on integers, which gives at least floor(cbrt x) from any `s > 0` (`newton3_ge`) and less than `s` where `s` is above it (`newton3_lt`).
- [`FastCbrtNewton.v`](theories/FastCbrtNewton.v): `cbrt_newton` and `cbrt_step_err` above, on real numbers. `cbrt_newton`: for `Z >= 1` and `y > 0` with `|y^3 - Z| <= 2^-32 + 2^-48`, the step `y - y (y^3 - Z) / (3 Z)` is within 2^-66 of cbrt Z. `cbrt_step_err`: for `Y` in [0.999, 2.001], `R` within `2^-53 / Z` of `1 / Z` and `|D| <= 2^-32 + 2^-48`, the correction that `cbrt_step` computes from `D` and an error `eta` of at most 2^-100, by four operations each within the error of a rounding to nearest, is at most 1/2 in magnitude and within 2^-82 of `Y D / (3 Z)`.
- [`FastCbrtStep.v`](theories/FastCbrtStep.v): `cbrt_step_correct` above, from the two theorems of `FastCbrtNewton.v` and `two_prod_err_correct`, and `cbrt_step_fma`, that `cbrt_step` returns the same with and without an FMA for `zz` in [1, 8).
- [`FastCbrtReduce.v`](theories/FastCbrtReduce.v): `cbrt_split_bits` and `cbrt_zz_rr` above. `cbrt_split_bits`: for a finite `x` other than 0, the `m` and `e` that `fast_cbrt` reads from the bits of `x`, after the leading zeros of a subnormal, satisfy `0 <= m < 2^52`, `-51 <= e <= 2046` and `|x| = (2^52 + m) 2^(e - 1075)`. `cbrt_zz_rr`: for `i` in {0, 1, 2}, `zz = (2^52 + m) 2^(i - 52)` lies in [1, 8) and `rr` within `2^-53 / zz` of `1 / zz`.
- [`FastCbrt.v`](theories/FastCbrt.v): `fast_cbrt_correct`, `fast_cbrt_dir_correct` and `fast_cbrt_near_correct` above, from the three files before it and the tests of `FastRoundErr.v`, and `fast_cbrt_dir_fma` and `fast_cbrt_near_fma`, that `fast_cbrt_dir` and `fast_cbrt_near` return the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastHypotFar.v`](theories/FastHypotFar.v): `hypot_far_dir` and `hypot_far_near` above.
- [`FastHypotSum.v`](theories/FastHypotSum.v): `hypot_sum_sq_correct` above: for `x` in [1/2, 1) and `y` in [2^-78, x], the double-double `ah + al` that `hypot_dd` computes has `ah` in [1/8, 4] and `|al| <= 2^-52 ah`, and is within 2^-102 of `x^2 + y^2`. `hypot_sum_sq_fma`: the errors of the two squares are the same with and without an FMA.
- [`FastHypotDd.v`](theories/FastHypotDd.v): `hypot_dd_correct` above, from `hypot_sum_sq_correct` and `dd_sqrt_correct`, and `hypot_dd_fma`, that `hypot_dd` returns the same with and without an FMA, from `hypot_sum_sq_fma` and `dd_sqrt_fma`. `hypot_dd_scale`, which `Assumptions.v` does not print: where the biased exponent `eb` of `big` is in [1, 2044] and that of `small` is less than 27 below it, multiplying both by 2^(1022 - eb) is exact and puts `big` in [1/2, 1) and `small` in [2^-78, big].
- [`FastHypot.v`](theories/FastHypot.v): `fast_hypot_dir_correct` and `fast_hypot_near_correct` above, from `hypot_far_dir`, `hypot_far_near`, `hypot_dd_correct` and the tests of `FastRoundErr.v`, and `fast_hypot_dir_fma` and `fast_hypot_near_fma`, that `fast_hypot` and `fast_hypot_near` return the same with and without an FMA, from `hypot_dd_fma`.
- [`DyCbrt.v`](theories/DyCbrt.v): `cbrt_dir_correct`, `cbrt_near_correct` and `cbrt_special` above.
- [`DyHypotExact.v`](theories/DyHypotExact.v) and [`DyHypotOdd.v`](theories/DyHypotOdd.v): `hypot_exact_branch` and `hypot_odd_correct` above.
- [`DyHypot.v`](theories/DyHypot.v): `hypot_dir_correct`, `hypot_near_correct`, `hypot_inf`, `hypot_nan` and `hypot_zeros` above.
- [`CrRoot.v`](theories/CrRoot.v): the theorems above of `sqrt_rd`, `sqrt_ru` and `sqrt_rn` of `inari::cr`.
- [`DyPoint.v`](theories/DyPoint.v): `kernel_point`, `k_exp_c` to `k_tanh_c`, `k_ln_c` to `k_atanh_c`, `k_atan_c` to `k_atan2_c`, `k_sin_c` to `k_tan_c`, `k_pow_c`, `k_pown_c`, `kernel_exp_correct` to `kernel_tanh_correct`, `kernel_ln_correct` to `kernel_atanh_correct`, `kernel_atan_correct` to `kernel_atan2_correct`, `kernel_sin_correct` to `kernel_tan_correct`, `kernel_pow_correct` and `kernel_pown_correct` above, and `k_cbrt_some`, `k_hypot_some`, `k_cbrt_c`, `k_hypot_c`, `kernel_cbrt_correct` and `kernel_hypot_correct`. `k_hypot_returns`: `DyadicRoot.hypot` returns `Some` at every pair of `f64`s, +∞ if one is infinite (`hypot_inf`), else NaN if one is NaN (`hypot_nan`), else the value of `hypot_dir_correct`. `kernel_field_correct`: a field made with `kernel_field` of a function that rounds `f` where it returns satisfies `point_correct` for `f`. `kernel_field_correct_dom`: the same for a function that does so at the finite arguments in a domain `D`, for `point_correct D f`. `kernel_field2_correct`: the same for a function of two `f64`s, in a field made with `kernel_field2`, for `point_correct2 D f`, with the two arguments in the order of `dir` and `f`, so that `k_pow` takes `x` first. `kernel_field_z_correct`: the same for a function of an `f64` and an integer, in a field made with `kernel_field_z`, at a finite `x` and an `i32` `n` in a domain `D`, in both directions `rnd`, as the first clause of `pown_c` states it. `k_pow_const` and `k_pown_const`: where `pow_dir` or `pown_dir` returns the same `f64` in both directions, `k_pow` or `k_pown` is that `f64` at both `RNDD` and `RNDU`.
- [`DyPointSide.v`](theories/DyPointSide.v): `kernel_point_rust`, `k_exp_rust_s` to `k_tan_rust_s`, `kernel_exp_encloses` to `kernel_pown_encloses`, `kernel_cbrt_encloses` and `kernel_hypot_encloses` above. `kernel_field_side`, `kernel_field2_side` and `kernel_field_z_side`: a field made with `kernel_field`, `kernel_field2` or `kernel_field_z` of a function that returns values on the side of `f` satisfies `point_side` for `f`, as the result rounded by definition is on the side too (`round_on_side`).
- [`DyPointDec.v`](theories/DyPointDec.v): `kernel_dec_exp_correct` to `kernel_dec_pown_correct`, `kernel_dec_cbrt_correct` and `kernel_dec_hypot_correct`, the 23 theorems about the decorated elementary functions with `kernel_point`, each `dec_<f>_correct_point kernel_point k_<f>_c` of `PointDec.v`, with `k_sin_c` and `k_cos_c` too for `sin`, `cos` and `tan`. They cover the same calls of the kernel as those of `DyPoint.v`, which for `cbrt` and `hypot` are all of them.

## What is assumed

[`Model.v`](theories/Model.v) transcribes the Rust functions line by line. It has to be checked against the Rust code by reading, and updated whenever those functions change. So do the headers of [`ComCommon.v`](theories/ComCommon.v) and [`Valid.v`](theories/Valid.v), which argue from the Rust code that the library makes every `DecInterval` and every `Interval` at the places and by the operations that `Model.v` transcribes. [`ModelTrigTable.v`](theories/ModelTrigTable.v), which holds the table `SC` that `Model.v` reads as `DyadicTrig.SC`, is trusted in the same way, but a test checks it in place of a reader: the ignored test `write_sc_tables` of `src/dyadic/trig/tests.rs` writes it from MPFR, together with `src/dyadic/trig/sc_table.rs`, and the test `sc_table_in_rocq`, which is compiled with the MPFR tests on 64-bit Linux and macOS, checks that it is, byte for byte, the text that the same code makes of the Rust `SC`. That test returns without a check when the file is missing, as `rocq/` is not in the package that `cargo package` makes. The bullets below on the places that make a `DecInterval` and an `Interval` say which parts of those arguments `check-sites.sh` checks. `Model.v`'s modelling choices:

- `f64` is Flocq's binary64, and Rust's `+`, `-`, `*` and `/` are Flocq's `Bplus`, `Bminus`, `Bmult` and `Bdiv` rounded to nearest, ties to even. `generic.rs` relies on this default rounding mode. `f64::mul_add` is Flocq's `Bfma` in the same mode, with a single rounding, as Rust specifies it whether or not the target has an FMA instruction. `f64::sqrt` is Flocq's `Bsqrt`, also rounded to nearest, ties to even. Flocq has no remainder, so `x % y` of `f64`s is `frem`, C's `fmod`: the exact `x - k * y` for the real quotient `x / y` truncated to an integer `k`, which is always a float, with the sign of `x`. `x.rem_euclid(y)` is `rem_euclid`, the standard library's `let r = x % y; if r < 0.0 { r + y.abs() } else { r }`.
- NaN payloads and signs are not modelled: all NaNs are the same value, so `Bopp` keeps a NaN as it is where Rust's unary `-` flips its sign bit. The functions modelled here only read the exponent field of a NaN, which is all ones for every NaN, except `add_odd`. It reads the lowest bit of `s`, which for a NaN is part of the payload, but returns a NaN for a NaN `s` either way.
- Bit patterns are integers rather than `u64`, so `b + 1` and `b - 1` in `step_up` cannot wrap around. `step_up_no_wrap` in `Encoding.v` shows that for finite floats they stay in the range of `u64`, so they don't wrap around (or panic) in Rust either. The same holds for ±∞ and NaN, but is not proved: `b + 1` is only computed if `x > 0`, so the sign bit of `b` is clear, and `b - 1` only if `x < 0`, so it is set. `step_up_if` computes `b - 2 * (u & (b >> 63)) + u`, where `u` is `up as u64`, 1 or 0, and `&` and `>>` are `Z.land` and `Z.shiftr`, which agree with them on integers in the range of `u64`. `step_up_if_no_wrap` in `Encoding.v` shows that `2 * (u & (b >> 63))` is at most 2, that the subtraction stays at or above 0 and that the sum stays below `2^64` for every float, finite or not, so none of them wraps around (or panics) in Rust. Its proof uses only that `b` is in the range of `u64`, so this also holds for the NaNs with other payloads and signs, which the model does not have (see above).
- The other integer types, `u128`, `u64`, `u32` and `i32`, are integers too. `rhs % 2 == 0` of the `i32` `rhs` in `Interval::powi` is `Z.rem n 2 =? 0`, as Rust's `%` of integers truncates the quotient, as `Z.rem` does, and with the divisor 2 it cannot overflow. In the slow path and in `mul1_ru_tiny`, every operation that panics in a debug build returns `None`: one whose result does not fit its type, a shift by a negative amount or by at least the width of the type, and the `debug_assert!`s in `sum_round_up` and `mul1_ru_tiny`. A release build would wrap around instead, so a theorem that a function returns `Some` covers both kinds of build. The integer `/` and `%` in `div1_ru_slow` return `None` for a zero divisor, which panics in both kinds of build. Arithmetic on the fields of a bit pattern, in `exponent` and `decompose`, cannot overflow and is not checked.
- Casts that could change a value reduce it modulo the size of the target type, as Rust does: `(e + 1023) as u64`, `kept as u64` and `(ex - ey) as u32`. Casts that keep the value are left out: those of the 11-bit exponent field in `exponent` and `decompose`, and, each with a comment, the `leading_zeros() as i32` casts, `sh as i32` and the `as u128` casts of significands.
- `kept as f64` is Flocq's `binary_normalize` rounded to nearest, ties to even, like Rust's conversion from `u64`. A float literal is the same conversion of its value: Rust rounds a literal to nearest too, and the ones used, `134217729.0` (`2^27 + 1`) in `split`, `1.0` and `18446744073709551616.0` (`2^64`) in `sqrt1_slow` and `mul1_ru_tiny`, `1.0` in `Interval::sign`, in `Interval::cosh`, in `Interval::pow` and in the domains of `acos`, `acosh`, `asin` and `atanh`, `1.0`, `2.0`, `3.0`, `4.0` and `8.0` in `Interval::cos`, `Interval::sin`, `Interval::tan`, `rem_euclid_2`, `quadrant` and `quadrants`, and `4503599627370496.0` (`2^52`) in `round_ties_even1`, are exact. `-1.0` in `Interval::sign`, in `Interval::recip`, in `Interval::atanh`, in `Interval::cos`, in `Interval::sin` and in the domains of `acos`, `asin` and `atanh` is the negation of the literal `1.0`, and `-0.0` in `Interval::powi` that of `0.0`, as Rust parses them. The bounds of the constants of `Interval` from `E` to `TAU`, decimal literals that Rust rounds to nearest, are given by the bit patterns of the floats they round to. The test [`tests/model_constants.rs`](../tests/model_constants.rs), which CI runs on every push and pull request to `main`, reads the definitions of these constants in `Model.v`, in the form that `Model.v` describes, and checks that they are those of the 19 constants, each once, that their bit patterns are those of the bounds of the Rust constants, and that the two decimals in the comment above each round to nearest to them. That these decimals are the literals of the Rust constants is checked by reading. The test skips if the crate has neither `rocq/` nor `.git`, as in the published crate, which leaves out `rocq/`, and is ignored on WASI, where CI gives it no files to read. The `const`s in `sqrt1_slow`, `mul1_ru_tiny` and `round_ties_even1` are evaluated at compile time, rounded to nearest as at run time. `0.5` in `impl_dec!(round, ...)`, `impl_dec!(round_ties_even, ...)` and `Interval::cancel_minus` is given by its bit pattern, and `0.0` in `max1`, `min1`, `classify`, `Interval::abs`, `Interval::sign`, `Interval::sqr`, `Interval::sqrt`, `Interval::zero`, `impl_dec!(sign, ...)`, `impl_dec!(trunc, ...)`, `DecInterval::sqrt`, `DecInterval::recip`, `DecInterval::div`, `DecInterval::mul_rev_to_pair`, `impl_log!`, `Interval::atan2`, `Interval::cosh`, `Interval::cos`, `Interval::pow`, `Interval::powi`, `Interval::sin`, `Interval::tan`, `rem_euclid_2` and `quadrant` is +0. `*` is Flocq's `Bmult` rounded to nearest, ties to even, and unary `-` is `Bopp`.
- `F64X2` is a pair, and so are the array `[Self; 2]` that `mul_rev_to_pair` returns and the array `[f64; 2]` that `extract` returns. `lanewise2` takes the two paths as functions. `sqrt1_slow` takes `f` as a function too, one that returns an `option` like the models passed for it.
- `sqrt1_rd_impl` and `sqrt1_ru_impl` call themselves through `sqrt1_slow`. Rocq functions must terminate, so the models take the number of nested calls they may make and return `None` if they would make more. `sqrt1_rd` and `sqrt1_ru` allow one. A result `Some r` means that the Rust function returns `r`.
- A const generic parameter, such as `FMA`, is an ordinary argument. So is `HAS_FMA`, which `mul_ru`, `div_ru`, `mul_add_ru`, `sqrt1_rd` and `sqrt1_ru` pass for it, as its value depends on the target. `Interval::mul` and `Interval::sqr`, which call `mul_ru`, `Interval::mul_add`, which calls `mul_add_ru`, `Interval::sqrt`, which calls `sqrt1_ru` and `sqrt_rd_ru`, `Interval::recip`, `Interval::div` and its ten helpers, and `Interval::mul_rev_to_pair`, which call `div_ru`, `Interval::cos`, `Interval::sin` and `Interval::tan`, which call `Interval::div`, and `tan` also `Interval::mul`, and their decorated versions take it as the argument `has_fma`. The fast path of `exp`, `exp2` and `exp10` (`fast_exp` and `fast_exp_by`, which call `two_prod_err::<HAS_FMA>`) takes it as the argument `fma`, and `DyadicExp.exp_dir_with` and the others pass `false` for it, as `fast_exp_dir_fma` and the others show that `true` gives the same result. The same holds of the fast paths of `sinh`, `cosh` and `tanh`, whose `fast_sinh_poly`, `fast_cosh_poly` and `dd_div` call `two_prod_err::<HAS_FMA>` too (`fast_sinh_dir_fma` and the others), and of the fast path of `ln`, `log2` and `log10` (`log1p_poly`, `ln_core` and `fast_log_by`, which call `two_prod_err::<HAS_FMA>`), for which `DyadicLog.ln_dir_with` and the others pass `false`, as `fast_ln_dir_fma` and the others show that `true` gives the same result at every finite `x > 0`. The same holds of the fast path of `sin`, `cos` and `tan` (`fast_mul`, which `fast_reduce_big` and `fast_reduce_band_big` call, and `fast_eval`, `fast_div` and `fast_band_reduce`, which call `two_prod_err::<HAS_FMA>`), for which `DyadicTrig.sin_dir_with` and the others pass `false`, as `fast_sin_dir_fma` and the others show that `true` gives the same result at every argument of magnitude at least 2^-27. The same holds of the fast path of `atan`, `atan2`, `asin` and `acos` (`fast_atan_poly`, `fast_atan_core` and `fast_sqrt1m`, which call `two_prod_err::<HAS_FMA>`, and `dd_div` and `dd_sqrt`), for which `DyadicAtan.atan_dir_with` and the others pass `false`, as `fast_atan_dir_fma` and the others show that `true` gives the same result at every argument that the rules leave to it. The same holds of the fast paths of the cube root and `hypot` (`cbrt_step` and `hypot_dd`, which call `two_prod_err::<HAS_FMA>`, and `dd_sqrt`), for which `DyadicRoot.cbrt` and the others pass `false`, as `fast_cbrt_dir_fma` and the others show that `true` gives the same result wherever the arguments are finite and other than 0, as they are wherever the fast paths are called. `exact_div`, which calls `two_prod_err::<HAS_FMA>`, takes it as `fma` too, and `tiny_quotient` and `midpoint_quotient` pass `false` for it, as `exact_div_fma` shows that `true` gives the same result where they call it. The functions of the kernel `sin`, `cos`, `tan`, `atan`, `asin`, `acos`, `atan2`, `ln`, `log2`, `log10`, `sinh`, `pow` and `hypot`, which call a copy per direction, `<f>_dir::<true>` if `up` and `<f>_dir::<false>` otherwise, of a function `<f>_dir` with a const generic `UP`, are modelled as the body of `<f>_dir` with the argument `up` for `UP`.
- `f64::floor`, `f64::ceil`, `f64::round` and `f64::trunc` are Flocq's `Bnearbyint` toward -∞, toward +∞, to nearest with ties away from zero, and toward zero, IEEE 754's roundToIntegralTowardNegative, roundToIntegralTowardPositive, roundToIntegralTiesToAway and roundToIntegralTowardZero. `f64::abs` is Flocq's `Babs`. `f64::is_finite` in `Interval::contains` is Flocq's `is_finite`, which is false exactly for the infinities and NaN, as `f64::is_finite` is, and its `&` of two `bool`s is `&&`, which gives the same value. `x.copysign(y)` is `x` if `x` and `y` have the same sign, and `-x` otherwise. As NaNs have no sign in the model, it takes a NaN `y` as positive, but its only callers, `round_ties_even1` and the functions of `DyadicAtan`, never pass a NaN for `y`. `p.is_sign_negative()` in `mul1_ru_tiny` is Flocq's `Bsign p`, which differs from it only for a NaN, and `p = x * y` is not NaN there, as `x` and `y` are finite. `wx.partial_cmp(&wy)` in `Interval::cancel_minus` is Flocq's `Bcompare`, which, like `f64::partial_cmp`, returns `None` if either is NaN, and otherwise compares the values, with `0.0` equal to `-0.0` and -∞ and +∞ below and above every other float. `Less`, `Equal` and `Greater` are `Lt`, `Eq` and `Gt`, and `unreachable!()` is `None`.
- `Interval` is a record of its `rep`, and `DecInterval` of an `Interval` and a `Decoration`, whose variants compare by their discriminants, as the derived `Ord` does. `valid` states the form of `rep` from the comment on `struct Interval`, with the bounds that `TryFrom<(f64, f64)>` accepts. The theorems assume that their argument is `valid`, which `made_valid` in `Valid.v` proves for the intervals that the constructors make and the proved operations compute from them.
- A comparison of two `F64X2`s, such as `lt`, returns a mask, whose lanes have every bit set where the comparison holds and every bit clear where it does not. A lane with every bit set is a NaN, which `to_bits` would give the bits of another NaN, so the model keeps the bit patterns of the lanes of a mask instead. `all` and `bitmask` take these bit patterns, and so does `and` as its second argument. `and` reads the lanes of its first argument with `to_bits`, and `or` those of both of its arguments, which gives their bits only if they are not NaN. `Interval::sign`, their only caller, passes no NaN: it calls `and` on `1.0` and `-1.0`, and `or` on the results, whose lanes are `1.0`, `-1.0` or `0.0`.
- `max1` and `min1` read `to_bits` only of two zeros, whose bit patterns the model gives exactly. `f64::NAN` in `maximum1` and `minimum1` is the model's only NaN.
- `unsafe { transmute(c) }` in `classify`, from a `u8` to an `IntervalClass`, is `transmute_class`, which returns `None` unless `c` is the discriminant of a variant, as the behaviour is undefined then. The variants `E`, `M`, `N0`, `N1`, `P0`, `P1` and `Z` are `CE`, `CM` and so on, as `Z` is the type of integers. The casts `bitmask(...) as u8` keep the value, which is below 4, and are left out, and `|` of two `bool`s in `either_empty` is `||`, which gives the same value. `transmute` in `classify2`, from a `u8` to an `IntervalClass2`, is `transmute_class2`, which returns `None` unless `c / 16` and `c % 16` are both discriminants of `IntervalClass`, and otherwise the pair of the variants that `transmute_class` decodes them to, written `(CM, CN0)` for the variant `M_N0` and so on. That is the variant with discriminant `c`, as every discriminant of `IntervalClass` is below 16. The casts `c as u8` of an `IntervalClass` in `classify2` are `class_discriminant`.
- `HAS_MAXIMUM` depends on the backend: it is `true` in `generic.rs` and `false` in `x86_64.rs`. So `Interval::max`, `Interval::min` and their decorated versions take it as the argument `has_maximum`, as for `HAS_FMA`, and the theorems hold for either value. The branch for `false` calls `either_empty` and the backend's `max` and `min`, which are the portable backend's here, as every backend function in the model is.
- The places that make a `DecInterval`, for `ComCommon.v`, are the ones listed above. `git grep -n 'new_unchecked\|transmute' -- src` finds the callers of `new_unchecked` and the `transmute`s, and `git grep -nE '(Self|DecInterval) *\{|\b[xd]: ' -- src/interval/dec_interval.rs` finds the declarations of the fields `x` and `d`, without `pub`, and the one struct expression of `DecInterval`, in `new_unchecked`. [`check-sites.sh`](check-sites.sh), which CI runs on every push and pull request to `main`, fails if the lines that these two greps find differ from those in [`sites.txt`](sites.txt). It compares the lines without their line numbers and indentation, so it fails if a line is added, removed, changed or moved to another file, but not if it only moves within its file. In particular, it fails if the line of a field declaration changes, for example to `pub(crate) x: Interval,`. It also fails if this file no longer gives one of the six greps of this bullet and the next one verbatim. That the two greps find every place that makes a `DecInterval`, and that the places among their lines are the ones listed above, is still checked by reading, and so is that `src/interval/dec_interval.rs` declares no module, has no struct expression other than the one in `new_unchecked`, neither assigns to a field nor takes a mutable reference to one, and derives only `Clone`, `Copy` and `Debug`, which copy or read the fields, and that the other `unsafe` code outside `src/simd` only calls MPFR, in code that only the tests compile. The greps find the body of `def_com!` but not its uses, so the compiler checks the bounds of each use instead. `def_com!` reads the lanes `[-a; b]` of the constant of `Interval` that it decorates `com` with a `transmute` into `[f64; 2]`, the reverse of the one in `const_interval!`, and asserts in a `const` that -∞ < `a` <= `b` < +∞ (`def_com_ok` in `Model.v`), so that a use of the macro compiles only if this holds, whatever made that constant. `def_com_ok_bounds` shows that the constant of `Interval` is then `const_interval a b` for finite floats `a` and `b` with `const_interval_ok a b`, as `def_com_com_common` and `dmade_def_com` in `Valid.v` assume. What is left to read is that `def_com_ok` transcribes the assertion, with the two lanes that the `transmute` gives as the pair `rep`. `try_from_be_bytes` and its siblings take the floats `a` and `b` that `f64::from_be_bytes` and so on decode, which the model leaves out, as the theorem holds for any. Their `a.is_sign_negative()` is Flocq's `Bsign a` and `b.is_sign_positive()` its negation, which differ from Rust only for a NaN, where `a <= b` already makes the condition false. The `transmute` in `const_dec_interval!` puts an `Interval` and a `Decoration` into the fields of a `DecInterval`, as both structs are `#[repr(C)]` with the same fields in the same order, which is checked by reading too. `f64::NEG_INFINITY` and `f64::INFINITY` are `B754_infinity true` and `B754_infinity false`.
- The places that make an `Interval`, for `Valid.v`, are the ones listed there and the operations. [`check-sites.sh`](check-sites.sh) also runs the four greps below, in CI as above. It fails if one of the two that must find nothing finds something, or if the lines that the other two find differ from those in [`sites.txt`](sites.txt), compared as for `ComCommon.v`. The rest of this argument is still checked by reading. The field `rep` of `Interval` is `pub(crate)`, so only the library can make an `Interval` with a struct expression. `git grep -nE '\brep *:' -- src ':!src/simd'` finds every struct expression of `Interval`, as none uses the shorthand `{ rep }` (`git grep -nE '\{ *rep *[,}]' -- src` finds nothing), and `git grep -n 'with_infsup_raw\|transmute' -- src ':!src/simd'` finds the calls of `with_infsup_raw` and the `transmute`s, of which those in `Interval::EMPTY` and `const_interval!` make an `Interval`. As for `ComCommon.v`, the other `unsafe` code outside `src/simd` only calls MPFR, in code that only the tests compile, and the backends in `src/simd` make no `Interval`. Nothing assigns to `rep` (`git grep -nE '\.rep *=[^=]' -- src ':!src/simd'` finds nothing), and `x += y` and the other compound assignments assign what an operation returns. Each struct expression and each call of `with_infsup_raw` is in one of those places, in a proved operation, or in `floors_exact`, which only `cos`, `sin` and `tan` call, and whose singletons `[a, a]` and `[b, b]` of finite floats are `valid` (`tc_with_valid` in `TrigCert.v`) and are not returned. The places that make a `DecInterval` are those of `ComCommon.v`, above, and `DecInterval::interval` returns the interval of one. A `Result` is an `option`, with `Ok(x)` as `Some x` and the `Err` as `None`. `const_interval_ok` is the condition that `const_interval!` asserts in a `const`, so that a use of the macro compiles only if it is true. `def_com!` asserts `def_com_ok` in the same way, and `def_com_ok_bounds` shows that this gives `const_interval_ok` for the bounds of the constant that it decorates, as `dmade_def_com` assumes. `From<DecNInterval>` and `try_from_ninterval_exact` take what the grammar of `src/parse.rs` returns, and the theorems of `Valid.v` about them hold for any `DecNInterval` and `NInterval`, with no hypothesis about the grammar, for a call that does not panic.
- The conversions of `src/parse.rs` are modelled from the `NInterval` or `DecNInterval` that the grammar returns. The grammar, apart from the step that decorates a literal, is not modelled (see "What is not proved yet"), and `ninterval_ok` in `Model.v` states what it is checked by reading to return: `infsup_interval` returns `InfSup(a, b)` only if `a <= b`, `a` is not `Infinity` and `b` is not `NegInfinity`, and the other rules return `NInterval::EMPTY`, `NInterval::ENTIRE`, a `Point` of a rational, or, in `uncertain_interval`, the bounds `center - radius` or `center`, and `center + radius` or `center`, for a radius that is not negative, with -∞ and +∞ in place of `center - radius` and `center + radius` for an unbounded radius. And each bound that is a `Rational` has `rat_ok`, as `number_ok` states: its denominator is positive, as the comment of `Rat` says, its exponents `e2` and `e5` are `i32`s, and its numerator and denominator have at most `2^63` bits, `2^60` bytes, more memory than any machine has. `Model.v` transcribes the step of the grammar that decorates a literal: `NInterval::EMPTY`, `DecNInterval::EMPTY`, `DecNInterval::NAI`, `DecNInterval::new` and `DecNInterval::set_dec` as `n_EMPTY`, `dn_EMPTY`, `dn_NAI`, `dn_new` and `dn_set_dec`, and the arm `(Ok(x), Some(d))` of the closure in `decorated_interval`, which returns `DecNInterval::set_dec(x, d)` if its decoration is `d` and rejects the literal otherwise, as `dn_decorated`, with `Ok(xd)` as `Some xd` and the `Err` as `None`. `ParseDec.v` proves `dec_ninterval_ok` of `dn_NAI`, and of what `dn_new`, `dn_set_dec` and `dn_decorated` return for an `x` with `ninterval_ok` (see `from_dec_ninterval_correct` above). Besides `ninterval_ok`, two things are checked by reading. The `nom` combinators of `decorated_interval` call this step: `alt` returns `Ok(DecNInterval::NAI)` for `[nai]`, and otherwise `map` applies the closure to what `interval` and `opt(preceded(char('_'), decoration))` return, so that the arm `(Ok(x), None)` returns `DecNInterval::new(x)` for a literal without a decoration and the arm `(Ok(x), Some(d))` is `dn_decorated x d` for one with a decoration `d`. And `FromStr` for `DecInterval` passes the `x` of `Ok(("", Ok(x)))` to `From<DecNInterval>`. A `Rational` holds a `Rat` of `src/parse/rat.rs`, the record `Rat` of `Model.v`, with each `Nat` as the `Z` that it holds, whose limbs are `NatLimbs.to_limbs` of it, and the value `rat_R`, `(-1)^neg * num * 2^e2 * 5^e5 / den`, which need not be in lowest terms. That reading of the limbs needs a `Nat` to have no zero limb at the top, as the comment of `src/nat.rs` says. The operations of `Nat` that `Model.v` transcribes keep that (`NatOps.v`), and that `Nat::from_dec`, `Nat::from_hex` and `-`, with which the grammar computes the `Nat`s of a `Rat`, return no zero limb at the top is checked by reading. `NInterval::InfSup` and `NInterval::Point` are `NInfSup` and `NPoint`, the derived `PartialOrd` of `Number` is `number_le`, which compares the variants in the order of their declaration and two `Rat`s by `impl Ord for Rat`, taken to compare their values (see "What is not proved yet"), and `x == NInterval::EMPTY` is `nis_empty x`. `Model.v` transcribes `scale`, `round_up_scaled`, `rational_to_f64` and `number_to_f64`, which round a bound to an `f64` with no call to MPFR, as functions that return an `option`, with `None` where an `i128` operation would overflow or an `as` cast to `u64` or `i32` would change the value (`checked_i` and `checked_u`), or where a `Nat` would be divided by zero, which panics (`NatLimbs.div_rem_z`). The theorems show that they return `Some` for a bound with `number_ok`. `round_up_scaled` calls `Generic.round_up_exact`, the model of `round_up_exact` in `src/simd/generic.rs`.
- `Model.v` transcribes the functions of `src/format/digits.rs` as `format_decompose`, `format_exact_dec`, `format_away`, `format_cut_dec`, `format_fixed`, `format_sci`, `format_cut_bin` and `format_hex`, which return an `option`, with `None` where an `i64`, `u64` or `u32` operation would overflow, a `u64` would be shifted by 64 or more, or a `Nat` would be divided by zero (`checked_i`, `checked_u`, `shl` and `shr`, and `NatLimbs.div_rem_z`). As for `src/parse.rs`, each `Nat` is the `Z` that it holds, with the limbs `NatLimbs.to_limbs` of it, and `Nat::from_u64`, `Nat::pow5`, `<<`, `*`, `+`, `div_rem`, `is_zero` and `digits10` are their loops of `NatLimbs` on those limbs, which `NatOps.v` proves to compute the operations on `Z`, `digits10` Flocq's `Zdigits radix10`. The casts that could change a value are written as Rust computes them: `as i64` of a `u64` or a `usize` as `(z + 2^63) mod 2^64 - 2^63`, `as u64` as `z mod 2^64` and `as u32` as `z mod 2^32`, while `i64::from` and `u64::from` keep the value. A precision is a `usize`, an integer `p` with `0 <= p < 2^64` on the 64-bit targets, and on smaller ones too. The functions cast it only after capping it at 1100, or in `hex` below 13, so that the casts keep its value whatever the width of `usize`. `!(1 << 63)` is `2 ^ 63 - 1`, and `x.is_sign_negative()` is `Bsign x`, as `x` is not NaN there. The rest of `src/format.rs` is not modelled.
- `Limbs`, `NatLimbs` and `Dyadic` in `Model.v` transcribe the loops of `src/limbs.rs`, the operations of `Nat` above and the dyadic kernel of `src/dyadic.rs` with each slice of limbs as a `list Z`, the least significant limb first. A function that writes a slice `out: &mut [u64]` returns its new contents. The bounds checks of slices, `split_at_mut` and the `usize` arithmetic on lengths and bit counts are not modelled: for the lengths that the callers pass, every range and index is in bounds and none of that arithmetic overflows, which is checked by reading. Nor are `with_buf`, which gives the kernel its scratch limbs, the scratch of `limbs::div_rem`, and the buffer that `reduce` of `src/dyadic/trig.rs` splits into its slices and that scratch: each function writes every limb of the scratch that it reads before it reads it, so its contents do not matter. Products of limbs in `u128` are checked (`checked_u 128`), `overflowing_add` and `overflowing_sub` are `ovf_add` and `ovf_sub`, and `expect`, `assert!` and `panic!` are `None`. The loop of D3 in `knuth_step`, the `loop` of `isqrt` and the `while` of `digits10` take fuel, which the theorems show is enough. The const generic `N` of `Dyadic<N>` is the `nat` `n`, and the evaluation `f.eval::<N>()` that Ziv's strategy calls is a function `ev` of `n`. `isqrt` seeds Newton's iteration with bits of `(u as f64).sqrt()`, for `u` the top bits `t[0]` of its argument with their low 11 bits cleared, which the model computes with `Z.land`, `of_u64` and `fsqrt`, but the proof uses only that the 32 bits it keeps, with the implicit bit set, are at least 2^31 and below 2^32 (`seed_bounds`), which holds for every `f64`, so it holds whatever `f64::sqrt` returns.
- `DyadicConsts`, `DyadicExp`, `DyadicLog`, `DyadicAtan`, `DyadicTrig` and `DyadicPow` in `Model.v` transcribe `src/dyadic/consts.rs`, `src/dyadic/exp.rs`, `src/dyadic/log.rs`, `src/dyadic/atan.rs`, `src/dyadic/trig.rs` and `src/dyadic/pow.rs` in the same way, the tables of ln 2, ln 10 and π as lists of the limbs of the Rust arrays, in the same order. `Fast` transcribes `src/dyadic/fast.rs` in the same way, except the switch and the counter that only the tests compile, and `DyadicExp` the tables `T1` and `T2` of `src/dyadic/exp/tables.rs` as lists of the pairs of bit patterns of the Rust arrays, in the same order, indexed with `nth` at `k & 63` and `(k >> 6) & 63`, which are in [0, 63], and the constants of `fast_exp` by their bit patterns. In `scale` and `two_pow`, `(e + 1023) as u64` and `<< 52` are reduced modulo 2^64, and in `round_dir`, `up as u64` is `Z.b2z up`, `l.to_bits() >> 63` is `Z.shiftr (to_bits l) 63`, and `h.to_bits() + up as u64 - (l.to_bits() >> 63)` stays within `u64` for the `h` between 1/2 and 2 that `round_dir` is given, from `fast_exp`, from `round_dir_wide`, which passes it that of `fast_exp_edge`, or from `round_dir_signed`, which negates a negative `h`, as it does in `round_dir_err`, which checks that `h` is between 1/2 and 2 itself. In `sub_unit`, `(1 - e) as u64` and `<< 52` are reduced modulo 2^64, as in `scale`, and in `round_dir_sub`, `round_near_sub`, `round_dir_sub_err` and `round_near_sub_err`, `s.to_bits() + 1` and `s.to_bits() - 1`, or in `round_dir_sub` `s.to_bits() + up as u64 - (u.to_bits() >> 63)`, as in `round_dir`, and `r.to_bits() - c.to_bits()` stay within `u64` for the `h` and `e` that they are given, from `round_dir_wide`, `round_near_wide`, `round_dir_wide_err` and `round_near_wide_err`, which pass them those of `fast_exp_edge`, with `h` = RN(`h` + `l`), 1/2 < `h` < 2 and `e` >= -1085 (`fast_exp_edge_correct`), and `e` <= -1022 where they call them, as the theorems of `FastRoundSub.v` and `FastRoundWideErr.v` assume: C = 2^(-1022 - e) is in [1, 2^63], the bits are taken only where C <= s < 2C, and r is at least C, as s = C gives u = RN(h + l) = h > 0, so that the `f64` before s is not taken. In `normalize`, `(h.to_bits() >> 52) as i64` is `Z.shiftr (to_bits h) 52`, which keeps its value, as it is below 2^12. `DyadicTrig` transcribes the table `SC` of `src/dyadic/trig/sc_table.rs` as the list `SC` of [`ModelTrigTable.v`](theories/ModelTrigTable.v), which `Model.v` imports, of the 4-tuples of bit patterns of the Rust array, in the same order, indexed with `nth` at `i` up to 1024 and at 2048 - `i` above it, which are in [1, 1024] for the `i` in [1, 2047] that `fast_sc` is given, so that `i as usize` and `(2048 - i) as usize` keep their values and `2048 - i` cannot overflow, with `i > 1024` as `1024 <? i`, and `f64::from_bits(r[k])` of the row `r` as `sc_at` of that row read with `sc`, which returns part k of the 4-tuple, counted from 0, at the indices `k`, `k + 1`, `2 - k` and `3 - k` that `fast_sc` takes, which are in [0, 3] as `k` is 0 or 2, and the constants of the fast path by their bit patterns. It transcribes `INV2PI` as the list of its limbs, indexed with `nth` at `i + j` and `i + j + 1`, which are at most 19 for the `k = be - 1011` in [28, 1035] of `fast_reduce_big` and `j` up to 2. In `fast_reduce_big`, the `u64` and `u128` values are integers, reduced modulo 2^64 where Rust drops the bits above 64 (`<<` of a `u64`, `wrapping_mul`, `wrapping_add`, `& u128::from(u64::MAX)` and `c as u64`), `as i64` of a `u64` at or above 2^63 subtracts 2^64, the other casts between integers keep their values, and `as f64` of an `i64` is `of_i64`, which rounds to nearest, ties to even. In `fast_reduce_band_big`, whose steps up to n are those of `fast_reduce_big`, `p0 as u64` is `p0` modulo 2^64, `a ^ (a >> 63)` of an `i64` is `Z.lxor` and `Z.shiftr`, which give the same integer, at least 0, `leading_zeros()` of it is `leading_zeros 64`, and `- 1` of that cannot overflow, as it is at least 1, `a << s` of an `i64` is `<<` of its bits, read back as an `i64` as above, `|` of an `i64` and an `as i64` of a `u64` is `Z.lor` of their bits, `t & 2047` of an `i64` is `Z.land`, which `as u64` keeps and `<< 42` shifts without dropping a bit, as it is below 2^53, `u64::from(970 - s) << 52` is `Z.shiftl`, as s is at most 63, `as f64` of a `u64` below 2^53 is `of_i64`, and the rest is as in `fast_reduce_big`. In `fast_quad`, `fast_tan`, `fast_band`, `fast_band_big`, `fast_band_eval` and `fast_band_tan`, `n & 2047`, `n & 2048`, `n >> 11`, `q & 1` and `q & 2` are `Z.land` and `Z.shiftr`, which agree with Rust's on a negative `n`, and in `fast_unscale`, `((h.to_bits() >> 52) & 0x7ff) as i64` is `Z.land (Z.shiftr (to_bits h) 52) 0x7ff`, and `(1023 - e) as u64` and `<< 52` are reduced modulo 2^64, as in `scale`. In `fast_one_dir`, the switch that only the tests compile, at its top, is not transcribed, as in `Fast`. `c as i64` of an `f64` is `to_i64`, which rounds toward zero and saturates, and gives 0 for NaN, as Rust's cast does, `ks.to_bits() as i32 as i64` in `fast_exp` and in `fast_reduce` of `DyadicTrig` is `low_i32 (to_bits ks)`, as `as i32` of a `u64` keeps its low 32 bits, read in two's complement, and `as i64` of an `i32` keeps its value, and `as usize` of an integer from 1 to 22 keeps its value. `x.ceil()` and `x.floor()` are `fceil` and `ffloor`, as above, and `<`, `>`, `<=` and `>=` of `Dyadic`s are those of `PartialOrd`, which compares with `cmp`. The literals `1e1` to `1e22` of `POW10` are `binary_normalize` of their values rounded to nearest, as for `kept as f64` above, and the other literals, `LOG2_E` and `LOG2_10` of `std::f64::consts` among them, are given by their bit patterns. In `DyadicLog`, `f64::NAN` is the model's NaN, `POW10.iter().position(|&p| p == x)` is the first `k` from 0 to 22 with `eq (pow10 k) x`, `k as f64` is `of_u64 k`, the `i128` sum of `reduce`, which cannot overflow, is computed without a check, and `x.is_infinite()` is `x == f64::INFINITY || x == f64::NEG_INFINITY`. In `DyadicAtan`, `x.abs()` is `fabs x`, `y.is_infinite()` is as in `DyadicLog`, `x.is_sign_positive()` is `negb (Bsign x)`, as `x` is not NaN there, the `Option<f64>` that `tiny_quotient`, `tiny_quotient_dyadic`, `midpoint_quotient` and `midpoint_quotient_dyadic` return is an `option f64` inside the `option` of the model, while that of `exact_div`, which cannot panic, is the model's `option f64`, a `match` arm `Some(q) if c` is an `if c` inside the arm `Some q`, `q.to_bits() & 1` is `Z.land (to_bits q) 1`, `q.to_bits() - 1`, which does not wrap as the bits are odd, is an integer subtraction, and the arithmetic of `terms` on `u64`s and `i64`s, which cannot overflow, is computed without a check. `DyadicAtan` transcribes the table `ATAN_TABLE` of `src/dyadic/atan/tables.rs` as a list of the pairs of bit patterns of the Rust array, in the same order, indexed with `nth` at `i`, `ks.to_bits() as i32 as i64`, which is `low_i32 (to_bits ks)`, as above, and is in [0, 64] for the `th` up to 129/128 that `fast_atan_core` and `fast_atan1` are given, so that `i as usize` keeps its value, and the constants of the fast path and those of `tiny_quotient`, `midpoint_quotient` and `exact_div` by their bit patterns, which the test `exact_div_constants` checks for the last four, `TWO_M968`, `TWO_128`, `TWO_996` and `TWO_M1021`, and the arrays `[lo, hi]` that `pi_bounds` returns as pairs `(lo, hi)` of bit patterns, which `pi_bounds_correct` shows are the `f64`s just below and just above k π, and which the test `pi_bounds_are_roundings` checks in the Rust against MPFR. In `pi_times`, `up == c.is_sign_positive()` is `Bool.eqb up (negb (Bsign c))`, as `c` is not NaN where `pi_bounds` gives a pair. In its `split`, the shifts, masks and `|` of the `u64` `b` are those of the integer `b`, which is in [0, 2^64), and `(b >> 52) as i64` is `Z.shiftr b 52`, which is below 2^12. In `DyadicTrig`, `f64::NAN` is the model's NaN, `x.abs()` is `fabs x`, `-t` is `opp t`, `&PI[66 - l..]` is `skipn`, `c.copy_from_slice(p)` and the `sub_assign` after it in `reduce` are `c`, whose borrow is dropped, as is the carry of its `add_assign`, `(d.exp + 64) as usize` there is `Z.to_nat`, as `d.exp + 64` is not negative, `u64::from(odd)` is `Z.b2z odd`, `a != b` of two `bool`s is `xorb`, and the arithmetic of `reduce`, `terms`, `series` and the `eval` of `Cos` on `i64`s and `u64`s, which cannot overflow, is computed without a check. In `cube`, `cos_small`, `sin_small` and `tan_small`, `u128::from` of an integer keeps its value, as does `as u64` where the value is below 2^64, `x < 0.0` is `lt x zero`, `1.0f64.to_bits()` is `0x3ff0000000000000`, `/` of `u128`s is `Z.div`, and their arithmetic on `u64`s and `u128`s, whose shifts are by less than 128 and whose sums, differences and products, for the `m < 2^53` that `cube` is given, neither overflow nor go below 0, is computed without a check. `o.unwrap_or_else(|| e)` of an `Option<f64>` `o` is `match o with Some v => Some v | None => e end`. In `DyadicPow`, the functions of `src/dyadic/exp.rs`, `src/dyadic/log.rs` and `src/dyadic/consts.rs` that `src/dyadic/pow.rs` calls are those of `DyadicExp`, `DyadicLog` and `DyadicConsts`, `f64::NAN` is the model's NaN, `k as f64` and `e as f64` of `i64`s are `of_i64`, which is `binary_normalize` rounded to nearest, as `of_u64`, `mx as f64` and `q as f64` of `u64`s are `of_u64`, `c as u64` and `y as i32` of `f64`s are `to_u64` and `to_i32`, which round toward zero and saturate, and give 0 for NaN, and `f64::from(y as i32)` is `of_i64 (to_i32 y)`, which is exact, as is `f64::from(n)` of an `i32` `n`, `of_i64 n`. `m.trailing_zeros()` is `trailing_zeros`, the position of the lowest bit `Z.land m (- m)` that is set, `m >> z` is `Z.shiftr`, as `z < 64` for the nonzero `x` that the callers of `odd_parts` pass, `(v as u64).leading_zeros()` in `ilog2` takes `v mod 2^64`, `ex % 2` and `n % 2` are `Z.rem`, and `ex /= 2` is `Z.quot`. `my << ey`, for the `ey <= 11` that `exact` keeps, stays below 2^64, `t as u64` of a `u128` `t` below 2^53 is `t`, and `e as i64` of the `i128` `e` of `exact`, after the check that puts it in [-1074, 1023], is `e`. `n.unsigned_abs()` of an `i32` `n` other than 0 is `Z.to_pos (Z.abs n)`, and `powi` recurses on that `positive`, with `xH` for `n == 1`, and `xO q` and `xI q` for the even `n` and the odd `n` above 1, with `q` for `n / 2`. `x.is_sign_negative()` is `Bsign x`, as `x` is not NaN there, `x.abs()` is `fabs x`, `-x` is `opp x`, `y.is_infinite()` in `pow` and `x.is_infinite()` in `pown` are as in `DyadicLog`, `a == b` of two `bool`s is `Bool.eqb`, and the `Option<f64>` that `exact` returns is an `option f64` inside the `option` of the model, as for `tiny_quotient`, while the `Option<u64>` of `small_power`, which cannot panic, is the model's `option Z`, with `None` where it returns `None`. The `i128` products and sums of `exact`, and its `-ey`, `63 - q.leading_zeros()` and `s * s`, which stays below 2^54, the `u128` product of `small_power`, and the arithmetic on `i64`s of `odd_parts`, `expo`, `ilog2`, `ln_expo`, `pow` and `pown_pos`, none of which can overflow, are computed without a check. `T_OVER` and `T_UNDER` are given by their bit patterns, `0x40862e432ca57a78` and `0xc087500000000000`. In `fast_pow_wide_dir` and `fast_pow_wide_near`, the `?` on the result of `fast_exp_edge` is a `match` that returns `None` where it is `None`, as in `DyadicExp.fast_exp_edge`, `f64::INFINITY` and `0.0` are `infinity` and `zero`, and the switch that only the tests compile, at their top, is not transcribed, as in `Fast`. `fast_pow_dir_call` and `fast_pow_near_call`, which `pow` and `pow_near` call, are `#[inline(never)]` functions whose bodies are only the calls `fast_pow_dir(x, y, up)` and `fast_pow_near(x, y)`, of functions that are `#[inline(always)]`, so that they compute the same values as those, and as `Model.v` has no attributes, `DyadicPow` defines them as `fast_pow_dir` and `fast_pow_near`. The functions return an `option`, with `None` where the Rust panics: at an `assert!` of `src/dyadic/exp.rs`, `src/dyadic/log.rs`, `src/dyadic/atan.rs`, `src/dyadic/trig.rs` or `src/dyadic/pow.rs`, whose one `assert!` is that of `to_k`, `|k| <= 2^14`, or at a panic of an operation of the kernel; `ziv` does not panic at its cap. The functions of each module that call `ziv` are in a section `Driver` with the driver `ziv` as a parameter, as `exp_dir_with` and the others, and `exp_dir` and the others after it are `exp_dir_with Dyadic.ziv`, the Rust. The fields of a record `point` are total functions, so `kernel_field` in `DyPoint.v`, and alike `kernel_field2` for atan2 and x^y and `kernel_field_z` for x^n, take the result of the kernel with the driver `ziv_strict` of `DyDriver.v`, which returns only where a rounding test passes, where it returns one, and the result rounded by definition, `round_f64` of `MpfrIdeal.v`, where it does not: where the call reaches the cap of `ziv` with no test passing, or where the Rust panics. Where the kernel with `ziv_strict` returns, the Rust returns the same value (`exp_dir_strict` and the others). The theorems about `kernel_point` therefore cover the calls at which a rule or a fast path settles the argument without calling `ziv` or a rounding test of `ziv` passes, and say nothing about the calls that reach the cap without a test passing, where the Rust returns a value on the correct side (`exp_dir_side` and the others), or that panic. That a rounding test passes by the cap is not proved (see "What is not proved yet"). `kernel_point_rust` in `DyPointSide.v` takes instead the result of the kernel with the driver `ziv`, the Rust, where it returns, and the result rounded by definition only where the Rust panics, so that its theorems, `kernel_exp_encloses` and the others, cover the calls that reach the cap too, with intervals that contain the range in place of the tightest ones, and say nothing about the calls that panic. The library computes the bounds of the elementary functions of `src/elementary.rs` with these functions of the kernel, through `src/mpfr_calls/kernel.rs`, which no `cfg` gates, so the theorems about `kernel_point`, in `DyPoint.v`, `DyPointDec.v` and the constructors `made_kernel_<f>` and `dmade_kernel_<f>` of `Valid.v`, are those of the functions that it ships, for those calls, and the theorems about `kernel_point_rust`, in `DyPointSide.v`, are those of its interval functions at every argument at which no call of the kernel panics.
- The functions rounded to nearest are transcribed in the same way. `Dyadic.round_near` and `Dyadic.to_f64_near` transcribe `round_near` and `to_f64_near` of `src/dyadic.rs`, where `(lsb - (b - 128)) as u32` and `lsb as i32` keep their values, as `b` is in [-1074, 1024] there, `kept % 2 == 1` is `kept mod 2 =? 1`, and `kept + 1`, which stays at or below 2^53, is computed without a check. `Dyadic.round_bounds_near`, `Dyadic.ziv_near_at`, `Dyadic.ziv_near_cap` and `Dyadic.ziv_near` transcribe the functions of those names, where `lo.to_bits() == hi.to_bits()` compares the bit patterns as integers, which, unlike `==` of `f64`s, tell -0 from +0, and `lo.to_bits() & 1` is `Z.land (to_bits lo) 1`, and, as in `ziv`, the statements under `#[cfg(test)]` are not transcribed. The functions of each module that call `ziv_near` are in a section `DriverNear` with the driver `ziv_near` as a parameter, as in `Driver`, and `exp_near` and the others after it are `exp_near_with Dyadic.ziv_near`, the Rust. `TINY_NEAR`, 2^-27, is `DyadicExp.tiny_near`, and the literal `1e23` of `exp10_near` is `from_bits 0x44b52d02c7e14af6`, both given by their bit patterns. In `DyadicAtan`, the `Option<f64>` that `midpoint_quotient` returns is an `option f64` inside the `option` of the model, as for `tiny_quotient`, its `a.exp - b.exp` and `lo.exp + i64::from(lo.m[0].trailing_zeros())`, which cannot overflow, are computed without a check, its derived `lo == hi` of two `Dyadic<1>`s compares their fields `neg`, `exp` and `m[0]`, and, in `atan2_near`, the guard `q.abs() < TINY_NEAR` of the arm `Some(q)` of the `match` on `tiny_quotient` is an `if` inside the arm `Some q`, and its `else` and the arm `None` are both the arm `_`. In `DyadicPow`, `small_power_near` is `small_power` with the bound 2^54 in place of 2^53, and `exact_near` is `exact` with `small_power_near` in place of `small_power`, -1075 in place of -1074 as the least `e`, and `round` and `to_f64_near` of `src/dyadic.rs` in place of `q as f64`, `from_f64`, `shl` and `to_f64`, and `e as i64`, after the check that puts `e` in [-1075, 1023], is `e`. The module `Cr` transcribes `pow_dir` and `pow_near_signed` of `src/cr.rs`, where `-x` is `opp x`, `y.floor()` is `ffloor y`, `f64::NAN` is the model's NaN, and `dyadic::is_odd_integer`, `dyadic::pow` and `dyadic::pow_near` are `DyadicPow.is_odd_integer`, `DyadicPow.pow_dir_with` and `DyadicPow.pow_near_with`, with the driver a parameter as there. The public functions of `src/cr.rs`, the `_rn`, `_rd` and `_ru` functions that the macro `cr_fn!` writes and those of `atan2`, `pow` and `powi`, each call one function, of the kernel or `pow_dir` or `pow_near_signed`, and nothing else, and are not transcribed. As for the directed functions, the theorems of the functions rounded to nearest take the driver `ziv_near_strict` of `DyDriverNear.v`, which returns only where a rounding test passes, and the `_strict` theorems, such as `exp_near_strict`, show that where the kernel with `ziv_near_strict` returns, the Rust returns the same value. They say nothing about the calls that reach the cap of `ziv_near` without a test passing, where the Rust returns the rounding to nearest of a bound of the enclosure at 64 limbs (`ziv_near_cap_round`), or that panic.
- `DyadicRoot` in `Model.v` transcribes `src/dyadic/root.rs` in the same way, with `None` where the Rust panics. A `u128` product of two `u64`s, which cannot overflow, is a product of integers, and `w as u64` and `(w >> 64) as u64` of such a `w` are `w mod 2^64` and `w / 2^64`. `x.is_infinite()` is as in `DyadicLog`, `x.abs()` is `fabs x`, and `f64::INFINITY` and `f64::NAN` are `infinity` and the model's NaN. In `icbrt`, `t[0] as f64` is `of_u64`, `c as u64` is `to_u64`, as in `DyadicPow`, the literals `3.0` and `8_589_934_592.0` (2^33) are given by their bit patterns, `a.clamp(lo, hi)` is `Z.max lo (Z.min hi a)`, the `loop`, which `icbrt_correct` shows to end, has the fuel `val x + 2`, as `isqrt` has, and the `u64` sum `2 * s + q[0]` is checked as one. In `cbrt_odd`, `(a.exp - 96).rem_euclid(3)` is `Z.modulo`, `k as u64` is `k`, as `k` is in [96, 98], and `(a.exp - k) / 3` is `Z.quot`. In `hypot_odd`, `g as u64` is `g mod 2^64`, `h as i64` is `h`, the carry of `add_assign` is dropped, and `257 - bit_len(&sum)` is checked. In the fast paths, `>>`, `<<`, `&` and `|` on bit patterns are `Z.shiftr`, `Z.shiftl`, `Z.land` and `Z.lor`, `bits.leading_zeros()` is `leading_zeros 64 bits`, `(m << nz) & 0x000f_ffff_ffff_ffff` is `Z.land (Z.shiftl m nz) 0x000fffffffffffff`, as the mask also drops the bits that the shift moves above bit 63, the `u32` subtraction `- 11` of `fast_cbrt`, which cannot overflow for the subnormal `x` that reach it, is computed without a check, the `i64` division and remainder of the positive `e + 3072` are `Z.div` and `Z.modulo`, the casts `(bits >> 52) as i64`, `i64::from(nz)`, `i as u64`, `(1023 - i) as u64`, `i as usize` and `(2045 - eb) as u64`, which keep their values, are left out, `1.0 / z` is `div Fast.one z`, `up != (x < 0.0)` is `xorb up (lt x zero)`, the `?` on an `Option` is a `match` that returns `None` where it is `None`, `big.to_bits() + 1` is `to_bits big + 1`, which stays below 2^64 for a finite `big`, and, as in `ziv`, the statements under `#[cfg(test)]` of `fast_hypot` and `fast_hypot_near` are not transcribed. The other arithmetic on `i64`s and `u64`s, which cannot overflow, is computed without a check. `hypot_rn`, `hypot_rd` and `hypot_ru` of `src/cr.rs` call `hypot_near` and `hypot` of the kernel and nothing else, and `sqrt_rn`, `sqrt_rd` and `sqrt_ru` call `x.sqrt()`, which is `fsqrt`, and `sqrt1_rd` and `sqrt1_ru` of the backend that `src/simd.rs` selects, which are `Generic.sqrt1_rd` and `Generic.sqrt1_ru` for the portable one, and nothing else, so they are not transcribed. On the x86-64 backends but `avx512f`, and on wasm32, `sqrt1_rd` and `sqrt1_ru` call those of the portable backend.
- MPFR is modelled only for the MPFR backend of the functions of `src/elementary.rs` that are proved here, which only the tests compile, and only as far as `mpfr_fn!`, `mpfr_fn2!` and `mpfr_fn_si!` in `src/mpfr_calls/mpfr.rs` call it. An MPFR number is an `mpfr_t`: NaN, an infinity or a zero with its sign, or a real other than 0. `Float::with_val(f64::MANTISSA_DIGITS, x)` is `with_val x`, an MPFR number of 53 bits that holds `x` exactly, keeping the sign of a zero and of an infinity, which needs an exponent range that contains that of `f64` (`range_ok` in `Mpfr.v`). The MPFR functions that these macros call and `mpfr::get_d` are the fields of a record `m : mpfr`, each a function of the rounding direction and of an MPFR number, of two for `mpfr_atan2`, `mpfr_hypot` and `mpfr_pow`, and of an MPFR number and an integer for `mpfr_pow_si`, which `Model.v` leaves abstract. Every model of an elementary function takes as its first argument a record `pt : point` with a field `p_f` for each pair of functions `f_rd` and `f_ru` that these macros define, with `f_rd pt` as `p_f pt RNDD` and `f_ru pt` as `p_f pt RNDU`. The theorems about the library's functions pass it `kernel_point` of `DyPoint.v` (above), and those about the MPFR backend `mpfr_point m`, which `Model.v` makes of `m`: its field `p_exp` is `mpfr_fn m (mpfr_exp m)`, and the others are made the same way of the MPFR function that the macro calls, with `mpfr_fn2` for `mpfr_fn2!` and `mpfr_fn_si` for `mpfr_fn_si!`, and a coercion lets the theorems write it as `m`. `mpfr::f(x.as_raw_mut(), x.as_raw(), rnd)` sets `x` to `f rnd x`, as MPFR lets the result be the argument (§4.3 of the MPFR manual), and its ternary result, which the code ignores, is left out. In the same way, in `mpfr_fn2!`, `mpfr::atan2(x.as_raw_mut(), x.as_raw(), y.as_raw(), rnd)` sets `x` to `mpfr_atan2 rnd x y`, `mpfr::hypot(x.as_raw_mut(), x.as_raw(), y.as_raw(), rnd)` to `mpfr_hypot rnd x y`, and `mpfr::pow(x.as_raw_mut(), x.as_raw(), y.as_raw(), rnd)` to `mpfr_pow rnd x y`, where `y` is `with_val y` too, and in `mpfr_fn_si!`, `mpfr::pow_si(x.as_raw_mut(), x.as_raw(), y.into(), rnd)` sets `x` to `mpfr_pow_si rnd x n`, where `n` is the integer that the `i32` `y` holds, which `y.into()` converts to a `c_long` exactly. The `let` arm of each of these macros first calls `assert_thread_safe` in `src/mpfr_calls/mpfr.rs`, which is left out. `mpfr::rnd_t::RNDD` and `RNDU` are `RNDD` and `RNDU`. `(-a).max(b)` in `Interval::cosh` is `fmax first (opp a) b`: the larger of the two, or, if they compare equal, `-a` if `first` and `b` otherwise, as Rust's `f64::max` may return either. Neither is NaN there. `x.max(y)` and `x.min(y)` in `Interval::cos` and `Interval::sin`, of two bounds computed at finite floats, by the kernel or by `mpfr_fn!` functions, which are not NaN, are `fmax first x y` and `fmin first x y` in the same way, and each path calls at most one of them. `z_ad.min(z_bc)` and `z_ac.max(z_bd)` in `Interval::pow`, two calls on the same path, are `fmin first_min` and `fmax first_max`, with an argument for each. Their arguments are results of `pow_rd` and `pow_ru`, of the kernel or of `mpfr_fn2!`, which are not NaN there, as their lanes show (`pxl_not_nan` and `pxh_not_nan`). `Interval::cos`, `Interval::sin` and `Interval::tan_impl` call `floors_exact` only if `n == 0.0` and the test of `b - a` before it are false, and `floors_exact` divides `[b, b]` only if its first test is true, which the model writes with `if`s, as the divisions return an `option`. The `match` in `quadrant` on two `bool`s is nested `if`s. `||` in `impl_log!` and `Interval::atanh` and in `interior`, and `|` in `subset`, are Rocq's `||`, which gives the same value.

The elementary functions that the library ships assume nothing of MPFR. Their theorems, about `kernel_point`, hold where each call of the kernel returns by a rule or a passing rounding test, as the Rust returns there what `kernel_field` takes (`exp_dir_strict` and the others, in the bullet on `DyadicExp` above). They say nothing of the Rust at the arguments at which a call of the kernel reaches the cap of `ziv` without a test passing, or panics: there `kernel_field` takes the correct rounding by definition (see "What is not proved yet"). Those of `cbrt` and `hypot` hold at every argument, as their functions of the kernel do not use `ziv` and return at every argument (`k_cbrt_some` and `k_hypot_some`).
